Ownership\nProof of Preference Points Claimed\nEach party to a Consortium/Joint Venture shall submit separate certified copies in the above regard.\nRespond to a request for clarification received up to seven (7) working days before the tender closing time\nstated in the Tender Data and notify all tenderers who drew procurement documents.\nThe time and location for opening of the tender offers is:\nTime: 12H00, 08 September 2026\nLocation: Tender Box, Foyer of Ray Nkonyeni Local Municipality Offices, 10 Connor Street, Port Shepstone\nTenders will be opened immediately after closing time for tenders at 12H00.\nF.3.8.2 Tenders will be considered non-responsive if:\n• the tenderer has not completed and/or signed the Offer portion of C1.1 Form of Offer and\nAcceptance.\n• the tenderer does not comply with the Contractor’s CIDB grading designation specified in\nabove.\n• The tenderer has failed to clarify or submit any supporting documentation within the time for\nsubmission stated in the employer’s written request\nThe procedure for evaluation of responsive Bid Offers will be METHOD 4: (Financial Offer, preference, and\nquality (functionality) with 80/20 Preference Points System. Score quality, rejecting all Bid Offers that fail to\nscore the minimum number of points for quality stated in the Bid Data. The total score awarded will be the\naddition of the two scores for price and preference.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nThe following formula will be used in Calculation of Percentage for Functionality\nPS= (So x Ap)\nMs\nWhere:\n• Ps = percentage scored for functionality by Bid/proposal under consideration\n• So = total score of bid/proposal under consideration\n• Ms = Maximum possible score\n• Ap = percentage allocated for functionality\nAdd the following:\nScore the financial offers of remaining responsive tender offers using the following formula:\nNf = W1 x [1-(P-Pm) / Pm]\nWhere:\n• W1 = 80 for financial values up to R 50, 000, 000.00 (inclusive of VAT) of all responsive tenders received:\n• Pm = the value of the comparative offer of the most favourable tender.\n• P = the value of the comparative offer under consideration.\nF.3.11.8 Scoring Preferences\nUp to 20 points (for financial values up to R 50, 000, 000.00), will be awarded to bidders who complete the\npreference schedule and who are found to be eligible for the preference claimed.\n• The applicable preference point system for this tender is the 80/20 preference point system.\nPoints for this tender (even in the case of a tender for income-generating contracts) shall be awarded for:\n(a) Price; and\n(b) Specific Goals.\nThe maximum points for this tender are allocated as follows:\nPOINTS\nPRICE 80\nSPECIFIC GOALS 20\nTotal points for Price and SPECIFIC GOALS 100\nFailure on the part of a tenderer to submit proof or documentation required in terms of this tender to claim\npoints for specific goals with the tender, will be interpreted to mean that preference points for specific goals\nare not claimed.\nThe organ of state reserves the right to require of a tenderer, either before a tender is adjudicated or at any\ntime subsequently, to substantiate any claim in regard to preferences, in any manner required by the organ of\nstate.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 9\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nF.3.11.8 Scoring Quality\nTenderers are to submit information in respect of the following criteria upon which they will be scored for\nQuality. Failure to submit the relevant information will/or may result in zero scores.\nThe quality will comprise scores for the following based on criteria indicated in the respective tender returnable:\ni) A maximum of 20 points will be awarded for relevant qualifications and demonstrated\nexperience of the Company Owner\nii) A maximum of 20 points will be awarded for qualifications and demonstrated experience of the\nproposed Site Agent.\niii) A maximum of 30 points will be awarded for demonstrated experience with respect to\nundertaking construction of roads and bridge structures to the value more than R10 million.\nScore quality, rejecting all tender offers that fail to score the minimum number of 60% (42 out of 70) of the\npoints for quality stated in the tender data. 20\nAPPENDIX: STANDARD CONDITIONS OF TENDER\n(As contained in Annexure F of Board Notice 86 of 2010: Standards for Uniformity in Construction\nProcurement)\nF.1 General\nF.1.1 Actions\nF1.1.1. The employer and each tenderer submitting a tender offer shall comply with these conditions\nof tender. In their dealings with each other, they shall discharge their duties and obligations\nas set out in F.2 and F.3, timeously and with integrity, and behave equitably, honestly and\ntransparently, comply with all legal obligations and not engage in anticompetitive practices.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 12\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nF1.1.2. The employer and the tenderer and all their agents and employees involved in the tender\nprocess shall avoid conflicts of interest and, where a conflict of interest is perceived or\nknown, declare any such interest in their tender submissions. Employees, agents and\nadvisors of the employer shall declare any conflict of interest to whoever is responsible for\noverseeing the procurement process at the start of any deliberations relating to the\nprocurement process or as soon as they become aware of such conflict and abstain from\nany decisions where such conflict exists or recuse themselves from the procurement\nprocess, as appropriate.\nNote 1 ) A conflict of interest may arise due to a conflict of roles which might provide an\nincentive for improper acts in some circumstances. A conflict of interest can create\nan appearance of impropriety that can undermine confidence in the ability of the\nperson to act properly in his or her position even if no improper acts result.\n1. Conflicts of interest in respect of those engaged in the procurement process\ninclude direct, indirect or family interests in the tender or outcome of the\nprocurement process and any personal bias, inclination, obligation, allegiance or\nloyalty which would in any way affect any decision taken.\nF.1.1.3 The employer shall not seek, and a tenderer shall not submit a tender without having a\nfirm intention and the capacity to proceed with the contract.\nF1.2 Tender Documents\nThe documents issued by the employer for the purpose of a tender offer are listed in the tender\ndata.\nF.1.3 Interpretation\nF.1.3.1 The tender data and additional requirements contained in the tender schedules that are\nincluded in the returnable documents are deemed to be part of these conditions of tender.\nF.1.3.2 These conditions of tender, the tender data and tender schedules which are only\nrequired for tender evaluation purposes, shall not form part of any contract arising from the\ninvitation to tender.\nF.1.3.3 For the purposes of these conditions for the calling for expressions of interest, the\nfollowing definitions apply:\n(a) conflict of interest means any situation in which\ni) someone in a position of trust has competing professional or personal\ninterest which make it difficult to fulfil his or her duties impartially;\nii) an individual or organisation is in a position to exploit a professional or official\ncapacity in some way for their personal or corporate benefit; or\niii) incompatibility or contradictory interests exist between an employee and the\norganisation which employs that employee.\n(b) comparative offer means the tenderer’s financial offer after the factors of non-\nfirm prices, all unconditional discounts and any other tendered parameters that\nwill affect the value of the financial offer have been taken into consideration\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 13\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\n(c) corrupt practice means the offering, giving, receiving or soliciting of anything of\nvalue to influence the action of the employer or his staff or agents in the tender\nprocess; and\n(d) fraudulent practice means the misrepresentation of the facts in order to\ninfluence the tender process or the award of a contract arising from a tender offer\nto the detriment of the employer, including collusive practices intended to\nestablish prices at artificial levels\n(e) Organisation means a company, firm, enterprise, association or other legal\nentity, whether incorporated or not, or a public body\n(f) Quality (functionality) means the totality of features and characteristics of a\nproduct or service that bear on its ability to satisfy stated or implied needs.\nF.1.4 Communication and employer’s agent\nEach communication between the employer and a tenderer shall be to or\nfrom the employer's agent only, and in a form that can be read, copied and\nrecorded. Writing shall be in the English language. The employer shall not\ntake any responsibility for non-receipt of communications from or by a\ntenderer. The name and contact details of the employer’s agent are stated\nin the tender data.\nF.1.5 The employer’s right to accept or reject any tender offer\nF.1.5.1 The employer may accept or reject any variation, deviation, tender offer,\nor alternative tender offer, and may cancel the tender process and reject\nall tender offers at any time before the formation of a contract. The\nemployer shall not accept or incur any liability to a tenderer for such\ncancellation and rejection, but will give reasons for such action upon\nwritten request to do so.\nF.1.5.2 The employer may not subsequent to the cancellation or abandonment of a\ntender process or the rejection of all tender offers re-issue a tender\ncovering substantially the same scope of work within a period of six months\nunless only one tender was received and such tender was returned\nunopened to the tenderer.\nF.1.6 Procurement Procedures\nF.1.6.1 General\nF.1.6.1.1 At the conclusion of each round of negotiations, tenderers shall be invited by the\nEmployer to make a fresh tender offer, based on the same evaluation criteria, with or without adjusted\nweightings. Tenderers shall be advised when they are to submit their best and final offer.\nF.1.6.1.2 The contract shall be awarded in accordance with the provisions of F.3.11 and F.3.13 after\ntenderers have been requested to submit their best and final offer.\nF.2 Tenderer’s obligations\nF.2.1 Eligibility\nF.2.1.1 Submit a tender offer only if the tenderer complies with the criteria stated in the tender data and\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 14\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nthe tenderer, or any of his principals, is not under any restriction to do business with employer.\nF.2.1.2 Notify the Employer of any proposed material change in the capabilities or formation of the\ntendering entity (or both) or any other criteria which formed part of the qualifying requirements used by the\nEmployer as the basis in a prior process to invite the tenderer to submit a tender offer and obtain the\nEmployer’s written approval prior to do so prior to the closing time of tenders.\nF.2.2 Cost of tendering\nAccept that the employer will not compensate the tenderer for any costs incurred in the preparation and\nsubmission of a tender offer, including the costs of any testing necessary to demonstrate that aspects of\nthe offer satisfy requirements.\nF.2.3 Check documents\nCheck the tender documents on receipt for completeness and notify the employer of any discrepancy or\nomission.\nF.2.4 Confidentiality and copyright of documents\nTreat as confidential all matters arising in connection with the tender. Use and copy the documents issued\nby the employer only for the purpose of preparing and submitting a tender offer in response to the invitation.\nF.2.5 Reference documents\nObtain, as necessary for submitting a tender offer, copies of the latest versions of standards, specifications,\nconditions of contract and other publications, which are not attached but which are incorporated into the\ntender documents by reference.\nF.2.6 Acknowledge addenda\nAcknowledge receipt of addenda to the tender documents, which the employer may issue, and if\nnecessary, apply for an extension to the closing time stated in the tender data, in order to take the\naddenda into account.\nF.2.7 Site visit and clarification meeting\nAttend, where required, a site visit and clarification meeting at which tenderers may familiarize\nthemselves with aspects of the proposed work, services or supply and raise questions. Details of the\nmeeting(s) are stated in the tender data.\nF.2.8 Seek clarification\nRequest clarification of the tender documents, if necessary, by notifying the employer at least five working\ndays before the closing time stated in the tender data.\nF.2.9 Insurance\nBe aware that the extent of insurance to be provided by the employer (if any) may not be for the full cover\nrequired in terms of the conditions of contract identified in the contract data. The tenderer is advised to\nseek qualified advice regarding insurance.\nF.2.10 Pricing the tender offer\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 15\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nF.2.10.1 Include in the rates, prices, and the tendered total of the prices (if any) all duties, taxes (except\nValue Added Tax (VAT), and other levies payable by the successful tenderer, such duties, taxes and\nlevies being those applicable 14 days before the closing time stated in the tender data.\nF2.10.2 Show VAT payable by the employer separately as an addition to the tendered total of the prices.\nF.2.10.3 Provide rates and prices that are fixed for the duration of the contract and not subject to\nadjustment except as provided for in the conditions of contract identified in the contract data.\nF.2.10.4 State the rates and prices in Rand unless instructed otherwise in the tender data. The\nconditions of contract identified in the contract data may provide for part payment in other currencies.\nF.2.11 Alterations to documents\nNot make any alterations or additions to the tender documents, except to comply with instructions issued\nby the employer, or necessary to correct errors made by the tenderer. All signatories to the tender offer\nshall initial all such alterations. Erasures and the use of masking fluid are prohibited.\nF.2.12 Alternative tender offers\nF.2.12.1 Unless otherwise stated in the tender data, submit alternative tender offers only if a main tender\noffer, strictly in accordance with all the requirements of the tender documents, is also submitted. The\nalternative tender offer is to be submitted with the main tender offer together with a schedule that\ncompares the requirements of the tender documents with the alternative requirements the tenderer\nproposes.\nF.2.12.2 Accept that an alternative tender offer may be based only on the criteria stated in the tender\ndata or criteria otherwise acceptable to the employer.\nF.2.13 Submitting a tender offer\nF.2.13.1 Submit one tender offer only, either as a single tendering entity or as a member in a joint\nventure, to provide the whole of the works, services or supply identified in the contract data, unless stated\notherwise in the tender data.\nF.2.13.2 Return all returnable documents to the employer after completing them in their entirety, either\nelectronically (if they were issued in electronic format) or by writing in black ink.\nF.2.13.3 Submit the parts of the tender offer communicated on paper as an original plus the number of\ncopies stated in the tender data, with an English translation of any documentation in a language other\nthan English, and the parts communicated electronically in the same format as they were issued by the\nemployer.\nF.2.13.4 Sign the original and all copies of the tender offer where required in terms of the tender data.\nThe employer will hold all authorized signatories liable on behalf of the tenderer. Signatories for tenderers\nproposing to contract as joint ventures shall state which of the signatories is the lead partner whom the\nemployer shall hold liable for the purpose of the tender offer.\nF.2.13.5 Seal the original and each copy of the tender offer as separate packages marking the packages\nas "ORIGINAL" and "COPY". Each package shall state on the outside the employer's address and\nidentification details stated in the tender data, as well as the tenderer's name and contact address.\nF.2.13.6 Where a two-envelope system is required in terms of the tender data, place and seal the\nreturnable documents listed in the tender data in an envelope marked “financial proposal” and place the\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 16\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nremaining returnable documents in an envelope marked “technical proposal”. Each envelope shall state\non the outside the employer’s address and identification details stated in the tender data, as well as the\ntenderer's name and contact address.\nF.2.13.7 Seal the original tender offer and copy packages together in an outer package that states on\nthe outside only the employer's address and identification details as stated in the tender data.\nF.2.13.8 Accept that the employer shall not assume any responsibility for the misplacement or\npremature opening of the tender offer if the outer package is not sealed and marked as stated.\nF.2.13.9 Accept that tender offers submitted by facsimile or email will be rejected by the Employer,\nunless stated otherwise in the tender data.\nF.2.14 Information and data to be completed in all respects\nAccept that tender offers, which do not provide all the data or information requested completely and, in\nthe form, required, may be regarded by the employer as non-responsive.\nF.2.15 Closing time\nF.2.15.1 Ensure that the employer receives the tender offer at the address specified in the tender data\nnot later than the closing time stated in the tender data. Proof of posting shall not be accepted as proof of\ndelivery. The employer shall not accept tender offers submitted by telegraph, telex, facsimile or e-mail,\nunless stated otherwise in the tender data.\nF.2.15.2 Accept that, if the employer extends the closing time stated in the tender data for any reason,\nthe requirements of these conditions of tender apply equally to the extended deadline.\nF.2.16 Tender offer validity\nF.2.16.1 Hold the tender offer(s) valid for acceptance by the employer at any time during the validity\nperiod stated in the tender data after the closing time stated in the tender data.\nF.2.16.2 If requested by the employer, consider extending the validity period stated in the tender data for\nan agreed additional period.\nF.2.17 Clarification of tender offer after submission\nProvide clarification of a tender offer in response to a request to do so from the employer during the\nevaluation of tender offers. This may include providing a breakdown of rates or prices and correction of\narithmetical errors by the adjustment of certain rates or item prices (or both). No change in the\ncompetitive position of tenderers or substance of the tender offer is sought, offered or permitted.\nF.2.18 Provide other material\nF.2.18.1 Provide, on request by the employer, any other material that has a bearing on the tender offer,\nthe tenderer’s commercial position (including notarized joint venture agreements), preferencing\narrangements, or samples of materials, considered necessary by the employer for the purpose of a full\nand fair risk assessment. Should the tenderer not provide the material, or a satisfactory reason as to why\nit cannot be provided, by the time for submission stated in the employer’s request, the employer may\nregard the tender offer as non-responsive.\nF.2.18.2 Dispose of samples of materials provided for evaluation by the employer, where required.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 17\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nF.2.19 Inspections, tests and analysis\nProvide access during working hours to premises for inspections, tests and analysis as provided for in the\ntender data.\nF.2.20 Submit securities, bonds, policies, etc.\nIf requested, submit for the employer’s acceptance before formation of the contract, all securities, bonds,\nguarantees, policies and certificates of insurance required in terms of the conditions of contract identified\nin the contract data.\nF.2.21 Check final draft\nCheck the final draft of the contract provided by the employer within the time available for the employer to\nissue the contract.\nF.2.22 Return of other tender documents\nIf so instructed by the employer, return all retained tender documents within 28 days after the expiry of\nthe validity period stated in the tender data.\nF.2.23 Certificates\nInclude in the tender submission or provide the employer with any certificates as stated in the tender\ndata.\nF.3 The employer’s undertakings\nF.3.1 Respond to requests from the tenderer\nF.3.1.1 Respond to a request for clarification received up to five working days prior to the tender closing\ntime stated in the Tender Data and notify all tenderers who drew procurement documents.\nF.3.1.2 Consider any request to make material change in the capabilities or formation of the tendering\nentity (or both) or any other criteria which formed part of the qualifying requirements used to prequalify a\ntenderer to submit a tender offer in terms of a previous procurement process and deny any such request\nif as a consequence:\na) An individual firm, or joint venture as a whole, or any individual member of the joint\nventure fails to meet any of the collective or individual qualifying requirements.\nb) The new partners to a joint venture were not prequalified in the first instance, either as\nindividual firms or as another joint venture; or\nc) In the opinion of the Employer, acceptance of the material change would compromise the\nprequalification process.\nF.3.2 Issue Addenda\nIf necessary, issue addenda that may amend or amplify the tender documents to each tenderer during the\nperiod from the date of the Tender Notice until seven days before the tender closing time stated in the\nTender Data. If, as a result a tenderer applies for an extension to the closing time stated in the Tender\nData, the Employer may grant such extension and will then notify it to all tenderers who drew documents.\nF.3.3 Return late tender offers\nReturn tender offers received after the closing time stated in the Tender Data, unopened, (unless it is\nnecessary to open a tender submission to obtain a forwarding address), to the tenderer concerned.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 18\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\nF.3.4 Opening of tender submissions\nF.3.4.1 Open valid tender submissions in the presence of tenderers’ agents who choose to attend at the\ntime and place stated in the tender data. Tender submissions for which acceptable reasons for withdrawal\nhave been submitted will not be opened.\nF.3.4.2 Announce at the opening held immediately after the opening of tender submissions, at a venue\nindicated in the tender data, the name of each tenderer whose tender offer is opened, the total of his\nprices, preferences claimed and time for completion, if any, for the main tender offer only.\nF.3.4.3 Make available the record outlined in F.3.4.2 to all interested persons upon request.\nF.3.5 Non-disclosure\nNot disclose to tenderers, or to any other person not officially concerned with such processes, information\nrelating to the evaluation and comparison of tender offers, the final evaluation price and\nrecommendations for the award of a contract, until after the award of the contract to the successful\ntenderer.\nF.3.6 Grounds for rejection and disqualification\nDetermine whether there has been any effort by a tenderer to influence the processing of tender offers\nand instantly disqualify a tenderer (and his tender offer) if it is established that he engaged in corrupt or\nfraudulent practices.\nF.3.8 Test for responsiveness\nF.3.8.1 Determine, on opening and before detailed evaluation, whether each tender offer properly\nreceived:\na) meets the requirements of these Conditions of Tender,\nb) has been properly and fully completed and signed, and\nc) is responsive to the other requirements of the tender documents.\nF.3.8.2 A responsive tender is one that conforms to all the terms, conditions, and specifications of the\ntender documents without material deviation or qualification. A material deviation or qualification is one\nwhich, in the Employer's opinion, would:\n• detrimentally affect the scope, quality, or performance of the works, services or supply identified\nin the Scope of Work,\n• change the Employer's or the tenderer's risks and responsibilities under the contract, or\n• affect the competitive position of other tenderers presenting responsive tenders, if it were to be\nrectified.\nReject a non-responsive tender offer and not allow it to be subsequently made responsive by correction\nor withdrawal of the non-conforming deviation or reservation.\nF.3.9 Arithmetical errors\nF.3.9.1 Check responsive tender offers for arithmetical errors between amounts in words and amounts in\nfigures. Where there is a discrepancy between the amounts in figures and in words, the amount in words\nshall govern.\nF.3.9.2 Check the highest ranked tender or tenderer with the highest number of tender evaluation points\nafter the evaluation of tenders in accordance with F.3.11 for:\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 19\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T1\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Tendering Procedures\na) The gross misplacement of the decimal point in the unit rate,\nb) Omissions made in completing the pricing schedule or bills of quantities or\nc) Arithmetic errors in\n• Line-item totals resulting from the product of unit rate and a quantity in bills of quantities or\nschedule of prices; or\n• The summation of the prices.\nF3.9.2 Notify the tenderers of all errors or omissions that are identified in the tender offer and invite the\ntenderer to either confirm the tender offer as tendered or accept the corrected total of prices.\nF.3.9.3 Where the tenderer elects to confirm the tender offer as tendered, correct the errors as follows:\na) If bills of quantities or pricing schedules apply and there is an error in the line-item total resulting\nfrom the product of the unit rate and the quantity, the line-item total as quoted shall govern, and\nthe unit rate will be corrected. Where there is an obviously gross misplacement of the decimal\npoint in the unit rate, the line-item total as quoted shall govern and the unit rate shall be\ncorrected.\nb) Where there is an error in the total of the prices either as a result of other corrections required by\nthis checking process or in the tenderer's addition of prices, the total of the prices shall govern\nand the tenderer will be asked to revise selected item prices (and their rates if a bill of quantities\napplies) to achieve the tendered total of the prices.\nConsider the rejection of a tender offer if the tenderer does not correct or accept the correction of his\narithmetical errors in the manner described above.\nF.3.10 Clarification of a tender offer\nObtain clarification from a tenderer on any matter that could give rise to ambiguity in a contract arising\nfrom the tender offer.\nF.3.11 Evaluation of tender offers\nF3.11.1 General\nAppoint an evaluation panel of not less than three persons. score for quality in respect of a submission; and\nW2 is the maximum possible number of tender evaluation points awarded for the quality as stated in the\ntender data\nF.3.12 Acceptance of tender CERTIFICATE OF INDEPENDENT TENDER DETERMINATION (RNM/MBD 9)\n1. This Municipal Bidding Document (MBD) must form part of all bids¹ invited.\n2. Section 4 (1) (b) (iii) of the Competition Act No. 89 of 1998, as amended, prohibits an agreement\nbetween, or concerted practice by, firms, or a decision by an association of firms, if it is between\nparties in a horizontal relationship and if it involves collusive bidding (or bid rigging).² Collusive\nbidding is a pe se prohibition meaning that it cannot be justified under any grounds.\n1. Municipal Supply Regulation 38 (1) prescribes that a supply chain management policy must provide\nmeasures for the combating of abuse of the supply chain management system, and must enable\nthe accounting officer, among others, to:\n(a) take all reasonable steps to prevent such abuse.\n(b) reject the bid of any bidder if that bidder or any of its directors has abused the supply\nchain management system of the municipality or municipal entity or has committed\nany improper conduct in relation to such system; and\n(c) cancel a contract awarded to a person if the person committed any corrupt or\nfraudulent act during the bidding process or the execution of the contract.\n1. This MBD serves as a certificate of declaration that would be used by institutions to ensure that,\nwhen bids are considered, reasonable steps are taken to prevent any form of bid-rigging.\n1. In order to give effect to the above, the attached Certificate of Bid Determination (MBD 9) must be\ncompleted and submitted with the bid:\n¹ Includes price quotations, advertised competitive bids, limited bids, and proposals.\n² Bid rigging (or collusive bidding) occurs when businesses, that would otherwise be expected to compete, secretly conspire to raise\nprices, or lower the quality of goods and / or services for purchasers who wish to acquire goods and / or services through a bidding\nprocess. Bid rigging is, therefore, an agreement between competitors not to compete.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8 60\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: T2\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Returnable Documents\n(RNM/MBD 9)\nCERTIFICATE OF INDEPENDENT TENDER DETERMINATION\nI, the undersigned, in submitting the accompanying tender:\n(Tender Number and\nDescription) in response to the invitation for the tender made by:\n(Name of Municipality/ Municipality Entity)\ndo hereby make the following statements that I certify to be true and complete in every respect:\nI certify, on behalf of ....................................................................................................................................... that:\n(Name of Bidder)\n1. All incidents referred to in terms of Section 24 of the Occupational Health and Safety Act shall be reported by the\nContractor to the Department of Labour and the Employer.\n1. The units of measurement described in the Bill of Quantities are metric units. Abbreviations used in the Bill of\nQuantities are as follows:\n% = per cent m2.pass = square metre-pass\nh = hour m3 = cubic metre\nha = hectare m3.km = cubic metre-kilometre\nkg = kilogram MN = meganewton\nkℓ = kilolitre MN.m = meganewton-metre\nkm = kilometre MPa = megapascal\nkm-pass = kilometre-pass No. = number\nkPa = kiloposcal Prov sum = Provisional sum\nkW = kilowatt P C sum = Prime Cost sum\nℓ = litre sum = lump sum\nm = metre t = ton (1 000 kg)\nmm = millimetre W/day = Work day\nm2 = square metre\n1. The prices and rates to be inserted in the Bill of Quantities are to be the full inclusive prices for the work described\nunder the items. Such prices and rates shall cover all costs and expenses that may be required in accordance with\nthe provisions of the Scope of Work, and shall cover the cost of all general risks, liabilities, and obligations set forth\nor implied in the Contract Data, as well as overhead charges and profit. These prices shall be used as a basis for\nassessment of payment for additional work that may have to be carried out.\n1. It will be assumed that prices included in the Bill of Quantities are based on Acts, Ordinances, Regulations, By-laws,\nInternational Standards and National Standards that were published 28 days before the closing date for tenders.\n(Refer to www.sabs.co.za or www.iso.org for information on standards).\n1. Where the Scope of Work requires detailed drawings and designs or other information to be provided, all costs\nassociated therewith are deemed to have been provided for and included in the unit rates and sum amount tendered\nfor such items.\n1. An item against which no price is entered will be considered to be covered by the other prices or rates in the Bill of\nQuantities. A single lump sum will apply should a number of items be grouped together for pricing purposes.\n1. The quantities set out in the Bill of Quantities are approximate and do not necessarily represent the actual amount\nof work to be done. The quantities of work accepted and certified for payment will be used for determining payments\ndue and not the quantities given in the Bills of Quantities.\n1. Reasonable compensation will be received where no pay item appears in respect of work required in the Bills of\nQuantities in terms of the Contract and which is not covered in any other pay item.\n1. The short descriptions of the items of payment given in the Bill of Quantities are only for the purposes of identifying\nthe items. More details regarding the extent of the work entailed under each item appear in the Scope of Work.\n1. Descriptions in the Bill of Quantities are abbreviated and comply generally with those in the SANS 1200\nStandardised Specifications.\n1. Items against which N/A, left blank or – (dash) is entered are to\nbe considered as incomplete and will also invalidate the tender. Items against which Nil or zero (0) is entered are to be\nconsidered to be fully priced and the tenderer will provide the items in questions as specified at zero (0) or Nil price\n1. DESCRIPTION OF THE WORKS ............................................................................... 117\n2. DRAWINGS ................................................................................................................. 124\n3. PROCUREMENT ......................................................................................................... 125\n4. CONSTRUCTION ........................................................................................................ 125\n5. MANAGEMENT ........................................................................................................... 126\nPART B: AMENDMENTS TO THE STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS\nPROJECT SPECIFICATIONS RELATING TO THE STANDARD\nSPECIFICATIONS AND OTHER ADDITIONAL SPECIFICATIONS ................................ 129\nC3.3 PARTICULAR SPECIFICATIONS\nPART A: GENERAL REQUIREMENTS AND PROVISIONS ................................... 130\nPART C: ENVIRONMENTAL SPECIFICATION……………………………………… 137\nPART D: OHSA 1993 HEALTH AND SAFETY SPECIFICATION ........................... 199\nF8: EMPLOYER’S AGENT’S QUALITY MANAGEMENT SPECIFICATION…. 223\n116\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nC3.1: STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS\nThe Standard Specifications on which this contract is based are the ‘Standard Specifications for\nRoad and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS),\nOctober 2020’ and ‘Standard Specifications for Subsurface Investigations 2010’.\nC3.2: PROJECT SPECIFICATIONS\nThe Project Specifications, consisting of two parts, form an integral part of the Contract and supplement\nthe Standard Specifications.\nPart A contains a general description of the Works, the Site and the requirements to be met.\nPart B contains variations, amendments and additions to the Standard Specifications and, if applicable,\nthe Particular Specifications.\nIn the event of any discrepancy between a part or parts of the Standard or Particular Specifications and\nthe Project Specifications, the Project Specifications shall take precedence. In the event of a discrepancy\nbetween the Specifications (including the Project Specifications) and the drawings and / or the Bill of\nQuantities, the discrepancy shall be resolved by the Employer’s Agent before the execution of the work\nunder the relevant item.\nThe Standard Specifications, which form part of this contract, have been written to cover all phases of\nwork normally required for road contracts, and they may therefore cover items not applicable to this\nparticular contract.\n117\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPART A: GENERAL\n1. DESCRIPTION OF THE WORKS\n1.1 Employer’s objectives\nThis contract is for the Construction of Mgodlwa Bridge in Ward 8 under the Ray Nkonyeni\nMunicipality area of jurisdiction. The construction work for the project is the “Construction of\nMgodlwa Bridge in Ward 8”.\nThe description of the contract contained in the Scope of Work is merely an outline of the Contract\nWorks and shall not limit the work to be carried out by the Contractor under this Contract. Details\nof some of the major items are given in this section and approximate detailed quantities for each\ntype of work to be carried out in accordance with the Contract Documents are included in the Bill of\nQuantities.\n1.2 Location of the Works\nFrom the district road, an existing rural access road branches westward toward the project area and\nprovides direct access to the proposed bridge crossing. The access route traverses predominantly rural\nterrain comprising scattered residential homesteads, agricultural land, and grazing areas before reaching\nthe river crossing location.\nA locality map has been shown in Section C4.1 Site information.\n1.3 Overview of the Works\nThe project is in ward 7. The project entails the demolition and reconstruction of the Mgodlwa\nBridge including the rehabilitation of the roads leading up to the bridge. This involves the\nconstruction of a reinforced concrete sub-structure and super-structure, with approach slabs,\nroads and gabion structures. The scope of works also includes the construction of culvert, gabion\nand stormwater management.\n.\n1.4 Extent of the Works\nThe work that is to be carried out under the contract is as provided for in the Bill of Quantities,\nDrawings and project particular specifications. However, if during construction conditions are found\nto differ from those anticipated, the Employer’s Agent reserves the right to modify the scope of the\nwork to suit the prevailing conditions and circumstances. Variations introduced in this manner will\nbe dealt with in accordance with the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\nThe work to be undertaken by the Contractor includes, but is not limited to, the following:\n(a) Site Establishment\n(b) Demolishing the existing bridge structure on site and carting away debris to the nearby\nsanitary landfill site.\n(c) Bulk excavation works for cast in-situ/precast bases\n(d) Provision of drainage facilities to divert the stream where necessary and to dewater the\nfoundation excavations for the bridge cast in-situ/precast bases.\n(e) Construction of the reinforced concrete bases including temporary works.\n(f) Placing precast culvert bridge structure on the bases including any temporary works\nneeded.\n(g) Construction of reinforced concrete slab on the bridge structure as a walkway including\n118\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\napproaches to the bridge.\n(h) Construction of gabion protection where necessary.\n(i) Stone pitching on approaches to bridge abutments\n(j) Reinstatement of existing concrete walkways on either side of the bridge damaged during\nexecution of construction works.\n(k) Site clearing and cleaning on completion of the work.\n(l) Construction of access road\nA typical general arrangement detail of the proposed bridge is included in TMH7\n1.5 Detailed description of the Works\nThe following description is a broad outline of the works and does not limit the work to be\nexecuted by the Contractor in terms of the contract. The description of some of the major items\nindicated in this section are indicative, not absolute, and are provided to define in general terms\nthe overall scope of the project.\nThe Employer requires the Contractor to establish the necessary machinery, equipment,\nmethodology and personnel to carry out the construction, inclusive of, but not necessarily limited\nto the main activities mentioned in Section 1.4.\nThe approximate quantities of each type of work to be carried out in accordance with the contract\ndocuments are listed in the Schedule of Quantities on the following tables.\nThe site shall not only comprise the proclaimed road reserve but shall be extended in the broader\nsense to take account of all areas occupied by the Contractor, be it deliberate or unintentional, in\nthe execution of the contract. The site includes all the land within the proclaimed limits of the road\nreserve along the extent of the works, borrow pits and quarry sites, stockpile areas, locations set\naside for construction and supervision accommodation and any other location required for the\nexecution of the Works.\nIncidental intrusion into private or tribal property outside the road reserve shall not be permitted\nwithout the owner’s written authority. Any such agreement reached with a private or tribal\nlandowner (occupier) shall include the proviso that any material or equipment on that site shall\nremain the exclusive property of the Employer in terms of the contract.\n1.5.1 Access to the Site\nFrom the district road, an existing rural access road branches westward toward the project area\nand provides direct access to the proposed bridge crossing. The access route traverses\npredominantly rural terrain comprising scattered residential homesteads, agricultural land, and\ngrazing areas before reaching the river crossing location.\n1.5.2 Demolition work\nDemolition of the existing bridge structure as shown on the drawings will be required\n1.5.3 Spoiling of surplus material\nSurplus material shall be spoiled in designated areas approved by the Employer’s Agent. The spoil\nmaterial shall be disposed of in accordance with subclause A4.1.7.2(l) of the COTO Standard\nSpecifications.\n119\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n1.5.4 Quantities\nThe approximate leading quantities for the permanent Works to be constructed are as follows:\nDescription Source Approximate quantity\nConcrete Commercial 50 m3\nSteel reinforcement Commercial 80 ton\n1.5.5 Material investigations\nIt is required to establish a site laboratory for the Employer’s Agent’s site personnel, as it is\nenvisaged that the acceptance control be undertaken by an established commercial laboratory in\nDurban. The contractor remains responsible for his production control testing as required per the\nstandard (plus any special) project specifications. The Employer’s Agent reserves the right to test\nsamples at an approved laboratory. Materials information is furnished in section C4.4 at the end\nof this document.\n1.5.6 Material sources, spoil and stockpile areas\nSpoil areas and areas for the temporary stockpiling of construction materials shall be determined\nand agreed on site in conjunction with the Employer, Employer’s Agent, the PLC (where\napplicable) and the local communities (where applicable). The Contractor shall be permitted to\nuse only these agreed spoil and stockpile areas, which shall be landscaped and vegetated on\ncompletion of the work.\n1.5.7 Accommodation of traffic\nThe gravel route is not heavily trafficked and will need to be blocked for the duration of the\nproject.\nSafe access will need to be given to pedestrians during the course of the project.\n1.5.8 Accommodation of other contractors\nOther contractors working on the site shall be communicated with in order to ensure the smooth\nrunning of all contracts, the Contractor shall be required to liaise with, cooperate with and\naccommodate all other contractors working on the site, particularly when such other contractors\nare working in the same area simultaneously.\n1.5.9 Existing services\nIt is also expected that unknown domestic services crossings requiring relocation or protection may\nbe encountered along the route as the work proceeds. The Contractor shall make every effort to\nestablish the location of these services in any area prior to excavations commencing in that area.\nSuch efforts shall include diligent enquiry and discussions with adjacent landowners, visual surface\ninspection and exploratory trenching investigation as necessary.\n1.5.10 Employer’s Agent’s site offices and laboratory\nThe Contractor shall be required to provide furnished site office facilities, laboratory facilities,\n120\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nablution facilities, and carports for the Employer’s Agent at a site located in close proximity to the\nContractor’s office establishment and to the Works. A possible area for such site establishment\nshall be indicated at the Site Clarification Meeting.\n1.5.11 Climate\nThe project is situated in a climatic area that can be described as moderate with a Weinert N-\nvalue of 2.0. This is the limit defining the boundary between moderate and wet, i.e. N-value of 2.0.\n1.5.12 Environment\nThe Contractor’s attention is called to clause A1.2.3.3 of Standard Specifications for Road and\nBridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020 and\nto the requirements of Part C: Environmental Management Specification contained in section\nC3.3 Particular Specifications.\n1.5.13 Labour\nA Project Liaison Committee (PLC) has been established and is a vital means of communication\nbetween all parties involved with the project. The composition of the PLC comprises\nrepresentatives of the Employer, the Employer’s Agent and formal structures within the\ncommunity.\nThe Contractor shall make use of these communication channels, and shall appoint from amongst\nhis site personnel a responsible person to participate in the affairs of the PLC, and this\nrepresentative will be also required to attend the monthly PLC meetings.\nIt is mandatory that the Contractor shall interact with the community via proactive project liaison\nand project participation by its leaders and constituted organisations and forums, as well as\nthrough the employment of its people, and these activities shall constitute essential facets of the\nproject.\nLocal labour is to be used and the employment of such labour is to be done in conjunction with\nthe PLC. The PLC has formed a Labour Committee who shall assist the Contractor with the\nrecruitment of local labourers to ensure an equal distribution of people employed between the\nvarious Amakhosi in the area.\n1.5.14 Labour-enhanced construction methods\nLabour enhanced construction shall mean the economically efficient employment of as great a\nportion of local labour as is technically feasible to produce a standard of construction as\ndemanded by the specifications with completion by the Due Completion Date, where local labour\nis the primary resource, supported by plant for activities that cannot be carried out feasibly by\nlabour only.\nLabour enhanced construction activities are to be planned as task-based work as a general rule.\nA task means a fixed quantity of work, to be performed to a clearly defined quality. Task-based\nwork means work in which a worker is paid a fixed rate for performing a task, which is clearly\ndefined in terms of quantity and quality. Typically, a particular task can be completed within a\nworking day.\nAppropriate portions of the Works included in the Contract shall be carried out using labour\nenhanced construction methods.\nExcept where the use of equipment is essential in order, in the opinion of the Employer’s Agent,\n121\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nto meet the specified requirements by the Due Completion Date, or where the use of equipment is\nessential as a result of occupational health and safety considerations, the Contractor shall use\nonly hand tools and equipment in the construction of those portions of the Works that are required\nin terms of these Project Specifications to be constructed using labour enhanced construction\nmethods.\nSuch portions of the Works shall be constructed utilizing only the local labour of the Contractor\nand/or the local labour of subcontractors, supplemented by the Contractor’s key personnel to the\nextent necessary and unavoidable, unless otherwise instructed by the Employer’s Agent and in\naccordance with the further provisions of the relevant sections of Part B of the Project\nSpecifications.\nSubject to considerations of occupational health and safety, and subject to the nature of the in situ\nmaterials being such that they can be excavated efficiently by hand, the portions of the Works to\nbe carried out under supervision using labour enhanced construction methods are listed under the\nlabour enhanced item numbers as per COTO -Draft Standard(DS) October 2020 specifications in\nthe bill of quantities, include, but are not limited to, the following:\nErection of the Contractor’s and Employer’s Agent’s site establishment facilities;\nProvision of domestic services at the site establishment facilities;\nProvision of flagmen and labour for erecting traffic accommodation facilities;\nClearing of the Site;\nExcavation for structures and open drains up to 1,5 m deep where the depth of the water table\npermits such excavation to be carried out safely, and the subsequent backfilling thereof;\nBedding, selected fill, backfilling and compaction of all trenches for prefabricated culverts\nirrespective of depth, but assisted by mechanical compaction equipment in order to achieve the\nspecified densities;\nTransportation and spoiling of all trench materials, where the disposal site is located within 20\nmetres of the source;\nMixing and placing of concrete for the channel and backing to the prefabricated kerbs;\nMixing and placing of concrete for concrete lined drains and sidewalks;\nMixing and placing of concrete for minor drainage structures and road furniture structures;\nMixing and placing of concrete for concrete edge beams at gravel road access points;\nInstallation of prefabricated kerbs;\nConstruction of all brickwork required for drainage structures and manholes;\nErection of falsework and formwork;\nFixing of reinforcement;\nSpreading of offloaded earthworks materials to the extent scheduled;\nSpreading of offloaded pavement layers materials to the extent scheduled;\nSpreading of stabilising agent;\nMaintenance patching of surfacing;\nSlurry seal surfacing;\nExcavation for and construction of stone pitching, and subsequent backfilling;\nExcavation for and construction of gabion boxes and mattresses, and subsequent backfilling;\nDismantling / erection of fences;\nExcavation and subsequent backfilling for guardrail;\nDismantling / erection of guardrail;\nExcavation and subsequent backfilling for road signs;\nDismantling / erection of road signs;\nSpreading of topsoil;\nPlanting of grass cuttings, grass sodding and hand sowing of grass seeds; and\nCleaning and tidying up of the Site.\nCompiler to add to / remove from the list above in accordance with the specific requirements of\nthis contract.\nIn respect of those portions of the Works which are not listed above, the construction methods\n122\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nadopted and the equipment utilized shall be at the discretion of the Contractor, provided always\nthat the construction methods adopted and the equipment utilized by the Contractor are\nappropriate in respect of the nature of the Works to be executed and the standards to be\nachieved in terms of the Contract.\n1.6 Temporary Works\nThe Temporary Works required under this Contract shall include the traffic/pedestrian\naccommodation measures implemented, the provision of drainage control, falsework and\nformwork during the construction of the minor drainage structures, and the provision of any\nscaffolding or temporary propping used during the erection of the road signs.\nAll Temporary Works shall be removed from the Site on completion of the Contract.\n1.7 Maintenance of the Works during the construction period\nThe Contractor shall take note of the various requirements of the General Conditions of Contract\n2015 and the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road\nAuthorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020 with respect to the care and protection of\nthe Works.\nThe handing-over of the road reserve for this contract is described in clause A1.2.3 of Standard\nSpecifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft\nStandard (DS), October 2020. The Contractor shall be responsible for maintaining this portion of\nthe road from the date of hand-over until the issue of the Certificate of Practical Completion.\n1.8 Testing of materials\nA prime cost sum has been allowed in chapter 20 of the Bill of Quantities for all acceptance\ncontrol testing laboratory work to be carried out by the Employer’s Agent using the laboratory\nfacilities which may include and/or commercial laboratory facilities.\nThe Contractor shall carry out at his own cost the required process control testing as specified in terms of\nthe Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft\nStandard (DS), October 2020.\n1.9 Power supply and other services\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements concerning the supply of electrical power and\nall other services. No direct payment shall be made for the provision of electrical and other\nservices. The cost thereof shall be deemed to be included in the rates and amounts tendered for\nthe various items of work for which these services are required.\n1.10 Construction in confined areas\nIt may be necessary for the Contractor to work within confined areas. Except where provided for\nin the specifications, no additional payment shall be made for work done in restricted areas. In\ncertain places the width of the fill material and pavement layers may decrease to zero and the\n123\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nworking space may be confined. The method of construction in these confined areas largely\ndepends on the Contractor’s constructional plant. However, the Contractor shall note that, unless\notherwise provided for in terms of the scheduled payment items in the Standard Specifications for\nRoad and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS),\nOctober 2020 or these project specifications, measurement and payment shall be in accordance\nwith the specified cross sections and dimensions only, irrespective of the method used for\nachieving these cross sections and dimensions, and that the tendered rates and amounts shall\ninclude full compensation for all special equipment and construction methods and for all\ndifficulties encountered when working in confined areas and narrow widths, and at or around\nobstructions, and that no extra payment shall be made nor shall any claim for additional payment\nbe considered in such cases.\n1.11 Contractor’s campsite\nPossible locations for a campsite shall be pointed out at the clarification meeting. (Project Specific\nClarification meetings)\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements for the provision of his campsite and housing\nfor construction personnel but the chosen site shall be subject to the approval of the Employer’s\nAgent, the local authorities and, where applicable, the Project Liaison Committee (PLC)\nassociated with the project.\nThe standard of the Contractor's camp, offices, accommodation, ablution, and other facilities must\ncomply with the requirements of all local authority, environmental and industrial regulations\nconcerned. In establishing and maintaining his campsite, due cognisance is to be taken of the\nrequirements of clause A1.3.3 of the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South\nAfrican Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020.\nThe Contractor is to fully familiarise himself with all local by-laws and Government regulations for\nthe employment, transport and accommodation of labour on site.\nThe Contractor shall particularly note that there is a high risk of theft, vandalism and damage to\nproperty in this area and strict security will be required for all plant, establishment, temporary\nworks and partially completed works. The Contractor shall be responsible for providing security\nfor all plant, establishment, temporary works and partially completed works. No separate\npayment shall be made for the provision of such security since full compensation for these costs\nshall be deemed to be included in the amount tendered for item C1.3.1.3 (The contractor’s\ngeneral obligations: Time-related obligations).\n1.12 Additional requirements for construction activities\nThe travelling public shall have the right of way on public roads, and the Contractor shall make\nuse of approved methods to control the movement of his equipment and vehicles so as not to\nconstitute a hazard on the road.\nThe Contractor’s tendered rates shall include full compensation for all costs which may arise from the\nconstruction and maintenance of deviations and construction under traffic. No claim for additional costs\nwhich may arise from these methods of traffic accommodation and no additional payment owing to\ninconvenience as a result of the Contractor’s method of working shall be considered.\n1.13 Construction programme\nThe Contractor shall submit a detailed time programme in accordance with clause 5.6.2 of the\nGeneral Conditions of Contract 2015, clause A1.2.7 of the Standard Specifications for Road and\nBridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020.\n124\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nIn addition to any other restrictions accommodated by the Contractor in compiling the\nconstruction programme, the following constraints shall be taken into account in the preparation\nthereof:\n(a) The whole of the Works (and the portions of the Works if completion in portions is required)\nshall be completed within the time period(s) stated (refer to the Contract Data in section\nC1.2.2).\n(b) Working days lost due to abnormal rainfall shall be treated as set out in clause A1.2.3.4 (b)\n(c) Allowance shall be made for non-working days and special non-working days (refer to the\nContract Data in section C1.2.2).\n(d) Construction activities must comply with all the specified environmental requirements\nincluding clause A1.2.3.3 of Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South\nAfrican Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020 and the requirements\nof Part C: Environmental Management Specification contained in section C3.3 Particular\nSpecifications.\n(e) Construction activities must comply with all the specified health and safety obligations\nincluding the requirements of Part D: OHSA 1993 Health and Safety Specification\ncontained in section C3.3 Particular Specifications.\n(f) Strict control of access to and from local public roads shall be required when construction\nvehicles, plant or equipment leave or enter the site.\n(g) Throughout the contract period traffic must be accommodated through the site and all other\ncontractors engaged on the construction of identified roads must be accommodated.\nThe Contractor’s programme of work shall take due cognisance of risks by limiting the duration of the\nexposure of the various construction elements to natural phenomena.\n1. DRAWINGS\nThe drawings that form part of the Tender documents are issued on electronically on request and\nshall be used for Tender purposes only. It is the contractor’s responsibility to print the design\ndrawing under his/her own costs.\nAny information in the possession of the Contractor which the Employer’s Agent requires to\ncomplete the as-built drawings shall be supplied to the Employer’s Agent before a certificate of\ncompletion will be issued.\nOnly figured dimensions shall be used and drawings shall not be scaled unless so instructed by the\nEmployer’s Agent. The Employer’s Agent will supply all figures / dimensions omitted from the\ndrawings.\nThe levels given on the drawings are subject to confirmation on site, and the Contractor shall\nsubmit all levels to the Employer’s Agent for confirmation before he commences any structural\nconstruction work. The Contractor shall also check all clearances given on the drawings and shall\ninform the Employer’s Agent of any discrepancies.\nAdditional drawing can be found on the KwaZulu-Natal Department of Transport Standard Detail of\ndrawings.\n1. PROCUREMENT\n125\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe contractor shall procure goods and services under provisional sums and prime cost sums\naccording to the Employer’s standards.\n1. CONSTRUCTION\n4.1 Applicable SANS 2001 standards for construction work\nAll construction work must conform with the relevant SABS or SANS specifications referred to in the\n‘Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO),\nDraft Standard (DS), October 2020’, or in Section C3.2 Project Specifications, Part B: Amendments\nto the Standard Specifications, of this document.\n4.2 Applicable national and international standards\nAll construction work must be undertaken in accordance with the ‘Standard Specifications for Road\nand Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020\nand as amended in Section C3.2 Project Specifications, Part B: Amendments to the Standard\nSpecifications, of this document.\n4.3 Particular / generic specifications\nAs well as the ‘Amendments to the Standard Specifications’ contained in Part B of Section C3.2\nProject Specifications, of this document, the following Particular Specifications as contained in\nSection C3.3, are applicable to this Contract:\nPart C: Environmental Management Specification\nPart D: OHSA 1993 Health and Safety Specification\nPart E: Expanded Public Works Programme\nPart F: Small Contractor Development\nThe ‘General Conditions of Contract for Construction Works, Third Edition (2015)’, published by the\nSouth African Institution of Civil Engineering which is applicable to this Contract.\n4.4 Certification by recognized bodies\nNo certification of items included in the Works is required\n4.5 Plant and materials provided by the Employer\nNo plant and materials are to be provided by the Employer.\n4.6 Services and facilities provided by the Employer\nThe Employer’s Agent’s site office and laboratory facilities will be paid for in terms of the Contract.\nNo other services or facilities are to be provided by the Employer.\n1. MANAGEMENT\n5.1 Applicable SANS 1921 standards\n126\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe following parts of SANS and Advertisements’ of the\nStandard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road\n4.14.6\nAuthorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020, and as detailed in\nthe ‘Example of clause A1.2.7.3 and Chapter 2 of the Standard\n4.17.1\nSpecifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road\nAuthorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020.\nServices that are known October\n2020.\nThe requirements for detection apparatus for locating underground services\n4.17.4\nare: none.\nThe additional health and safety requirements are described in Part D\n4.18 ‘OHSA 1993 MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nSite meetings shall be convened as described in clause A1.2.3.16 ‘Monthly Site Meetings’ of the\nStandard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO),\nDraft Standard (DS), October 2020. The Contractor shall keep on site a set of minutes of all site\nmeetings, daily records of resources (people and equipment employed), a site memoranda book, a\ncomplete set of contract working drawings and a copy of the procurement document, and shall\nmake these available at all reasonable times to all persons concerned with the contract.\n(b) Water and electricity\nThe Contractor is to provide water and electricity as described in clauses A1.2.3.21 ‘Water’ and\nA1.4.7.3 ‘Services’ of the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African\nRoad Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020.\n5.1.2 SANS 1921-6 Part 6: HIV/AIDS awareness\nSpecification data associated with SANS 1921-6\nClause No. Essential data\nA qualified service provider is one that is an accredited or\nprovisionally accredited training service provider in the HIV/AIDS\nfield. A list of accredited service providers can be obtained from\n4.2.1(a)\nthe Construction SETA (CETA) (tel. 011 265 5900), Health and\nWelfare SETA (HWSETA) (011 622 6852) or on the Health and\nWelfare SETA website: www.hwseta.org.za.\nThe HIV/AIDS awareness programme is to be repeated at four\n4.2.1(a)\nmonthly intervals throughout the duration of the contract.\n5.1.2.1 Additional clauses:\n(a) Workshops\nThe duration of each workshop shall not be less than 2½ hours.\n5.2 Recording of weather\nThe Contractor shall erect a rain gauge and record the rainfall as described in clause A1.2.3.4 of\nthe Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO),\nDraft Standard (DS), October 2020. This information together with other details of the prevailing\nweather conditions shall be recorded in the daily site diary.\n5.3 Unauthorised persons\nThe Contractor shall keep unauthorised persons away from the Works at all times. Under no\ncircumstances may the Contractor’s personnel be accommodated on the site.\n5.4 Management meetings\nManagement meetings are to be held weekly at a time convenient to all concerned, to discuss\nplanning and health and safety amongst other things. Attendance by the Construction Manager and\nthe Employer’s Agent’s Representatives on site is mandatory, and attendance by other interested\nparties will be by invitation.\n5.5 Forms for contract administration\nThe Employer’s Agent’s Representative will provide standard forms for ‘Site Diary’, ‘Site Memoranda’,\n128\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n‘Requests for Inspection’ and any others deemed to be necessary during the contract.\n5.6 Electronic payments\nPayments of approved payment claims will be made electronically upon submission of the\nContractor’s banking details.\n5.7 Daily records\nA site diary is to be compiled jointly by the Construction Manager and the Employer’s Agent’s\nRepresentatives on site and is to be agreed and signed by both parties. The original signed copy is\nto be retained by the Employer’s Agent’s Representative.\nThe Contractor is to keep daily records of people and equipment on site in a format to be agreed by\nthe Employer’s Agent’s Representative, and is to provide copies to the Employer’s Agent’s\nRepresentative when requested.\n5.8 Payment certificates\nDetails of measurements, proof of payment for items contained in provisional sums and prime cost\nsums, proof of ownership of Plant and materials on site and documentation pertaining to contract\nprice adjustment and special materials, are required as substantiation of claims for payment.\n5.9 Permits\nBelow are permits required before commencement of works:\n• Approved Health and Safety permit\n• Construction works permit\n• Environmental works permit\n• Water use licence permit\n5.10 Proof of compliance with the law\nThe contract is to provide proof of compliance as specified by legislative law and acts and this\ncontract document.\n5.11 Submission of reports\nThe contractor shall submit monthly reports in accordance with the specifications in Part E:\nExpanded Public Works Programme and Part F: Small Contractor Development.\nC3.2: PROJECT SPECIFICATIONS\nPART B: AMENDMENTS TO THE STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS\n129\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPROJECT SPECIFICATIONS RELATING TO THE STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS AND\nOTHER ADDITIONAL SPECIFICATIONS\nIn certain clauses in the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road\nAuthorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020, allowance is made for a choice to be\nspecified in the Project Specifications between alternative materials or methods of construction, and\nfor additional requirements to be specified to suit a particular contract. Details of such alternatives or\nadditional requirements applicable to this Contract are contained in this part of the Project\nSpecifications. It also contains the necessary additional specifications required for this Contract.\nThe clauses and payment items dealt with in this part of the Project Specifications are numbered ‘PS’\nwith a number corresponding to the relevant clause or item number in the Standard Specifications for\nRoad and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October\n2020.\nNew clauses and payment items not covered by clauses or items in the Standard Specifications for\nRoad and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October\n2020 have been included here and have also been designated with the prefix ‘PS’. Such clauses and\nitems have been given a new number following upon the last number used in the particular chapter\nreferred to in the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road\nAuthorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020.\nCHAPTER 1.2 GENERAL REQUIREMENTS AND PROVISIONS\nPART A: SPECIFICATION\n130\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD Scope\nRates for daywork shall be entered in the under item C1.2.8.1 in accordance with the following\nspecifications.\nb. Daywork Rates\nAccording to Clause 6.5 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015, certain work may be carried out\nusing rates tendered in the daywork schedule. A schedule of personnel, construction equipment and\nvehicles which may be required to perform work on a daywork basis is included in the Bill of\nQuantities. The quantities used in the Bill of Quantities are for tender evaluation purposes only and\nthe use or not of these items shall not constitute a variation in terms of Clause 6.3 of the General\nConditions of Contract 2015.\nNo work will be paid for as daywork without the written instruction or approval of the Employer’s Agent.\nc. Type of Work\nThe Employer’s Agent may order daywork in certain cases where it is necessary to vary or to extend\nthe works due to new or unforeseen circumstances to such an extent that the tendered rates for\nspecific items of work are no longer applicable, or where no suitable combination of tendered rates can\nbe used to pay for such work.\nAs a general rule, applicable rates for additional work items will be agreed between the Contractor and\nthe Employer’s Agent. Daywork will only be used in exceptional circumstances.\nd. Materials\nMaterials for use in works carried out under daywork shall be purchased by the Contractor who shall\nalso arrange for delivery to site, and shall be responsible for any other requirements associated with\n132\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nspecific materials. A Provisional Sum has been allowed in C1.2.8 of the Bill of Quantities for daywork\nmaterials. The Contractor shall enter a tendered percentage in the Bill of Quantities to cover his\nhandling costs and profit, as per other provisional and prime cost sums in this Contract.\nMaterials shall be paid for using the method described in C2.1, ‘Pricing Assumptions’. No contract price\nadjustment will be applicable to materials.\nThe Contractor shall submit proof of ownership for any materials used in daywork with his daywork\nclaim to the Employer’s Agent. Further, if specific materials are required for daywork, quotations will\nbe called for as per Clause 6.5.2 of the General Conditions of Contract 2015.\ne. Construction Equipment\nWhere daywork is ordered, the tendered rates for construction equipment in C1.2.8 of the Bill of\nQuantities shall be used in calculating the payment due for any construction equipment required to\nexecute the daywork. If no rate is included in the Bill of Quantities for a particular item of construction\nequipment, and where no other rate or combination of rates would provide suitable compensation,\nthen the daywork method of payment described in Clause 6.5.1.3 of the General Conditions of\nContract 2015 will be used.\nThe tendered rates for each item of construction equipment shall include for all operating costs\nassociated with the said item of construction equipment. Such costs are deemed to include fuel, re-\nfuelling costs, lubrication and routine servicing / maintenance, breakdowns and spares, all overhead\ncosts, site management costs and administration costs. The tendered rates shall also include the\nconstruction equipment operator and the general supervision of the construction equipment while it is\nengaged in the daywork.\nf. Salaries and Wages of Workmen\nThe salaries and wages of workmen executing daywork shall be paid for using the tendered rates in\nthe Bill of Quantities. The tendered rates shall include for all costs associated with the employment of\npersonnel, including salaries, wages, allowances, workmen’s compensation, medical aid and pension\ncontributions, government levies and taxes, training costs and any costs associated with living on the\nsite. The tendered rates shall also include for the transportation of the workmen to the site of the\ndaywork.\nAll overhead costs, administration costs, site management costs and the Contractor’s profit are\ndeemed to be covered by the daywork rates and no additions or mark ups will be made to the\ntendered rates.\nThe tendered rates shall also include any hand tools normally associated with the workmen’s job\ndescription e.g. picks, shovels, hammers, saws and spirit levels. The tendered rate for labourers shall\nalso include for the casual supervision by a gang leader or foreman. Only when specifically called for\nby the Employer’s Agent, will payment be made for the use of a gang leader or foreman supervising on\na continuous basis.\ng. Measurement and Payment\nThe following principles shall also apply to the measurement and payment of daywork.\nThe unit of measurement for construction equipment shall be the number of Vibroclock hours worked\nand each item of construction equipment shall be fitted with a Vibroclock, the cost of which shall be\nincluded in the rates. Excessive non-productive time when the engine is idling will not be paid for.\nWhere there is ambiguity between the flywheel horsepower and mass of the machine, the flywheel\nhorsepower shall govern the measurement category. Where width and mass are specified, mass shall\ngovern the measurement category.\n133\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to the requirements of Clauses 6.5.3 and 6.5.4 of the General\nConditions of Contract 2015 with regard to the submission of lists and statements of personnel,\nmaterials and construction equipment used for daywork.\nThe payment items under C1.2.8 in the Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South\nAfrican Road Authorities (COTO), Draft Standard (DS), October 2020 will be applicable.\nPSA1.2.3.18 Stakeholder liaison\nAdd the following to Clause A1.2.3.18:\na. Project Liaison Committee\nThe process of implementing infrastructure projects will be undertaken by means of structured\nengagement between those responsible for the delivery of the project and the community.\nA Project Liaison Committee (PLC) is a vital means of communication between the parties involved\nwith the project. A PLC may be formed if the project is such that a specific community can be\nidentified.\nThe PLC comprises representatives of the employer, the engineer and formal structures within the\ncommunity. The contractor shall make use of these communication channels, and shall appoint from\namongst his site personnel a responsible person to participate in the affairs of the PLC, and this\nrepresentative shall also attend the monthly PLC meetings when so requested.\nThe PLC shall meet at least once every month until such time as it is of the opinion that it could fulfil its\ntasks by meeting less frequently.\nThe PLC deals with local labour on the project, and is tasked with:\n• assisting with community liaison and the resolution of community disputes;\n• devising fair and transparent procedures that will assist the contractor in the engagement of labour;\n• advising on and monitoring labour issues; and\n• assisting in the resolution of labour disputes.\nAll labour recruitment, employment and associated risks shall remain the sole responsibility of the\ncontractor.\nA new pay item is included in Chapter 1.2 of the schedule of quantities relating to the payment of the\nPLC on a provisional sum basis. Payment under this item shall be made only for the period for which\nthe duties of the PLC are required, and not necessarily for the full duration of the contract.\nb. Community Liaison Officer (CLO)\nThe contractor, after consultation with the Project Liaison Committee (PLC), shall appoint a competent\nlocal person as a Community Liaison Officer (CLO).
