The contractor shall appoint the CLO as part of\nhis site personnel, and shall direct all his liaison efforts with the local community through the appointed\nCLO.\nThe period of employment and the remuneration of the CLO shall be determined jointly by the\ncontractor, the engineer and the employer.\nThe CLO shall:\n134\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\ni. represent the community and assist the contractor, the engineer and the employer with\ncommunication between them and the community;\nii. work an 8-hour day with a total of 40 hours worked per week, and shall be present on site each\nday except when performing off-site community liaison activities;\niii. communicate daily with the contractor on labour related issues such as numbers and skill;\niv. assist in the identification and screening of local labourers from the community in accordance with\nthe contractor’s requirements;\nv. inform local labour of their conditions of employment, including their period of employment;\nvi. attend disciplinary proceedings involving local labour, and ensure that hearings are fair and\nreasonable;\nvii. attend all meetings at which the community and/or local labour are present or are required to be\nrepresented;\nviii. attend monthly site meetings to report on community and local labour matters;\nix. keep a daily written record of interviews and community liaison;\nx. submit monthly returns regarding community liaison; and\nxi. carry out all such other duties as agreed upon between all parties concerned.\nA new pay item is included in Chapter 1.2 of the schedule of quantities relating to the payment\nof the CLO on a provisional sum basis. Payment under this item shall be made only for the\nperiod for which the duties of the CLO are required, and not necessarily for the full duration of\nthe contract.\nAdd the following at the end of Sub-Clause A1.2.3.23:\nPSA1.2.3.23 Work in restricted areas\nThe following restrictive working conditions shall apply:\nTraffic accommodation:\nThe contractor shall maintain at least one lane open to accommodate traffic through the site.\nFull temporary traffic closures on P3-1 will only be permitted for the duration of the jacking operation subject\nto the approval of the Employer’s Agent. The approval of the Employer’s Agent must be sought at least 7\ndays prior to the intended closure date. Temporary closures may commence no sooner than 00h00 and\nmust be lifted before 04h00. Failure of the Contractor to comply with the above will result in non-compliance\npenalties as specified in the Contract Data.\nAccess for bridge rehabilitation:\nOnly suspended access methods shall be permitted and no working in the watercourse shall be allowed\nGeotech Investigative Site Scope of work:\nThe Geotech scope of work (see pricing schedule “D”) shall commence no later than 2weeks from the date\nof the commencement works and be completed within 1.5months from the commencement of the works.\nAdd the following new Clause A1.2.3.24:\nPSA1.2.3.24 Compliance with the Road Traffic Act\nWhen a service necessitates vehicles or plant travelling or working on a public road, the following shall\napply:\n• The vehicles and plant shall be licensed in terms of the National Road Traffic Act 1996 (Act No. 93 of\n1. as amended.\n135\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Every driver and operator of a vehicle or an item of plant shall be in possession of a valid permit in respect\nof the class of vehicle or item of plant he / she is driving or operating.\nThe Contractor shall provide, erect and maintain sufficient road signs, barricades, fencing and guarding as\nmay be necessary or required by the Employer’s Agent or by any act, regulation or statutory authority in\norder to minimise the danger and inconvenience caused to vehicle and pedestrian traffic. The Contractor\nby accepting this contract shall be deemed to have indemnified the Employer and the Employer’s Agent\nagainst any claims, damages and / or costs that may arise in this regard.\nAdd the following new payment item.\nPSC1.2 MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nItem Unit\nPSC1.2.10 Community Participation\n(a) Cost for community Participation (PLC and CLO) ......................................... Prime Cost Sum\n(b) Handling costs and profit in respect of sub-item PSC1.2.10(a) above ........... percentage (%)\nExpenditure under this item shall be made in accordance with clause 6.6 of the General Conditions of\nContract 2015.\nThe tendered percentage is a percentage of the amount of expenditure approved by the Employer’s Agent\nunder sub-item PSC1.2.10(a), and shall include full compensation for the handling costs of the Contractor\nand the profit in connection with the provision of a Community Liaison Officer.”\nC1.3 CONTRACTOR’S SITE ESTABLISHMENT AND GENERAL OBLIGATIONS\nPART C: MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nPSC1.3.1.3 Time-related obligations\nAdd the following at the end of clause C1.3.1, Under Item C1.3.1.3\n“The amount payable to the contractor for time-related general obligations arising from extensions of time\ngranted in accordance with Clause 5.12.1.and Clause 5.12.2 of the GCC 2015, shall be calculated as\nfollows:\n(i) The Contractor shall apply for the extension of time in terms of the number of days delay incurred\ncalculated in accordance with Clause 5.1 of the GCC 2015.\n(ii) The number of days extension of time calculated in accordance with Clause 5.1 of the GCC 2015,\nfinally granted shall then be added to the due completion date of the contract by the Engineer, commencing\non the first working day after the day of the original due completion date. Non-working days and special\nnon-working days as defined in the contract data shall not be counted as working days in calculating the\nextended completion date.\n(iii) The number of calendar days extension of time granted from the original completion date to the\nextended completion date as calculated in (ii) above shall then be calculated, commencing on the first\ncalendar day after the day of the original completion date.\nThe following formula shall then be used to calculate the number of months extension of time granted;\nNo. of months extension of time granted\n136\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n= [(No. of calendar days extension of time granted / 365)] × 12\n(iv) The number of months extension of time granted calculated as in (iii) above shall be the number\nof additional months measured for payment for time-related general obligations under item PSC1.3.1.3 and\nin accordance with Clause 5.12.3 of the GCC 2015 as a result of the extensions of time granted.\nNOTE: The number of months extension of time granted calculated as in (iii) above shall also be included\nin the measurement of any other items scheduled under Sections C1.3, C1.4 and C1.5 or elsewhere in the\nschedule of quantities that involve the unit of measurement "month" and that were provided on site for the\nfull duration of the extended period. Where such items were provided for a portion of the extended period\nonly, a pro rata payment shall be made, based on the number of calendar days the item was provided on\nsite after the original completion date divided by the number of calendar days as calculated in (iii) above for\nthe extension of time granted."\nNOTE: This Project Specification only makes amendments to the General Conditions of Contract should\nthe appointed contractor apply for an extension of time and is granted and approved by the Employer,\nKwaZulu-Natal Department of Transport. Item PSC1.3.1.3 is as written and specified under item C1.3.1.3\nof the South Africa Community of Transport Officials during the initial construction period and amendments\napply when extension of time is granted and approved.\nPSA1.5.7.14 Penalty events\nWhenever the following events occur, the contractor shall be subjected to penalty conditions expressed in\nthe Contract Data.\n(i) Non-compliance with accommodation of traffic specifications\nFailure or refusal on the part of the contractor to take the necessary steps to ensure the safety and\nconvenience of the travelling public, accommodation of traffic, plant and personnel in accordance with these\nspecifications or as required by statutory authorities or ordered by the engineer, shall be sufficient cause\nfor the engineer to apply penalties as follows:\nA fixed penalty per occurrence at the rate provided in the Contract Data, shall be deducted for each and\nevery occurrence of non-compliance with any of the requirements of Section C1.5 of the standard\nspecifications and section C1.5 of the project specifications.\nIn addition, a time-related penalty per hour at the rate provided in the Contract Data, over and above the\nfixed penalty, shall be deducted for non-compliance to rectify any defects in the accommodation of traffic\nwithin the allowable time after the engineer has given an instruction to this effect. The engineer's instruction\nshall state the allowable time, which shall be the time in hours for reinstatement of the defects. Should the\ncontractor fail to adhere to this instruction, the time-related penalty shall be applied from the time the\ninstruction was given.\n(ii) Late occupation of lanes, ramps and/or crossroads\nThe contractor shall be charged a lane occupation levy if he continues to occupy interchange ramps,\ncrossroads and/or carriageway lanes beyond programmed completion dates. The levy shall be deducted\nfrom payments due on the relevant interim payment certificates at the rates provided in the Contract Data\nand the Pricing Schedule.”\n137\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPSAB EMPLOYER'S AGENT OFFICE\nPSAB 3 MATERIALS\nPSAB 3.1 NAMEBOARDS\nREPLACE THE FIRST SENTENCE OF SUBCLAUSE 3.1 OF SANS 1200 AB WITH THE\nFOLLOWING:\n"The Contractor shall supply and erect at locations approved by the Employer’s Agent, one\nnameboard (contract sign board) specified in C3.2 Scope of Works which, unless otherwise\nspecified in the Contract, shall comply with the recommendations for the standard board of the\nConsulting Engineers of South Africa with regard to size, painting, decorating and detail, and\nthe requirements described hereunder."\nPSAB 3.2 OFFICE BUILDING(S)\nREPLACE THE WORDS "as scheduled" IN PARENTHESIS IN THE FIRST LINE OF\nSUBCLAUSE 3.2 OF SANS 1200 AB WITH "as specified in C3.2 Scope of Work*;\nAND REPLACE SUBCLAUSE 3.2(j) OF SANS 1200 AB WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n"(j) a heater and fan or air-conditioning unit capable of both heating in summer and cooling in\nwinter."\nADD THE FOLLOWING SUBCLAUSES TO CLAUSE 3:\nPSAB 3.3 CARPORT\nThe Contractor shall construct one carport to park two vehicles for the sole use of the\nEmployer’s Agent and his staff. Each carport shall be constructed so that the vehicle parked\nunder it is always protected against the direct rays of the sun. The carport area shall be at least\n20 m² and the floor shall be covered with a layer of crushed stone to alleviate dusty and muddy\nconditions. The carport(s) shall be positioned so as to provide easy and convenient access to\nthe Employer’s Agent’s office."\nPSAB 4 PLANT\nPSAB 4.1 CELLULAR PHONE\nREPLACE SUBCLAUSE 4.1 OF SANS 1200 AB WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n"Subject to satisfactory transmission and reception quality in the vicinity of the Site, the\nContractor shall provide one cellular phone and associated service contracts from a reputable\ncellular service provider, for the exclusive use of the Employer’s Agent and his staff. The\nContractor shall further insure the cellular phones against loss or damage from whatever\ncause, and shall ensure that all cellular phone accounts are promptly paid on the due dates\nfor payment. The Contractor shall further, at his own cost, ensure the prompt repair of all\ncellular phones provided under this clause, when reasonably required by the Employer’s\nAgent."\n138\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nADD THE FOLLOWING NEW SUBCLAUSES TO CLAUSE 4 OF SANS 1200 AB:\n"PSAB 4.2 COMPUTER EQUIPMENT\nThe Contractor shall provide computer equipment on site for the exclusive use of the Employer’s\nAgent and his staff; such computer hardware and software shall comply with the specifications\nset out in subclauses PSAB 4.2.1 and PSAB 4.2.2 hereunder.\nPSAB 4.2.1 Computer hardware\n(a) Laptop\nLaptop shall comply with the following minimum specifications:\n(i) Processor (CPU): Intel Core i7 (14th Gen)\n(ii) RAM: 16GB DDR4 or DDR5\n(iii) Storage: minimum 512GB\n(iv) Display: 17.3” Full HD touch display\n(v) Graphics: intel Arc or Intel Iris Xe\n(vi) Operating System: Windows 11\n(vii) Battery Life: 6-12 hours\n(viii) Ports: USB-C, USB-A, HDMI, headphone jack\n(ix) Connectivity: Wi-Fi 6, Bluetooth 5\n(x) Backlit keyboard\n(xi) Optical wireless mouse\n(b) Portable Wi-Fi Router\nNetwork type: 5G\nMax Speed: minimum 200 Mbps\nConnected Devices: minim 4\nBattery Life: 12-24 hours\n(c) Printers\nPrinters shall, unless otherwise approved by the Employer’s Agent, be Hewlett-Packard 600\nSeries DeskJet printers.\nAll computer hardware shall be provided complete with the requisite connecting cables and all\ninterfacing devices and software necessary for its efficient operation as an integral system.\nPSAB 4.2.2 Computer software\nThe following software shall be properly installed on the computer, and the original licence\nagreements and disks shall be provided to the Employer’s Agent for safekeeping:\n(a) Microsoft Windows (latest edition)\n(b) MS-Office (latest edition).\nPSAB 4.4 SURVEY EQUIPMENT\nThe Contractor shall provide on-site and make available for the exclusive use of the Employer’s\n139\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nAgent and his staff, the survey equipment to be determined by the Employer’s Agent..\nAll survey equipment provided by the Contractor shall be in good condition, properly calibrated\nand fit for the purpose.\nIn addition to survey equipment provided by the Contractor for the exclusive use of the Employer’s\nAgent and his staff, the Contractor shall make available for use by the Employer’s Agent,\nTechnical Staff, as determined by the Employer’s Agent at all times when such is reasonably\nrequired by the Employer’s Agent and his staff for the purposes of the Contract.\nPSAB 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPSAB 5.4.2 Cellular phones\nThe Contractor shall advise the cellular service provider of any faults which develop in the cellular\nphone service and/or the cellular phone handsets and shall, in such circumstances, arrange for\nthe earliest possible restoration of the said service.\nThe costs of any necessary repairs and/or the replacement of components to the handset of the\ncellular phone shall be for the Contractor’s account.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all accounts for cellular phone calls and the respective service\ncontracts are promptly paid. The Contractor shall, on production of an itemised statement, be\nreimbursed only for the cost of the Employer’s Agent’s cellular telephone calls."\nADD THE FOLLOWING NEW SUBCLAUSES TO CLAUSE 5 OF SANS 1200 AB:\n"PSAB 5.6 COMPUTER EQUIPMENT\nAll computer equipment provided shall be kept fully serviceable at all times by the Contractor.\nThe Contractor shall have any defective equipment repaired or replaced at his own cost within 12\nhours after notification by the Employer’s Agent's staff.\nThe Contractor shall further provide at his own cost, all paper and black ink cartridges and other\nconsumables reasonably required by the Employer’s Agent.\nPSAB 5.8 SURVEY EQUIPMENT\nAll survey equipment provided by the Contractor shall be kept fully serviceable at all times by the\nContractor. The Contractor shall have any defective equipment repaired or replaced at his own\ncost within 12 hours after notification by the Employer’s Agent's staff.\nWhere required by the Employer’s Agent, the Contractor shall, at his own cost,\n140\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.1 PARTICULAR SPECIFICATION: PES – ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT\nSPECIFICATION\nEMPLOYER'S ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT SPECIFICATION FOR ENVIRONMENTAL\nMANAGEMENT OF CONSTRUCTION PROJECTS\nTABLE OF CONTENTS\nPAGE\nF6.1 Introduction 139\nF6.2 Site Establishment and Housekeeping 145\nF6.3 Construction 147\nF6.4 Reinstatement and Rehabilitation 157\n141\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPS EMP ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT PLAN\nThe environmental management specification applicable to this contract is included in this section\nof these documents.\nThe Environmental Management Specification must be adhered to during construction. The\nEmployer’s Agent will notify the Contractor of a breach of specification and supply a time\nperiod within which remedial action will need to be carried out. Should the time period elapse\nthen the penalty will be imposed and the sum deducted from the following month’s certificate.\nAt the time of tender the Contractor shall nominate two people who will be responsible for\nensuring that the Contractor’s team and sub-contractors comply with the environmental\nmanagement requirements of this contract. While the positions are only part time, these staff\nmembers will be called upon from time to time to deal with any events that are not in compliance\nwith the specifications.\n142\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6 EMPLOYER’S ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT SPECIFICATION FOR\nENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT OF CONSTRUCTION PROJECTS\nF6.1 INTRODUCTION\nF.6.1.1 SCOPE\nThis specification is additional to the South African Bureau of Standards Standardised Specification for Civil\nEngineering Contracts and must be read in conjunction with the said specification.\nThis specification covers the principles, responsibilities and requirements generally applicable to implement\neffective environmental management during the execution of any construction contract. The aim of this\nspecification is to ensure that construction activities are conducted in an environmentally and socially responsible\nmanner.\nF.6.1.2 INTERPRETATIONS\nThis specification contains clauses that are generally applicable to the implementation of effective environmental\nmanagement on construction contracts. Interpretations of, and variations to, this specification are set out in the\nproject specification.\nF.6.1.2.1 Supporting specifications:\nReference is made to the SABS 1200 standards which are to be read in conjunction with this specification. All\naspects of these SABS requirements which are relevant to environmental management during construction\ncontracts will apply.\nF.6.1.2.2 Principles\nThe following principles should be considered at all times during construction phase activities:\n• The Environment is considered to be composed of both biophysical and social\ncomponents.\n• Construction is a disruptive activity and all due consideration must be given to the\nenvironment, particularly the social environment, during the execution of a project to\nminimise the impact on affected parties.\n• Minimisation of areas disturbed by construction activities will minimise many of the\nconstruction related environmental impacts of the project and reduce rehabilitation\nrequirements and costs.\n• As minimum requirements, all relevant standards relating to international, national,\nprovincial and local legislation, as applicable, shall be adhered to. This includes\nrequirements relating to waste emissions (e.g. hazardous, airborne, liquid and solid),\nwaste disposal practices, noise regulations, road traffic ordinance etc.\n• All effort should be made to minimise, reclaim or recycle ‘waste’ material.\nF.6.1.3 DEFINITIONS\nFor the purpose of this specification, the definitions given in SABS 1200 shall apply.\nAdditional definitions which shall apply to this specification are as follows:\nEnvironmental Control Officer: Either an Employer’s staff member or an Environmental Consultant assigned to\nthe project on a part or full-time basis. The Environmental Control Officer will be part of the Project staff and will\nadvise the Engineer on all environmental matters relating to the works, in terms of this specification and the\nproject specification, if applicable.\n143\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nEnvironmental Officer: Either an Employer’s employee (e.g. Quality Assurance Inspector) or Consultant\ndesignated to monitor the implementation and compliance with the environmental specifications and\nenvironmental management plan on a daily basis.\nCleared surface: “surface vegetation” as referred to in SABS 1200 C 2.3 will be deemed to be any woody or\nherbaceous vegetation but exclude grasses, sedges, rushes and reeds. Clearing and grubbing shall for the\npurpose of this specification mean the removal of all woody and herbaceous vegetation including stumps, but\nexcluding grass and groundcover vegetation.\nEngineer: Is to read Engineer or Supervisor (in the case of the NEC contract), whichever is applicable to the\nContract.\nInterested and Affected Parties (IAP): All persons who may be affected by the project either directly or indirectly,\nor who have an interest or stake in the area to be affected by the project. IAPs include landowners, tribal or local\nauthorities, public interest groups etc.\nLiquid Waste Stream: Any reagent solutions, fuels, oils, greases, contaminated run-off, sewerage and wash\nwater, etc.\nOpen Trench: Open trench will, for the purpose of this specification, be deemed to include: clearing and grubbing;\nstripping of topsoil; trenching; placing of bedding; pipe-laying; placing of selected fill; backfilling to ground level;\nremoving excess material; construction of cross berms to channel water (if required); and replacement of topsoil\nto final finished level (refer to Figure 1: Appendix A).\nProgressive Reinstatement: Reinstatement of disturbed areas to topsoil profile on an ongoing basis, immediately\nafter selected construction activities (e.g. backfilling of a trench) are completed. This allows for passive\nrehabilitation (i.e. natural recolonisation by vegetation) to commence. See also ‘Open Trench’ and\n‘Rehabilitation’.\nEmployers Agent: The person responsible for co-ordinating and integrating activities across multiple, functional\nlines.\nRehabilitation: Rehabilitation is defined as the return of a disturbed area to a state which approximates the state\n(where possible) which it was before disruption. Rehabilitation for the purposes of this specification is aimed at\npost-reinstatement revegetation of a disturbed area and the insurance of a stable land surface. Revegetation\nshould aim to accelerate the natural succession processes so that the plant community develops in the desired\nway, i.e. promote rapid vegetation establishment.\nRiparian vegetation: Vegetation occurring on the banks of a river or stream (i.e. vegetation fringing a water body).\nIn this specification, riparian vegetation in terms of removal, storage and replacement (see F.63 17.1 and F.63\n17.2), is only applied to sedge, grass, ground-cover, reed, bulrush, or herbaceous component of riparian\nvegetation and excludes the woody component.\nSedges: Grass-like plants growing in wetland/ marshy areas or adjacent to water.\nSubsoil: Subsoil is the soil horizons between the topsoil horizon and the underlying parent rock. Subsoil often has\nmore clay-like material than the topsoil. Subsoil is of less value to plants, in terms of nutrient (food) and oxygen\nsupply, than topsoil. When subsoil is exposed, it tends to erode fairly easily.\nTimeous: At least 5 working days prior to an activity.\nTopsoil: This is defined as the A horizon of the soil profile. Topsoil is the upper layer of soil from which plants\nobtain their nutrients for growth. It is often darker in colour, due to the organic (humic) fraction. Topsoil is\ndeemed for the purposes of this specification as the layer of soil from the surface to the specified depth\nrequired for excavation (see F.63 5.3, relevant SABS 1200 clause and project specification). Where topsoil is\nreferred to, it is deemed to be both the soil and grass / ground cover fraction. (see ‘Cleared Surface’) Veld:\n144\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThis is defined for the purpose of this specification as unimproved natural vegetation areas (e.g.\ngrasslands).\nWater body: Any open body of water including streams, dams, rivers, lakes, and the sea.\nWetland: A seasonally, temporally, or permanently wet area which also may exhibit a specific vegetation\ncommunity. It is often marshy in character.\nWetland Vegetation: Vegetation which is indicative of a wetland environment - for example, sedges, rushes, reeds,\nhydrophilic grasses and ground-covers, but for the purposes of this specification excludes woody species.\nXeriscaping: Landscaping with vegetation which has a low water usage. The objective is to conserve as much\nwater as possible, whilst still beautifying an area (i.e. conservation and aesthetics). Concept embraces utilising\nindigenous as opposed to exotic plants.\nF.6.1.4 ABBREVIATIONS\nDWAF : Department of Water Affairs and Forestry\nECO : Environmental Control Officer\nEMP : Environmental Management Plan\nEMPR : Environmental Management Programme Report EO\n: Environmental Officer\nIAPs : Interested and Affected Parties\nIEM : Integrated Environmental Management\nMSDS : Material Safety Data Sheet\nNEC : New Engineer Contract or The Engineering and Construction Contract\nIndicates the project specification must be referred to, to clarify the clause.\nF.6.1.5 DRAWINGS\nDrawings referred to in this specification are included in C4.4 Drawings of Section C4 Site Information.\nF.6.1.6 FORMS\nForms referred to in this specification are attached to this environmental specification.\nF.6.1.7 CONDITIONS OF CONTRACT\nF.61.7.1 Duties and Powers of the Employer’s Agent\nThe Employer’s Agent is ultimately responsible for ensuring compliance with the environmental specification\nand upholding the Employer’s Environmental Policy on a project.\nThe Employer’s Agent:\n• arranges information meetings for or consults with IAPs about the impending\nconstruction activities;\n• may on the recommendation of the Engineer and /or Environmental Officer order the\nContractor to suspend any or all works on site if the Contractor or his SubContractor/\nsupplier fails to comply with the said specifications;\n• maintains a register of complaints and queries by members of the public at the site\noffice as per attached pro-forma. This register is forwarded to the Environmental\nControl Officer on a monthly basis.\n145\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.1.7.2 Duties and Powers of the Engineer\nThe Engineer or Supervisor is responsible for:\n• enforcing the environmental specification on site;\n• monitoring compliance with the requirements of the specification;\n• assessing the Contractor’s environmental performance in consultation with the\nEnvironmental Officer from which a brief monthly statement of environmental\nperformance is drawn up for record purposes;\n• documenting, in conjunction with the Contractor, the state of the site prior to\nconstruction activities commencing. This documentation will be in the form of\nphotographs or video record.\nF.6.1.7.3 Duties and Powers of the Environmental Control Officer\nThe Environmental Control Officer:\n• briefs the Contractor about the requirements of the Environmental Specification and/ or\nEnvironmental Management Plan, as applicable;\n• advises the Employers Agent and Engineer/ Supervisor about the interpretation,\nimplementation and enforcement of the Environmental Specification and other related\nenvironmental matters;\n• attends site meetings, as necessary;\n• monitors the Constructor’s compliance with this specification and the project\nenvironmental specification as applicable;\n• undertakes periodic audits of the effectiveness of the environmental specifications on the\nsite;\n• communicates environmental policy issues to the Employers Agent;\n• provides technical advice relating to environmental issues to the Engineer/ Supervisor\nand Employers Agent;\n• reports on the performance of the project, in terms of environmental compliance.\nF.6.1.7.4 Duties and Powers of the Environmental Officer\nThe Environmental Officer:\n• attends site meetings;\n• monitors the site for compliance with the Environmental Specification and EMP;\n• reports on the performance of the project in terms of environmental compliance to the\nECO and Employers Agent as per the pro-forma attached;\n• liaises with the ECO on matters of policy and those requiring clarity and advice.\nF.6.1.7.5 Extent of the Contractor’s Obligations\nThe Contractor is required to:\n• provide information on previous environmental management experience and company\nenvironmental policy;\n• supply method statements for all activities requiring special attention as specified\nand/or requested by the Employers Agent, Environmental (Control) Officer and/or\nEngineer during the duration of the Contract;\n• be conversant with the requirements of this environmental specification and the\nproject specification as applicable;\n• brief his staff about the requirements of the environmental specification;\n• comply with requirements of the Environmental (Control) Officer in terms of this\nspecification and the project specification, as applicable, within the time period\nspecified;\n146\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• ensure any sub-Contractors/ suppliers who are utilised within the context of the contract\ncomply with the environmental requirements of the Employer, in terms of the\nspecifications. The Contractor will be held responsible for non-compliance on their\nbehalf;\n• bear the cost of any delays, with no extension of time granted, should he or his Sub-\nContractors/ Suppliers contravene the said specifications such that the Engineer orders\na suspension of work. The suspension will be enforced until such time as the offending\nparty(ies), procedure, or equipment is corrected;\n• bear the costs of any damages/ compensation resulting from non-adherence to the\nsaid specifications or written site instructions;\n• comply with all applicable legislation in terms of 7.6 below;\n• ensure that he informs the engineer timeously of any foreseeable activities which will\nrequire input from the Environmental (Control) Officer.\nThe Contractor will conduct all activities in a manner that minimises disturbance to directly affected residents and\nthe public in general, and foreseeable impacts on the environment.\nF.6.1.7.6 Compliance with Applicable Laws\nThe supreme law of the land is “The Constitution of the Republic of South Africa”, which states:\n“Every person shall have the right to an environment which is not detrimental to his or her health or well-being”\nLaws applicable to protection of the environment in terms of Environmental Management (and relating to\nconstruction activities) include but are not restricted to:\n• Animals Protection Act, Act No 71 of 1962\n• Atmospheric Pollution Prevention Act, No 45 of 1965\n• Conservation of Agricultural Resources Act, No 43\nof 1983\n• Environmental Conservation Act, No 73 of 1989\n• Environmental Planning Act, Act No 88 of 1967\n• Fertilisers, Farm Feeds, Agricultural Remedies and\nStock Remedies Act, No 36 of 1947\n• Forest Act, No 122 of 1984\n• Forest and Veld Conservation Act, Act No 13 of\n1941\n• Hazardous Substances Act, No 15 of 1973\n• Lake Areas Development Act No 34 of 1975\n• Land Survey Act, No 9 of 1921\n• Minerals Act, No 50 of 1991\n• Mountain Catchment Act, No 63 of 1970\n• National Monuments Act, No 28 of 1969\n• National Parks Act, No 57 of 1976\n• National Resources Development Act, Act no 51 of\n1947\n• Occupational Health and Safety Act, No 85 of 1993\n• Provincial and Local Government Ordinances and\nBylaws Soil Conservation Act, Act No 76 of 1969\n• Water Act, No 54 of 1956\n• Water Services Act No 108 of 1997\nAnd all regulations framed thereunder and amendments there to\n147\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.1.7.7 Compliance with the Environmental Specification\nThe Contractor is deemed not to have complied with the Environmental Specification if:\n• within the boundaries of the site, site extensions and haul/ access roads there is\nevidence of contravention of clauses;\n• if environmental damage ensues due to negligence;\n• the Contractor fails to comply with corrective or other instructions issued by the\nEmployers Agent or Engineer within a specified time,\n• the Contractor fails to respond adequately to complaints from the public.\nApplication of a penalty clause will apply for incidents of non-compliance. The penalty imposed will be per\nincident. Unless stated otherwise in the project specification, the penalties imposed per incident or violation will\nbe:\nFailure to demarcate working servitudes R1000\nWorking outside of the demarcated servitude R2000\nFailure to strip topsoil with intact vegetation R1000\nFailure to stockpile topsoil correctly R500\nFailure to stockpile materials in designated areas R500\nPollution of water bodies (including increased suspended solid loads) R1000\nFailure to control stormwater runoff R1000\nFailure to provide adequate sanitation R500\nUnauthorised removal of woody vegetation R2000\nFailure to erect temporary fences R500\nFailure to provide adequate waste disposal facilities and services R500\nFailure to reinstate disturbed areas within the specified time-frame R3000\nFailure to rehabilitate disturbed areas within the specified time-frame R3000\nAny other contravention of the project specific specification R400\nAny other contravention of the particular (general) environmental specification R300\n148\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.2 SITE ESTABLISHMENT AND HOUSEKEEPING\nF.6.2.1 LAYOUT\nThe Contractor will take into account any of the limitations identified in the project specification with regard to\nestablishment of site, in particular the location of access routes, and establishment layout.\nNotwithstanding the provision of a project specification, the Contractor will provide the Employers Agent and\nEnvironmental Control Officer with a layout design of the site indicating the position of all of the following, as\napplicable: offices, ablution facilities, storage areas, workshops, laboratories, batching plant, particulate matter\nstockpile area (i.e. soil/ granular chemicals/ cement fines etc), waste disposal facilities, hazardous substances\nstorage area, access routes, etc. This layout plan is to be submitted prior to site establishment for acceptance.\nAny changes to this plan require review by the Employers Agent in conjunction with the ECO.\nThe Contractor will take into account prevailing wind directions when designing the site layout to minimise\nimpacts due to dust, unpleasant odours etc.\nThe Contractor will take into account the positions of residences when designing the site layout in order to\nminimise noise impacts on the residents.\nSite security lighting is to be positioned such that the direct beam is focused away from residential properties and\ndoes not pose a nuisance or danger to road users.\nNo site establishment will be allowed within 100 m of a water body or drainage channel or on a flood plain unless\napproved by the Environmental (Control) Officer or specified in the project specification.\nF.6.2.2 SITE CLEARANCE\nNo trees or shrubs may be removed without the prior permission of the Environmental Officer, unless in keeping\nwith the final site reinstatement and rehabilitation plan.\nTopsoil is to be stripped from all areas where permanent or temporary structures and access roads are to be\nconstructed. Topsoil conservation is to be in terms of clause F.6.3 5.3 of this document.\nF.6.2.3 SERVICES\nF.6.2.3.1 Sanitation\nPortable chemical toilets are to be utilised at site unless a connection to sewer is possible or a proper septic tank\nsystem is installed. In the case of the septic tank, the installation will require the relevant approvals from the local\nauthority and will require removal upon completion of the contract, unless otherwise directed.\nSanitation facilities will be located within 100 m from any point of work, but not closer than 50 m to a water body.\nF.6.2.3.2 Solid Waste Facilities\nFacilities for solid waste collection are to be provided. These are to be at least a 200 l drum and clearly identified\nas the point for waste disposal.\nWaste is to be separated into paper, glass and metal with separate collection points for each. The Contractor will\nensure that the appropriate recycling Contractors receive this waste.\nThe Contractor is to institute a daily litter collection programme. The collected waste is to be disposed of regularly\nand proportionately to its\n149\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\ngeneration at a site designated for waste disposal.\nNo burning will be permitted on any site unless by approved incineration methods and in a low-risk fire area. In\nthe case of incineration, ash is to be co-disposed with spoil in a designated spoil dump.\nNo burying of waste will be allowed on any site.\nF.6.2.3.3 Cooking and Heating Facilities\nNo open fires will be allowed anywhere on site.\nContained fires (i.e. in a fire drum) will be allowed for heating and cooking only in designated areas, in other cases\ncooking is restricted to gas or electrical equipment.\nF.6.2.4 FUELS, HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES AND OTHER LIQUID POLLUTANTS\nF.62.4.1 Storage and handling\nAll potentially hazardous raw and waste materials are to be handled by trained staff and stored on site in\naccordance with manufacturer’s instructions and relevant legal requirements. The product MSDS is to be lodged\nwith the Engineer.\nStorage and handling areas for fuels, lubricants, chemicals and other hazardous substances are to be paved with\nconcrete to prevent accidental contamination of the soil. Alternatively, an impermeable liner may be placed\nbeneath above-ground storage tanks. The integrity of the liner is to remain intact for the duration of the contract,\nuntil removal.\nOpen storage vessels, for example shutter lubricant drums, are to be stored under cover to prevent ‘splash’\ncontamination.\nAll storage areas are to be bunded (with at least sandbags) and have a peripheral collection drain, with oil\ninterceptors (if required).\nThe bunded area is to be sufficiently large to contain a spillage equivalent to the volume of one container of the\nsubstances stored.\nAll products to be dispensed from 200 litre drums will be done so with appropriate equipment, and not\ndispensed by tipping of the drum.\nDaily checks are to be conducted on the dispensing mechanism of above-ground storage tanks to ensure the\ntimeous identification of faults.\nCollection containers (e.g. drip trays) are to be placed under all dispensing mechanisms of hydrocarbon or\nhazardous liquid substances to ensure contamination from leaks and dispensing is contained.\nThe dispensing mechanism of diesel and petrol storage tanks is to be stored in a container when not in use.\nF.6.2.4.2 Control of pollutants\nA drainage diversion system is to be installed to divert runoff from areas of potential pollution, e.g. batching area,\nvehicle maintenance area, work shops, chemical and fuel stores, etc if applicable.\nContaminated runoff and waste water is to be directed into a collection system (e.g. sump, attenuation dam, PVC\nporta-ponds etc.) for treatment or collection and disposal. The final collection point (e.g. sump) is to be PVC lined.\nCollected contaminated runoff/ wastewater is to be pumped out of the final collection point and disposed of at\nan appropriate landfill site. Sump liners are to be treated in the same manner.\n150\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe treated waste water, effluent and contaminated runoff may require analysis prior to discharge as detailed in\nthe project specification or instructed by the Environmental Officer.\nDetails regarding proposed methods for treatment of pollutants are to be submitted to the Environmental (Control)\nOfficer for acceptance upon award of the Contract.\nAny spillages, irrespective of their size, are to be contained and cleaned up immediately. The Pollution Control\nsection may provide technical assistance for clean up, if required. No spills may be hosed down into a stormwater\ndrain or sewer.\nUse of specialised cleanup techniques and/ or products may be required depending on the spill. This will be\ninstructed by the Environmental Control Officer. These will be to the Contractor’s cost.\nF.6.2.5 GENERAL\nSite staff are not permitted to use any open water body or other natural water source (e.g. springs) for purposes\nof bathing, or the washing of clothes, machinery or vehicles. Nor draw water from a spring without the permission\nof the community utilising that spring.\nF.6.2.6 MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nMeasurement and payment for compliance with clauses F.6.2.1 to 5 of the specification are deemed to be fully\nincluded in the Contractor’s rates for fixed and time related Preliminary and General Items scheduled under\nSABS 1200 A or AA.\nF.6.3 CONSTRUCTION\nF.6.3.1 CONSTRUCTION METHODS AND PROGRAMME\nF.6.3.1.1 Construction Method\nThe Contractor will provide method statements for construction activities (14 working days prior to the activity\ncommencing) relating to the following environments and those listed in the project environmental specification,\nunless methods have been prescribed in this or the project environmental specification:\n• rivers, streams, or any other open water body;\n• wetlands;\n• access roads (see F.63.13 below);\n• steep slopes (i.e. steeper than 1:4) or less if friable material is present;\n• indigenous bush/ forest;\n• close proximity (i.e. 50 m or less) to a residential dwelling;\n• drilling and/or blasting of rock.\nIf a construction method employed by the Contractor is not environmentally acceptable to the Employer, the\nContractor may be instructed to cease the utilisation of that method in favour of a more environmentally\nacceptable one, proposed either by himself or the Employer.\nF.6.3.1.2 Construction Programme\nThe Contractor will programme construction so as to minimize the impact on the environment and provide this\nprogramme to the Environmental Control Officer for perusal and acceptance at the onset of the contract period.\nThe Environmental Control Officer is to made aware of any amendments to the construction programme or\nalterations to the scope of work in order that their impacts on the environment can be assessed.\nThe Contractor (through the Employers Agent) will ensure that all affected landowners/ authorities are advised of\nthe proposed programme at the beginning of the contract period.\nF.6.3.2 AREAS OCCUPIED / DEMARCATION OF SITE\n151\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nRoutes for temporary access and haul roads are to be located within the approved demarcated areas and vehicle\nmovement is to be confined to these roads. Movement of vehicles outside the designated working areas is not\npermitted without authorization from the Engineer.\nAll construction activities are restricted to working areas designated on the drawings and/or demarcated and\napproved by the Engineer. Materials including spoil are stockpiled at designated areas.\nAny areas disturbed outside of the demarcated areas or without permission of the Environmental (Control) Officer\nor Engineer will be subject to reinstatement and rehabilitation (as per F.64 below) to the Contractor’s cost.\nIn terms of pipeline projects, a general maximum working servitude width of 15 m will apply for machine\nexcavation unless otherwise indicated in the project specification. A maximum width of 6 m will apply for manual\nexcavation. These maximum working servitude widths may vary depending on the sensitivity of the environment,\nas detailed in the project specification.\nIn sensitive biophysical environments, for example wetlands, indigenous forest / bush, pristine natural\ngrasslands, and sensitive social environments, as defined in the project specification or by the Environmental\nControl Officer, the working servitude is reduced as indicated in the project specification.\nThe working servitude shall contain all construction related activities, including, stockpiling of materials, placing\nof toilets, vehicle movement areas, etc.\nDemarcation of linear projects (executed with machine excavation) and features (e.g. pipelines, access roads,\netc.) will be by means of wooden stakes. These stakes will be at least 1 m high, painted white and placed at\nleast every 15 m, on either side of the linear feature, in all areas where works are occurring. Progressive\nmovement of stakes is required as linear projects progress.\nIn the case of a fenced site, the boundary fences will be denoted as the outermost limit of the site, but internal areas\nmay be demarcated with stakes as above. The site boundaries of non-fenced, but ‘contained’ projects are to be\ndelineated using stakes or temporary fencing, depending on the hazard which that site poses.\nF.6.3.3 SUPPLY OF WORKS FACILITIES\nNo water may be abstracted from water bodies for the purposes of construction, without approval of the Engineer\nin consultation with the Environmental Control Officer.\nF.6.3.4 CLEANLINESS\nSANS 1200 AD, clause 5.2.4, second sentence, is to read: “No rubbish or debris shall be deposited below the\nfull supply level (FSL).”\nF.6.3.5 SITE CLEARANCE\nF.6.3.5.1 Clearance\nSpoil sites will require clearing and grubbing in addition to those areas in terms of SANS 1200 C 5.1.\nThe site shall only be cleared immediately prior to construction activities commencing i.e. at the last practicable\nstage.\nNo trees or indigenous shrubs may be removed without the prior permission of the Environmental (Control)\nOfficer, unless in keeping with the final site reinstatement and rehabilitation plan.\nF.6.3.5.2 Disposal of materials\nMaterial obtained from clearing and grubbing operations shall be disposed of at appropriate municipal disposal\nfacilities. They are not to be disposed of as per Paragraph 1 of Sub-clause 3.1 of SANS 1200 C.\n152\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nWood obtained from clearing and grubbing operation remains the property of the landowner/ community and must\nbe stacked at sites designated by relevant person. The Contractor will be required to remove and dispose of any\nwood from site at a designated site for vegetation disposal, should the landowner/ community not require it.\nAll tree trunks and branches of diameter greater than 50mm are to be cut into lengths not exceeding 2400mm.\nBrush wood (i.e. < 50mm diameter) is to be disposed of, or utilised as specified in the project specification or upon\ninstruction of the Engineer.\nF.6.3.5.3 Conservation of topsoil\nThe Contractor is required to strip topsoil (as defined in this specification) together with grass, groundcover and\nsedges from all areas where permanent or temporary structures are located, construction related activities occur,\nand access roads are to be constructed, etc. The depth to which topsoil will be stripped shall be 200mm unless\nstated otherwise in the project specification.\nTopsoil is to be handled twice only - once to strip and stockpile, and secondly to replace, level, shape and scarify.\nTopsoil is to be replaced along the contour.\nTopsoil is to be replaced by direct return (i.e. replaced immediately on the area where construction is complete),\nrather than stockpiling it for extended periods. This is feasible for progressive construction (e.g. pipelines), but\nnot necessarily so for reservoirs, site establishments, dams, etc.\nTopsoil stockpiles are not to exceed 2 m in height.\nTopsoil stockpiles are to be maintained in a weed free condition (i.e. no ‘broad-leafed’ plants regarded as weeds\nin terms of the Conservation of Agricultural Resources Act No 43 of 1989, or those plants regarded as a ‘general\nnuisance in the area’ are to be growing on the stockpiles). The Environmental Control Officer will provide\nguidance as to which plants are weeds and require removal.\nThe stockpiles are not to be contaminated with sub-soil, or any other waste material. Topsoil\nmay not be compacted in any way, nor may any object be placed or stockpiled on it. Topsoil\nmay not be compacted in any way, nor may any object be placed or stockpiled on it.\nTopsoil which is to be stockpiled for periods exceeding 4 months is to be vegetated. In summer a mixture of\nEragrotis tef (Teff) and Eragrostis curvula (Weeping Lovegrass) (ratio 1:2) is to be applied at an application rate\nof 6 kg/ha, unless otherwise instructed in the project specification.\nIn winter, a mixture of Lolium multiflorum (Annual/Italian Rye grass) and Eragrostis curvula (Weeping Lovegrass)\n(ratio 1:1) is to applied at an application rate of 6kg/ha (see F.6.4 5.3 for sowing times), unless otherwise\ninstructed in the project specification. Fertiliser is to be applied as per F.6.4 5.2.\nF.6.3.5.4 Cutting of trees\nAny tree branches which require removal are to be properly pruned and sealant applied to the cut surface, if\nrequired.\nThe Contractor’s attention is drawn to Sub-clause 5.2.3.3 of SANS 1200 C with respect to work in indigenous\nforests.\nAny indigenous trees or bush which require removal in terms of the project, and which have not been identified\nin the project specification or EMP, are to be timeously indicated to the Environmental Officer prior to work\naffecting them.\n153\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.3.5.5 Landscape Preservation and Conservation of Flora\nNotwithstanding Clause 5.7 of SANS 1200 C, the Contractor will be required to transplant designated plants to\nalternative locations as specified in the project specification or identified by the Environmental Control Officer,\nupon the instruction of the Engineer.\nTransplanting shall be undertaken by employing the following method:\nRemoval\n• Mark the orientation of the tree/shrub (for example, the north-facing side of the trunk indicated by a small\narrow made with indelible ink) trunk. Do not scratch a mark on the surface of the trunk;\n• Delineate a circle from the trunk with a radius equivalent to the drip-line of the tree, or as indicated by the\nEnvironmental Control Officer on site;\n• Excavate the tree with an intact rootball\nReplanting\n• A hole 500mm larger in diameter than the anticipated rootball must be prepared in\nadvance of the tree removal in order that the tree can be replanted immediately;\n• The tree must be positioned as per its original orientation;\nA planting method known as ‘puddling’ must be employed. This method involves the\naddition of soil and water simultaneously to expels air from the planting hole. Place the\ntree in its new hole, making sure the top surface of the rootball is level with the ground\nlevel. Place a hose pipe in the hole and leave it running whilst extra soil is added around\nthe rootball;\n• ‘Compact’ the tree in the hole and attach tree stays for stabilisation.\nCompensatory planting of species may be required should transplantation not be feasible, as indicated in the\nproject specification or upon instruction of the Engineer.\nF.6.3.6 EARTHWORKS\nF.6.3.6.1 Backfill material\nWith reference to SANS 1200 DB sub-clause 3.5, no material stripped or excavated which is classed, in terms\nof this specification, as topsoil, may be used as backfill in any excavation.\nF.6.3.6.2 Excavation and backfilling\nDuring excavation ‘conservation of topsoil’, as specified in F.6.3 5.3 above will apply.\nExcavated material is to be stockpiled along a pipeline trench within the working servitude, unless otherwise\nauthorised.\nSurplus excavated soft, intermediate and hard rock material shall not be disposed of along the pipeline trench as\nindicated in SANS 1200 DB sub-clause 5.6.3 and 5.6.4, but shall be removed to a spoil site (see F.6.3.15 below)\ndesignated during the project if applicable, or agreed by the Engineer in conjunction with the Environmental\nControl Officer and Employers Agent.\nIn certain cases, for example to help stabilise the disturbed area or to reinstate the natural aesthetics of an area,\nexcess excavated intermediate and hard material may be disposed of in a designated manner along a pipeline\ntrench, as indicated by the Environmental Control Officer and Employers Agent, or in the project specification.\nIn this case, rock material shall not exceed 250mm in maximum dimension (see F.6.4 2.1).\nIn terms of SANS 1200 DB 5.6.5 and SABS 1200 LB 3.4.2, deficiency of backfill material shall not be made up\n154\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nby excavation within the free haul distance of 0.5km of site, without the prior approval of the Engineer of the\nsource of the material. Where backfill material is deficient, it should ideally be made up by importation from an\napproved borrow pit (i.e. one which operates within the ambient of an EMPR.) (See also F.6.3 14 below).\nThe Contractor will backfill in accordance with the requirements of progressive reinstatement.\nThe maximum length of open trench shall be specified in the project specification.\nF.6.3.7 SAFETY\nAll works which may pose a hazard to humans and animals are to be adequately protected and appropriate\nwarning signs erected. The Contractor’s attention is drawn to SANS 1200 D section 5.1 in this regard.\nWith reference to SANS 1200 D 5.1.1.3, where blasting is required in terms of the project, the Contractor will\nensure that all structures in the vicinity that could be affected by the activity will be inspected and their condition\nphotographically recorded (as necessary), prior to blasting.\nNotice of intent to blast is to be provided to landowners timeously.\nSpeed limits, appropriate to the vehicle driven, are to be observed at all times on access roads. Operators and\ndrivers are to ensure that they limit their potential to endanger humans and animals at all times, by observing\nstrict safety precautions.\nF.6.3.8 PLANT\nF.6.3.8.1 Silencing of plant\nWith reference to SABS 1200 A amend: “built up areas”: to read as “all areas within audible distance of\nresidents (albeit urban, peri-urban or rural areas).”\nAppropriate directional and intensity settings are to be maintained on all hooters and sirens.\nSilencer units on equipment and vehicles are to be maintained in good working order.\nConstruction activities are to be confined to normal working hours (07h30 - 17h00) Mondays to Saturdays, except\nfor the activities designated to be carried out at night.\nF.6.3.8.2 Appropriate use of plant\nThe Contractor will at all times use plant which is appropriate to the task in order to minimise the extent of damage\nto the environment.\nF.6.3.9 DEALING WITH WATER ON WORKS\nF.6.3.9.1 Disinfection of Potable Water Infrastructure\nDisinfection water is to be neutralised before release of this water to the environment.\nF.6.3.9.2 Discharge of water from site\nAny water which is discharged from site is to comply with the relevant Water Quality Guidelines implemented by\nDWS.\nWater discharged to the stormwater / sewer system may only be done so with the permission of the relevant local\nauthority.\n155\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.3.10 CONTROL OF EROSION\nSurface erosion protection measures will be required to prevent erosion where slopes are steeper than 1:8 on\nall soil types.\nErosion protection measures required may include all or some of the below, as specified in the project\nspecification or upon instruction of the Engineer in conjunction with the Environmental (Control) Officer:\n• use of groundcover or grass\n• construction of cut off berms (earth and/or rockpack) - these are to be angled across the\ncontour and normally would approximate an angle of 30o from the bisector of the contour.\n• placing of brush wood on bare surface;\n• pegging of wattle trunks or branches along the contour;\n• hard landscaping, e.g. use of Loffelstein walls, ground anchors, gabions etc.\nScour chambers are to be fitted with energy dissipaters, or the jet of water directed onto a protected (i.e. grouted\nstone pitching/ rock pack/ reno mattress) area to dissipate water velocity and to control and prevent erosion.\nStorm water drainage measures might be required on site to control runoff and prevent erosion.\nF.6.3.11 CONTROL OF POLLUTION\nNo waste in a solid, liquid or gaseous state shall be emitted from or spilled on the site without the approval of\nthe Engineer.\nNo mixed concrete shall be deposited directly onto the ground prior to placing. A board or other suitable platform\nis to be provided onto which the mixed concrete can be deposited whilst it awaits placing.\nExcess concrete from mixing shall be deposited in a designated area awaiting removal to an approved landfill site.\nThe Contractor will contain wash water from cement mixing operations, by directing the water into a sump for\ncollection. The material contained in the sump will be removed to an appropriate landfill site.\nNo concrete rubble shall be present at the site.\nLiquid wastes will not be disposed of to storm water drains. They may be disposed of to sewer only if permitted\nby (local council) legislation.\nIn the event of pollution of a water body (including sediment loading), the Contractor will provide alternative water\nsupply to users of that water body until the quality of the water body is restored to its previous unpolluted state. For\nthe sake of this clause, pollution is deemed to be a state which is substandard to the normal quality of the water\nbody, but is not necessarily in contravention of the South African Water Quality guideline standards for a\nprescribed activity.\nAny ancillary damages resulting from pollution of a water body will be repaired / remediated at the\nContractor’s cost.\nWhere, due to construction requirements, pollution of a water body may potentially occur, the Contractor is to\nensure adequate measures (e.g. attenuation/ settlement dams / oil absorbent products) are in place to prevent\npollution. A method statement is to be provided to this effect (see F.6.3 1).\nF.6.3.12 CONTROL OF FIRE\nThe Contractor will ensure he has the necessary fire fighting equipment on site in terms of SANS 1200. This will\ninclude at least rubber beaters when working in ‘veld’ areas, and at least one fire extinguisher of the appropriate\ntype when welding activities are undertaken, irrespective of the site.\n156\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.3.13 USE AND MAINTENANCE OF ACCESS FACILITIES\nF.6.3.13.1 Responsibility\nThe Employers Agent [not the Contractor (SANS 1200 AD 5.3.1)] will be responsible for obtaining permission for\ntemporary and permanent rights of way over all private property affected by project activities.\nThe Employers Agent will ensure that the Contractor has kept a photographic record of all access facilities and\nthat these are reinstated to a state not worse than upon commencement of the project and to the satisfaction of\nthe landowner (not withstanding that the project’s objective is not to upgrade landowners’ access roads).\nF.6.3.13.2 Fencing\nTemporary fencing is to consist of 1.2 m bonnox fencing, or similar, suitably tensioned and supported on 1.8 m\nfencing standards at 3 m intervals, with all necessary straining posts and stays.\nAll temporary fencing as indicated by the Engineer is removed on completion of the contract.\nF.6.3.13.3 New Access Roads\nAny construction roads created for execution of the project are to be designed to incorporate adequate drainage\nand water attenuation structures.\nAny access roads which incorporate ‘cut and fill’ aspects and/or which are to be surfaced during construction are\nto be authorised by the Environmental Control Officer and Employers Agent. Prior to construction of the road,\nthe Contractor will be required to provide a sketch plan of the road layout (referenced to local topographic, natural\nand man-made structures). Slope steepness, road width, drainage structures and their frequency will need to be\ndocumented and accompany the sketch layout.\nConstruction access roads may not be wider than that necessary (maximum width 4 m) for movement of vehicles\nin one direction only. Should two-way traffic be required, points people are to control vehicle movement on the\n‘single lane’ road or passing bays are to be used where specified in the project specification or as identified by the\nEngineer in conjunction with the Environmental Control Officer, unless otherwise stated in the project specification.\nThe cut and fill slopes of permanent roads will require grassing, as specified in the project specification or by the\nEnvironmental Control Officer, to increase stability and reduce aesthetic impacts. Hard landscaping may be\nrequired as per the project specification.\nTemporary construction roads will require rehabilitation on completion of construction activities for which they were\nrequired. These roads will require rehabilitation as per F.6.4 4 or as specified in the project specification.\nIn the case of access ‘tracks’, only ripping to loosen compaction will be required unless otherwise stated by the\nEnvironmental Control Officer or project specification.\nAccess roads created by the project may only remain unrehabilitated on written request of the landowner, with\nhis acceptance of the state of the road and a clause that the landowner accepts all responsibility for the road and\nits state.\nF.6.3.13.4 Maintenance of Existing Access Roads\nThe Contractor will record, photographically, the state of existing roads which are to be used for access, prior to\nplant utilising these roads.\nDuring the contract period, the Contractor will ensure that all existing water attenuation and drainage structures\nare maintained in a state in which they can optimally perform their function.\nUpon completion of the construction period, the Contractor will ensure that the access roads are returned to a\nstate not worse than prior to construction commencing.\n157\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.3.14 BORROW PITS\nWhere the Contractor is required to import material, this shall be from commercial sources or borrow areas\nspecified in the project specification.\nThe Contractor may source material from alternative borrow pits provided: the site location; method of winning\nmaterial and reinstatement and rehabilitation are environmentally acceptable and approved by the Environmental\nControl Officer.\nIn this regard, the Contractor shall give the Environmental Control Officer in writing, 30 days prior to opening up\nalternative borrow pits the following information for acceptance:\n• quantities of borrow material required;\n• method statement for excavation of material including depth and extent of excavation;\n• anticipated ‘active life’ of the borrow area;\n• proposal for reinstatement and rehabilitation of borrow area, including final profile;\n• written approval from the landowner/ relevant authority that material may be removed\nfrom their land subject to their stated conditions, requirements, and royalties, and if the\nproposal is acceptable to the Environmental Control Officer.\nDevelopment and rehabilitation of borrow pit areas are likely to include the following activities (but these must not\nbe regarded as exhaustive):\n• Stripping and stockpiling of topsoil as per F.6.3 5.3 of this specification;\n• Removal (to nominal depth of 500mm) and stockpiling of sub-soil;\n• Infill of borrow pit with spoil material;\n• Contouring of borrow pit to approximate natural topography and/ or reduce erosion\nimpacts on the site;\n• Placement of excavated subsoil over spoil material;\n• Placement of stripped topsoil on subsoil;\n• Grassing of topsoil in terms of clause F.6.4 4 of this specification.\nThe Contractor is to familiarise himself with the requirements of the Minerals Act No 50 of 1991 in terms of borrow\npit development, and the requirements of the EMPR, as applicable.\nF.6.3.15 SPOIL SITES\nWhere the Contractor is required to spoil material, spoil sites must be identified which are environmentally\nacceptable and approved by the ECO, unless spoil site areas have been identified in the project specification, in\nwhich case these will be the designated spoil sites.\nIf no spoil sites have been previously identified together with reinstatement and rehabilitation criteria, the Contractor is to\nprovide the following information to the ECO at least 30 days prior to requiring sites to spoil material:\n• the location, description of and access to alternative sites identified in order that they\nmay be assessed;\n• the quantity of material to be spoiled;\n• the type of material to be spoiled (i.e. blast rock/ excavated rock/ soft shale/ subsoil\netc.);\n• the proposed method of spoiling;\n• the proposed reinstatement and rehabilitation plan including final profile;\n• written approval from the landowner/ relevant authority that material may be spoilt on\nland subject to their stated conditions and requirements and if the proposal is\nacceptable to the ECO.\nDevelopment and rehabilitation of spoil areas are likely to include the following activities (but these must not be\n158\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nregarded as exhaustive):\n• Stripping and stockpiling of topsoil as per F.6.3 5.3 of this specification;\n• Removal (to nominal depth of 500mm) and stockpiling of sub-soil;\n• Placement of spoil material;\n• Contouring of spoil site to approximate natural topography and/ or reduce erosion\nimpacts on the site;\n• Placement of excavated subsoil over spoil material;\n• Placement of stripped topsoil on subsoil;\nGrassing of topsoil in terms of clause F.6.4 4 of this specification.\nF.6.3.16 NUISANCE\nF.6.3.16.1 Dust\nAt all times the Contractor shall control dust on the site, access roads, borrow pits and spoil dumps with water,\nchemical soil stabilisers or temporary surfacing as specified in the project specification or upon instruction of the\nEngineer.\nDust control shall be sufficient so as not to have significant impacts in terms of the biophysical and social\nenvironments. These impacts include visual pollution, decreased safety due to reduced visibility, health aspects,\nand ecological impacts due to dust particle accumulation.\nOn gravel or earth roads, vehicle speeds may not exceed 30km per hour.\nF.6.3.16.2 Noise\nThe operational layout of the construction site is to be designed to control and reduce noise from source (see\nclause F.6.2 1).\nMachinery and vehicle silencer units are to be maintained in good working order. Offending machinery and\n/Or vehicles will be banned from use on site until they have been repaired.\nConstruction activities generating output levels of 85 dB(A) or more (excessively noisy), in residential areas, are\nto be confined to working hours (08h00 - 17h00) Mondays to Fridays only.\n‘Normal’ or ‘noisy’ working hours may only be extended with the prior written approval of the Employers Agent, who\nhas been notified, at least 7 days in advance, of the impending work requiring extension.\nThe Employers Agent will ensure that the neighbours are timeously forewarned of imminent noisy activities.\nShould community complaints be received with regard to noise generation, the Contractor will, at the discretion\nof the Employers Agent and Environmental Control Officer, provide an independent and registered noise monitor\nto undertake a survey of noise output levels from site, and implement measures to reduce noise to legislated\nlevels.\nF.6.3.16.3 Visual\nAll site establishment components, as well as equipment, will be positioned to limit visual intrusion to neighbours\n(see clause F.6.2 1 above).\nThe type and colour of roofing and cladding materials are to be selected to reduce reflection.\nSecurity lighting (both temporary and permanent) and lighting required for specific works activities must be\nplaced such that it is not a nuisance to residents and the general public.\n159\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.3.16.4 Interference with neighbours and public\nNo construction staff may approach site neighbours, for whatever reason, without the knowledge and permission\nof the Employers Agent.\nComplaints from neighbours and public with regard to interference from contract staff will be regarded in a serious\nlight, and the offender(s) may be subject to disciplinary action.\nF.6.3.16.5 Disruption of Services\nDisruption of services, e.g. road access, water and electricity, must be kept to a minimum at all times.\nWhere service disruption is unavoidable, the Contractor is to advise the Employers Agent (at least 7 days in\nadvance), who in turn will timeously warn the affected parties.\nF.6.3.17 SPECIAL ENVIRONMENTS\nF.6.3.17.1 Wetlands\nPipeline trenches which traverse wetlands shall be constructed as specified in the project specification. The\nContractor will submit a method statement for work in wetland areas as per F.6.3 1.1\nConstruction may not permanently alter the surface or subsurface flow of water through the wetland.\nThe Contractor shall submit a method statement for review at least 14 days prior to commencing construction in a\nwetland.\nThe Contractor will remove all wetland vegetation with their root ball intact. This vegetation is to be kept moist at\nall times. It is to be placed in the shade and covered with moistened hessian cloth until replanting, which is to be\nundertaken immediately surface reinstatement is complete.\nNo construction materials may be stockpiled in any wetland areas.\nThe pre-construction profile of the wetland shall be returned to one similar as before construction, with no\ncreated “ridge or channel” features present.\nF.6.3.17.2 River/ stream courses\nThe Contractor shall submit a method statement for review 14 days prior to commencing construction. The\nmethod statement should highlight (but not be confined to) the following issues:\n• detailed plan of crossing including pipe protection works;\n• how water flow will be diverted during construction (if applicable);\n• containment of contaminated runoff and waste water;\n• width of working servitude (if not already detailed in project specification);\n• final expected profile of river/ stream banks;\n• reinstatement and rehabilitation of river/ stream banks.\nThe Contractor will remove herbaceous riparian vegetation as indicated in the project specification or by the\nEnvironmental Control Officer, with their root ball intact. This vegetation is to be kept moist by means of placing\nit in the shade, covered with moistened hessian cloth until it is replanted.\nThe Contractor shall not modify the banks or bed of a water course unless as specified in the project specification.\nRocks for use in gabion baskets/reno mattresses may not be obtained from a water course.\nThe Contractor will not pollute any water body as a result of construction activities (see also F.63 11).\n160\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Contractor shall not cause any physical damage to any aspects of a water course, other than those\nnecessary to complete the works as specified and in accordance with the accepted method statement\nWhere a stream or river-crossing requires the diversion of water, a method statement is to be provided to the\nEnvironmental Control Officer in this regard for review.\nF.6.4 REINSTATEMENT AND REHABILITATION\nScope: The intention of this section is to ensure that the condition of the areas disturbed by the project are\nreturned to a state that approximates what they were before the project or better, within reason. The concept of\nprogressive reinstatement is fundamental to cost effective (both financial and environmental) rehabilitation of a\nsite. This concept must be followed at all times. Where landscaping is utilised, the concept is to utilise and restore\nindigenous plants to the site, in terms of the concept of xeriscaping.\nReinstatement will be required for all areas disturbed by the project. For pipeline projects, this will include the\nfull working servitude, not just the top of actual excavation as per SANS 1200 DB (subclause 5.9.1.1)\nReinstatement and rehabilitation will ensure that all areas disturbed by the project are returned, within reason, to a\nstate not worse than before the project commenced.\nThe Contractor will reinstate and rehabilitate all disturbed areas outside of the demarcated working area (as\ndefined in terms of clause F.6.3 2 or the project specification) at his own cost and to the satisfaction of the\nEnvironmental Control Officer and Employers Agent.\nF.6.4.1 HOUSEKEEPING\nAll areas are to be cleared of rubble associated with construction. This includes the removal of surplus materials,\nexcavation and disposal of consolidated waste concrete and concrete wash water, litter, etc.\nAll soil contaminated by hydrocarbons, for example from leaking machines, refuelling spills etc., is to be\nexcavated to the depth of contaminant penetration, placed in 200 litre drums and removed to an appropriate\nlandfill site.\nF.6.4.2 FINISHING\nF.6.4.2.1 Final Grading\nFinal levels of all disturbed areas are, where feasible in terms of the project requirement, to be consistent with\nthe natural topography of the area.\nIn certain instances, it will be acceptable to reinstate rock onto a works area (e.g. pipeline servitude), provided that\nthat rock does not exceed 250mm in maximum dimension and is placed in a manner consistent with the natural\nsurrounds as indicated by the Environmental Control Officer and Employers Agent.\nAll drainage lines affected by construction are to be reinstated to approximate their original profile. Where this is\nnot feasible due to technical constraints, the profile is to be agreed upon by the Environmental Control Officer and\nEmployers Agent.\nAll compacted (disturbed) areas (including stockpile areas) are to be ripped (along contour) to a depth of 150mm\nprior to the replacement of topsoil.\nF.6.4.2.2 Topsoiling\nTopsoil is to be replaced to a minimum depth of 100mm.\nTopsoil is not to be compacted, but once replaced is to be scarified (to a depth of 50mm) consistent with the\nnatural contour.\n161\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nIf insufficient topsoil is available, subsoil or similar material may be used that may be a suitable substrate after\naddition of soil improving substances e.g. compost, pH rectifiers (lime or gypsum) etc. Soil testing may be\nrequired at an approved facility.\nF.6.4.3 REINSTATEMENT OF WATER COURSES AND WETLAND AREAS\nThe Contractor will ensure that water course banks are returned to their original profile unless the project\nspecification states otherwise.\nThe surface reinstatement of wetland areas is to ensure that no depressions remain which could act as channels\nfor preferential water flow thereby affecting the hydrological regime of the wetland.\nThe Contractor will preserve all riparian and wetland vegetation for use in rehabilitation of those environments.\nThis vegetation is to be kept moist at all times. It is to be placed in the shade and covered with moistened hessian\ncloth until replanting, which is to be undertaken immediately surface reinstatement is complete.\nPlants are to be, as nearly as possible, replanted in areas from which they were removed.\nF.6.4.4 VEGETATION RE-ESTABLISHMENT\nThe Contractor will ensure that all areas disturbed by contract activities are revegetated to the specified standard.\nThis standard is deemed to be an 85 % cover with no areas in excess of 0.04 m2 / m2 remaining unvegetated.\nRevegetation shall match the vegetation type which previously existed (e.g. kikuyu pastures are to be returned\nto kikuyu pasture; veld’ grass to ‘veld’ grass, etc.), unless stated otherwise in the project specification.\nPrior to re-grassing, and if required:\n• the area is to be scarified or ripped (along contour) to a depth of 50mm to loosen\ncompaction.\n• weeds present on site are to be removed.\nRe-grassing, where required, will be either by means of seeding, instant turf (sods), sprigs or plugs as specified\nin the project specification or as specified by the ECO.\nWhere sprigs or plugs are utilised, they are to be planted at 200mm centres. The fertiliser shall be applied as per\nF.6.4 5.2. During summer, 25mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until reasonable (60%) ground cover\nhas been obtained. During winter 15mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until reasonable (60%) ground\ncover has been obtained. The amount of irrigation to be applied will make up the difference between rainfall\nrecorded on site and minimum requirement.\nWhere instant turf is utilised, it shall be laid as specified in the project specification. The fertiliser shall be applied\nas per F.64 5.2. During summer, 25mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until all the turf is visibly growing.\nDuring winter 15mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until all the turf is visibly growing. The amount of\nirrigation to be applied will make up the difference between rainfall recorded on site and minimum requirement.\nGrassing shall be undertaken by a specialist grassing Sub-Contractor, unless permission is granted otherwise\nby the Engineer upon receipt of a written motivation from the Contractor.\nThe Contractor shall state in writing when the regrassing operation will commence and its expected duration\n(dates).\nGrassing in ‘veld’ areas is to be undertaken as per F.6.4 5 below. Cynodon dactylon species may be excluded or\nsubstituted from this mixture at the discretion of the Environmental Control Officer, or as specified in the project\nspecification.
