The seed bulk may be made up with the Eragrostis tef.\n162\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.6.4.5 “VELD GRASS” GRASSING SPECIFICATION\nThe area to be grassed should be estimated and converted to hectares,\ne.g. 100m X 100m = 10 000m2 = 1ha. All fertilizer and seeding rates used in this specification are with respect to\nhectares.\nF.6.4.5.1 Regional areas\nFor re-grassing three distinctive areas exist. These are defined as:\n• the Coastal area (a narrow band running from the coast to 15km inland of the coast)\n• the Coastal hinterland (a broad band (50km wide), generally defined as westwards of the\ncoastal belt, and below 800m a.s.l.)\n• the area above  800m a.s.l. (also called Midlands area).\nF.6.4.5.2 Fertiliser\nStandard 2:3:2 (N:P:K) fertiliser shall be used on all sites. The\nrate of application will be:\n• 200 kg/ha in the Coastal Hinterland areas, and\n• 300 kg/ha in the Midlands and Coastal areas.\nF.6.4.5.3 Planting times\nSummer (includes Spring) is considered to be between the 1 September and 28 (29) February.\nWinter (includes Autumn) is considered to be between 1 March and 31 August.\nRe-grassing will be undertaken (as far as possible) in summer as germination and establishment of grasses is\nmost effective, assuming reasonable spring rains.\nVegetation re-establishment is likely in many cases to be held off until this suitable growing season.\nHydroseeding with a winter mix will only be specified where regrassing is urgently required and cannot wait until\nthe summer season. In this case irrigation will be required as per F.6.4 5.4 below.\nF.6.4.5.4 Establishment and maintenance\nDuring summer, 25mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until reasonable (60%) ground cover has been\nobtained.\nDuring winter (where annual rye grass is specified) 15mm of irrigation shall be applied each week until\nreasonable (60%) ground cover has been obtained.\nIf rapid establishment is required, additional watering may be necessary as specified in the project\nspecification\nThe amount of irrigation to be applied will make up the difference between rainfall recorded on site and the\nminimum requirement.\nF.6.4.5.5 Grass Seed Selection and Application Rates\nThe specific seed selection and application rates for each of the defined areas are covered separately, as follows.\nF.6.4.5.5.1 Coastal area\nSummer mix (1 September - 28 February)\n163\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nEragrostis tef Teff 5\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 10\nDigitaria eriantha Smuts’ fingergrass 5\nTotal 30\nWinter mix (1 March - 31 August)\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nLolium multiflorum Annual/Italian rye grass 10\ncultivar - Midmar\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 5\nTotal 25\nF.6.2.1.1.1 Coastal hinterland.\nSummer mix (1 September - 28 February)\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nEragrostis tef Teff 5\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 10\nCenchrus ciliarus Blue buffalo grass 2\nCynodon dactylon Couch/KWeek/Star grass 10\nTotal 37\nWinter mix (1 March - 31 August)\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nLolium multiflorum Annual/Italian rye grass 10\ncultivar – Midmar\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 5\nCenchrus ciliarus Blue buffalo grass 2\nCynodon dactylon Couch/KWeek/Star grass 3\nTotal 30\nF.6.2.1.1.2 Midlands area\nSummer mix (1 September - 28 February)\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nEragrostis tef Teff 4\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 10\nDigitaria eriantha Smuts’ fingergrass 2\nCynodon dactylon Couch/KWeek/Star grass 2\nPaspalum notatum Lawn paspalum 2\nTotal 30\n164\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nWinter mix (1 March - 31 August)\nGrass species Common name General application rate (kg/ha)\nLolium multiflorum Annual/Italian rye grass 10\ncultivar - Midmar\nEragrostis curvula Weeping lovegrass 10\nChloris gayana Rhodes grass 5\nPaspalum notatum Lawn paspalum 2.5\nTotal 27.5\nF.6.2.1.2 Seeding methods\nTwo methods are recommended, namely hydroseeding and hand-broadcasting. The required method shall be\nas specified in the project specification.\nAll seed supplied should be labelled in accordance with the Government Seed Act No. 20 of 1961 and the\nContractor shall be required to produce such certification, if requested by the Engineer.\nF.6.2.1.2.1 Hydroseeding\nThe Grassing Contractor shall be conversant with this method.\nCellulose pulp (consisting of either wood shavings, shredded straw, shredded paper or cotton waste) shall be\nadded to the mix to be applied at a rate of 250 kg/ha.\nIn addition to the cellulose pulp, compost (consisting of either chicken litter, kraal manure, sugar cane filter cake or\nmushroom compost) shall be incorporated at a rate of 5m3/ha (100 X 50kg fertiliser bags/ha).\nF.6.2.1.2.2 Hand-broadcasting\nFertiliser, at the appropriate rate, is to be distributed by hand in a manner to ensure that there is an even spread\nof fertiliser over the site. This is to be done prior to seeding.\nThe seed mix is to be weighed and made up in an appropriately large container which shall be stirred to\nensure no settling out of the grass seed, and a uniform distribution of the different types of seed.\nThe seed is to distributed by hand in a regular grid broadcasting manner to ensure that there is an even spread\nof grass over the entire site.\nThe area seeded is to be raked over once the seed and fertiliser have been applied to incorporate these elements\ninto the topsoil.\nF.6.4.5.6 General\nWhere there is a possibility of neighbourhood livestock grazing a rehabilitated site, these should, as far as is\npracticable, be excluded for the first 3 months of re-grassing.\nF.6.4.6 LANDSCAPING\nLandscaping of the site may be required as indicated in the project specification.\nCompensatory planting of trees or shrubs may be required should the transplantation of such not be\nsuccessful in terms of F.6.3 5.5 or due to plants removed in terms of F.6.3 5.4\nPlanting of trees will be in accordance with the following method:\n• All tree holes shall be square in plan;\n• Tree holes shall be a minimum of 600mm by 600mm square by 700mm deep;\n165\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Holes are to be backfilled with excavated soil in a ratio of 3:1 with compost. The compost\nis to be weed free and have been composted at temperatures in the order of 65oC.\nWhere possible, any available topsoil should be placed in the hole at the level where the\ntree rootball will rest. A handful (half-a-cup) of each Superphosphate and\n2.3.2 should be mixed into the soil-compost mix;\n• The tree holes are to be backfilled to the point where the tree and its rootball are in the\ndesired position. The tree is to be removed temporarily and the hole filled with water and\nallowed to drain away. This operation of watering and draining should be repeated at\nleast four times in order that the surrounding ground and hole are thoroughly moist. The\ntree is then to be replaced and the remaining soil replaced;\n• All trees shall be tied (using a tree tie) to a suitable timber stake planted in the ground to a\ndepth of at least 500mm. The stake shall have a minimum diameter of 35mm and shall be\nat least 300mm higher than the planted tree;\n• Water retaining basins of at least 500mm diameters are to be formed around each\ntree;\n• The Contractor is to apply at least 10 litres of water per tree per fortnight for a period of\nat least 3 months.\nThe planting of shrubs will be in accordance with the tree planting method with the exception that the holes are\nto be a minimum of 400mm by 400mm square by 500mm deep, and that the tree stakes and ties are not required.\nF.6.4.7 ALIEN PLANT CONTROL\nAll sites disturbed by construction activities will be monitored for colonisation by invasive alien plant species. The\nEnvironmental Control Officer will identify those plants which require removal during both the\nconstruction and maintenance period, for the Contractor’s action.\nThe Environmental Control Officer will provide advice as to effective methods of removal and control of alien plant\nspecies.\n166\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPUBLIC COMPLAINTS REGISTER\nDATE\nREFERRED TO\nCOMPLAINANTS DESIGNATION/ REASON FOR ACTION BY ACHIEVED\nDATE ACTION TAKEN ACTION BY NW\nNAME AFFILIATION COMPLAINT DATE BY DATE\nenvironmental\ncontrol officer\n167\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nMONITORING OF COMPLIANCE WITH ENVIRONMENTAL SPECIFICATIONS\nPROJECT NAME:\n………………………………………………………………………………………………..\nCONTRACT NUMBER:\n………………………………………………………………………………………….\nEMPLOYERS AGENT:\n…………………………………………………………………………………………\nENGINEER’S SUPERVISOR:\n……………………………………………………….\nCONTRACTOR:\n………………………………………………………………………………………………….\nCONTRACT PERIOD:\n……………………………………………………………………………………………\n(including start and completion dates):\nPERIOD COVERED:\n……………………………………………………………………………………………\nREPORT PREPARED BY:\n………………………………………………………………………………………\n…………………\nSignature\n168\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nENVIRONMENTAL CONTROL OFFICER REPORT PROJECT\nNAME:\nCONTRACT N°:\nDATE OF SITE INSPECTIONS DURING REPORTING PERIOD:\nSpecification Remedial Action Authority Action\nSpec. No. Due Date\nBreach Recommended Responsible Taken\nPUBLIC COMPLAINTS\nDesignation/ Date of Reason for Action taken\nComplainant\nAffiliation complaint Complaint and date\nGOOD PERFORMANCE REPORT\nList any aspects of the Contract in which the Contractor is performing well and beyond that which is\nrequired in terms of the specification.\nPhotographs\nInclude photographs which illustrate aspects of non-compliance and good performance.\nPhotograph 1 Photograph 2\nCaption Caption\n169\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.7 PARTICULAR SPECIFICATION PE- THE CLIENT’S\nPRE- CONSTRUCTION HEALTH PLAN\nPE1. INTRODUCTION\nPE1.1 Purpose and Scope\nThis document describes the procedure upon which the Contractor shall comply with the\nrequirements set out in the client’s Health and Safety Specification.\nThis document defines the Management System that is implemented by the Contractor for the management\nof Health and Safety on the project, which includes ensuring subcontractor compliance with the same\nstandards.\nThe aim of this document is to present the safety aspects that will be controlled and managed on the\nproject.\n(a) Reference Documents\n• Occupational Health and Safety Act, (Act No. 85 of 1993)\n• Compensation for Occupational Injury and Diseases Act.\n• Client Health and Safety Specification.\n• Construction Regulations 2003.\n• The Construction Kit. (CD)\nPE1.2 Definitions\nThe following definitions will apply to the Safety Management Plan, acronyms given hereunder shall\napply:\nConstruction / Building Work (as defined by the Occupational Health and Safety Act: Construction\nRegulations 2003):\nMeans any work in connection with –\na) The erection, maintenance, alteration, renovation, repair, demolition or dismantling of or an addition\nto a building or any similar structure;\nb) The installation, erection, dismantling or maintenance of a fixed plant where such work\nincludes the risk of a person falling;\nc) The construction, maintenance, demolition or dismantling of any bridge, dam, canal, road, railway,\nrunway, sewer or water reticulation system or any similar civil Employer’s Agenting structure; or\nd) The moving of earth, clearing of land or making of an excavation or work on any similar type of\nwork.\nHazard Identification and Risk Assessment and Risk Control (HRA)\nMeans a documented plan, which identifies hazards, assesses the risks and detailing the control\nmeasures and safe working procedures, which are to be used to mitigate and control the occurrence\nof hazards and risks during construction or operation phases.\nSite\nMeans the area in the possession of the Contractor for the construction of the works. Where there is no\ndemarcated boundary it will include all adjacent areas, which are reasonably required for the activities for\nthe Contractor and approved for such use by the Employer’s Agent and/or client.\n170\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Act\nMeans, unless the context indicates otherwise, the Occupational Health and Safety Act, 1993 (ACT N⁰.\n85 of 1993) and Regulations promulgated there under. (OHSA)\nHazard\nMeans a source of or exposure to danger (source which may cause injury or damage to persons, or\nproperty)\nRisk\nMeans the probability or likelihood that a hazard can result in injury or damage.\nContractor’s Responsible Person (S)\nMeans any person appointed in writing by the Contractor to supervise construction or building work.\nThe appointment shall be as required by the OHSA which shall stipulate health and safety\nresponsibilities, area of responsibility and the proposed duration of the project.\nHazardous Chemical Substance (HCS)\nMeans any toxic, harmful, corrosive, irritant or asphyxiant substance, or a mixture or substances for\nwhich an occupational exposure limit is prescribed, or an occupational exposure limit is not prescribed,\nbut which creates a hazard to health.\nConstruction Plant (TEM)\nEncompasses all types of plant including but not limiting to, cranes, piling frames, boring machines,\nexcavators, dewatering equipment and road vehicles with or without lifting equipment.\nContractor\nMeans “subcontractor”.\nHealth and Safety Program\nEncompasses The Contractor safety planning spreadsheet.\nHealth and Safety Plan (HSP)\nThe content of this document which will be made available on site for inspection by an inspector, Technical\nOfficer, Agent, subcontractor, employee, registered employee organisation, health and safety\nrepresentative, or member of the health and safety committee.\nHealth and Safety File\nDescribes the safety file holding all records on health and safety for the project, which shall be available\nat all, times for evaluation, and copy of which will be forwarded to the client upon completion of the project.\nPE1.3 Responsibilities\n(a) Notification of Intention to Commence Construction Work\nThe Provincial Director of the Department of Labour shall be notified by the appointed safety consultant to\nthe Contractor, immediately upon receipt of the Letter of Acceptance of project commencement in accordance\nwith the following requirement\n171\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe demolition of a structure exceeding a height of 3 meters; or the use of\nexplosives to perform construction work; or\nThe dismantling of fixed plant at a height greater than 3 meters; or\nThe work exceeds 30 days or will involve more than 300 person days of construction work; and Includes\nexcavation work deeper than 1 meter; or\nIncludes working at a height greater than 3 meters above ground or a landing.\nA copy of the notification letter to the Provincial Director shall be forwarded to client for their records\nand shall be made available to an Inspector, Project Employer’s Agent or employee.\n(b) Assignment of Contractor’s Responsible Persons to Supervise Health and Safety on Site\nThe Contracts Manager and Site Agent shall ensure copies of all the appointment letters of the responsible\npersons appointed on site will be made available to the client and that all legal appointments shall be\nconducted in accordance with the requirements set out in the OHSA and Client specifications. The above shall\nalso be imposed upon all subcontractors.\n(c) Safety Officer Appointment\nA part--time Health and Safety consultant shall be appointed upon commencement of the project.\nThe safety officers shall be tasked with monthly inspections of the site, the results of which shall be forwarded\nto the client or his appointed representative.\n(d) Risk Assessment Competent Person\nThe Employers Agent shall appoint a competent person in writing at commencement of the project to control\nthe risk assessment process on site. A copy of the risk assessment appointment is attached with duties and\nresponsibilities defined. (Annexure E)\n(e) Competency for Contractor’s Responsible Persons\nThe Employers Agent acknowledges that all management personnel (responsible for health and safety) shall\nundergo a half-day Health and Safety Management Course, which is to be arranged and conducted by the\nappointed safety consultant prior to commencement of activities on site.\n(f) Health and Safety Representatives\nAt least one (1) Health and Safety Representative shall be nominated, elected and trained to carry out his /\nher functions in his / her area of responsibility. This will include areas where less than fifty\n(50) employees are engaged in activity. Employees elected shall be designated in writing for a specific area\nand period of time.\nThe designated persons shall conduct monthly inspections within their area of responsibility; the records shall\nbe kept for auditing and that deviations recorded are reported to the responsible supervisor within the\ndesignated persons area so that appropriate action can be taken.\nThe designated person/s shall be permitted to participate in the Health and Safety Committee Meetings.\nPE1.4 Objectives and Targets\n• Compliance with the Contractor Health and Safety Policy.\n• Everyone is responsible for organising accident prevention at his or her own level on site.\n• Safety training is important.\n• Prevention.\n• Working safely ensures your job.\n• The Contractor management commits itself to the objectives and targets.\n• Disabling Injury Frequency Rate (DIFR) of 2.0 or less.\n• 90% compliance on monthly Health and Safety Audits.\n• Compliance with the legal requirements set out in the Occupational Health and Safety Act, Act 85 of\n1993, (OHSA) and Regulations.\n172\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Compliance with the Client’s Safety Specification for Construction.\nPE1.5 Planning and Procedure\nThe procedures to be used for the project are to be in accordance with the Safety\nManual in use in the Contractor under the guidance of the appointed safety\nconsultant. The sub-headings covered under the Safety Manual are as follows:\n• Administration\n• Appointments\n• Safety Committees\n• Registers, checklists and permits\n• Incident Management\n• Emergency Planning\n• Contractors\n• Risk Assessments\n• Audits\n• Hazardous substance control\n• Training\n• Mining Requirements\n• Roads Requirements\n• Planning\nPE 1.6 Implementation of The Occupational Health and Safety Specification\nThe Contractor is committed to implementing client specific safety specification on\nthe project and is committed see that this forms an integral part of the project. It is our\nintention to make this specification part of other Contractors and Suppliers operating\nprocedures.\nPE1. 7 Application of the Health and Safety Specification\n(a) Compensation of Occupational Injuries and Diseases Act, Act No. 130 of 1993\n(COIDA)\nThe letter of good standing will be available on site for reference purposes as proof of good\nstanding.\nThe Contractor shall ensure all Sub-Contractors also comply with the above\nrequirements defined in the COIDA.\n(b) Occupational Health and Safety Policy\nThe COMPANY Health and Safety Policy is attached for reference purposes. (Annexure\nA)\n(c) Hazard Identification Risk Assessment\nThe Contract Manager shall ensure the Site Agent shall prior to the commencement\nof any construction work perform Hazard Identification, and the assessed risks shall\nform part of the health and safety plan applied on site.\nA copy of the HRA shall be made available for viewing to the client.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n182\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all HRA’s conducted will be conveyed to all personnel\nand contractors through the site training program and that these training sessions will\nbe presented by the competent person regarding the hazard and related work\nprocedures before any work commences.\nThe HRA Team that will be established will comprise members\nas follows:\nHealth and Safety Representative(s),\nHealth and Safety Committee Member(s)\nManagement Representative / Site Agent\nAttached in the form of Annexure C and D, the Hazardous Task Identification and\n(HTI) and format of the Risk Assessment (RA) is included.\nMethod statements form part of the Risk Process and will be conducted in accordance\nwith the Risk Process described above.\nBased on the activities carried out on all projects Hazard Investigation and Risk\nAssessments (HRA’s) will be done. Examples of which are:\nSite Establishment\nDemolition works\nExcavation\nConcrete works\nLifting operations\nHand held tools\nMotorised Equipment\n(d) Health and Safety Committee\nThe Project shall convene a health and safety committee meeting monthly. All\nmembers required to be in attendance shall be notified of such meeting by means of\na formal agenda.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure an attendance register and minutes are kept for auditing\npurposes, and that a copy of the minutes be circulated to all members in attendance\nwell before convening the next meeting and within 7 days, a copy of the minutes will\nbe forwarded to the project Employer’s Agent.\nMembers of the committee shall include the following and are\nnot limited to: Safety Consultant. (When available)\nContractor’s site representative.\n(Supervisory level) Contractor’s site\nrepresentatives. (Operating level)\nProject Employer’s Agent nominated representative. (Co-opted status)\n(e) Health and Safety Training\nTraining of personnel is a legal requirement and a necessity and is acknowledges as\nsuch. The Training Planning Matrix shall be provided upon request.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n183\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nInduction Training\nInduction training shall be attended with the Client as well as the Contractor\nInduction program requirements and records of attendance kept to prove the same.\nThe Contractor Induction format is attached for reference purposes.\n(Annexure E) Awareness Training\nWeekly awareness training shall be conducted using the Contractor Toolbox Talk\ndocuments, which shall be conducted by the site supervisors. (Annexure F)\nCompetency\nTraining identified through the Risk Assessment Process and conducted through this\nprocess shall be kept on file as proof of competency and training. (This may include\noperators)\nFirst Aid and Health & Safety Representative Training\nAll safety representatives elected and designated, including first aiders, shall be\ntrained should they not already be in possession of a valid certificate of training\nproving competence.\n(f) General Record Keeping\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all the Health and Safety records, required by both the\nOccupational Health and Safety Act, 85 of 1993 and Regulations are kept for\nreference purposes and auditing.\nFurther to the requirements set out above, the Site Agent will also maintain records\nthat may be defined through the risk assessment process, for auditing purposes.\nIn accordance with the requirements set out in the Construction Regulations 2003\nand the requirement set out in Client Specification the Site Agent shall ensure that a\ncopy of all Health and Safety records generated during the course of construction, be\nhanded over to the Project Employer’s Agent upon completion of construction.\nStatistics\nThe Site Agent shall ensure injury and incident records (Near Hits, First Aid, Medical\ncases, Disabling Lost Time Incidents), training etc. referred to above are kept on site.\nAll documents shall be made available to the client for inspection including the\nDepartment of Labour’s Inspectors as required by the Occupational Health and Safety\nAct, 85 of 1993.\nThe statistics formula as listed below shall be adhered to.\nDIFR (Disabling Injury Frequency Rate) DI’s x 1 000 000\nMan-hours\nDISR (Disabling Injury Severity Rate) Days Lost x 1 000\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n184\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nManhours\nGeneral Inspection, Monitoring and Reporting\nThe Contractor shall comply with the requirements set out by the client. We have\nattached a safety management plan upon which the dates of inspections and training\nand awareness will be entered, conducted and monitored.\nThe Contractor shall keep all records of inspections and investigations undertaken\nduring the contract for the specified legal period as defined in the OHSA and\nRegulations.\nInternal Audits\nInternal audits shall be conducted a minimum once per month by the project Employer’s\nAgent, as\nwell as the appointed safety consultant.\nThe Results shall be tabled and discussed at the Health and Safety Committee meetings.\nThe Audits to be conducted by the appointed safety consultant shall be conducted on\nthe audit schedule attached as per Annexure G.\nRecords of the audits shall be forwarded to the Project Employer’s Agent and shall be filed\non site for\nreference purposes.\nIncentives\nNo incentive scheme is being identified unless required by the client.\nPenalties\nNon-compliance with the client safety specifications can result in work stoppages and\npossible expulsion from site until the problem has been remedied including costs.\n(g) Emergency Procedures\nThe Site Agent shall make available to the Project Employer’s Agent a detailed\nEmergency Plan to\ntie into the evacuation plan already in place on the client’s premises.\nFirst Aid Box and Contents\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all working areas area adequately provided with first\naid attendants whether there are fifty (50) employees or less engaged on the contract.\nThe First Aid attendant shall be trained in accordance with the requirements set out\nin the OHSA with recognised and accredited service providers as defined above.\nProof of training attended (certificate) shall be attached to the written acceptance of\nappointment. It will be the first aid attendant’s responsibility to ensure the contents of\nthe first aid boxes are monitored and inspections recorded on the contents of the first\naid box register.\nThe first aid box shall be adequately stocked by The Contractor at all times and will\nbe accessible to all.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n185\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nAccident and Incident Reporting and Investigation\nShould accident investigation need to be conducted, the Employers Agent shall\nappoint a competent person in writing to conduct the said investigation. The procedure\nto be followed will be in accordance with the OHSA requirement on the Annexure 1 –\nRecording and Investigation of Incident form.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that the results of all investigations are communicated to\nthe employees engaged through incident recall and prescribed meetings. The Site\nAgent shall ensure that the investigations are kept for record purposes in accordance\nwith the prescribed requirements set out in the OHSA and the Contractor specific\nprocedures.\nShould there be an incident, the Project Employer’s Agent shall be notified within 48-\nhours if required by the client, of the occurrence. It is acknowledged that the client\nreserves the right to participate in all investigations into accidents or incidents.\nHazards and Potentially Hazardous Situations\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all other contractors or contractors are warned of\nhazardous or potentially hazardous situations, which may prevent them from\neffectively performing their duties, which includes the placement of adequate warning\nsigns.\nPersonal Protective Equipment and Clothing\nThe Contractor shall comply with OHSA requirements to provide PPE.\nThe Site Agent shall through the Risk Assessment process identify the specific PPE\nneeds per activity and then issue the PPE accordingly. (Reference to the OHSA\nGeneral Safety Regulation 2 – Employer to provide Personal Protective Equipment)\nShould PPE be lost or stolen, then the employee will be issued with a new set of PPE.\nShould PPE be worn out or damaged, the user shall return the worn or damaged\nPPE and will be issued with a replacement set. Training in the use of this shall be\nprovided.\nOveralls and hardhats shall be identifiable. (Principal Contractor different from the\ncontractors) PPE shall be provided to visitors as well.\nSafety Signage\nThe Site Agent in conjunction with the appointed safety consultant shall assess\nthe Health and Safety Signage requirement in conjunction with the Risk\nAssessments conducted and will place the signage at strategic positions on\nthe site works.\nThe Contractor shall also maintain the signage to ensure its effectiveness at all times\nand under all conditions. Signage, which cannot be repaired, shall be replaced.\nPermits\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n186\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Contractor shall ensure that access to site works is restricted to\nconstruction personnel. All attempts will be made to restrict spectator\naccess.\nAccess to the site shall be by the Project Employer’s Agents (Clients) authorisation on the\nprescribed\nform. (Permits and ID cards shall be issued by the client)\nSpecial permits for hot work and isolation permits shall be applied for to the Project\nEmployer’s Agent\nprior to commencing with the activity.\nContractors and Suppliers\nThe Site Agent shall enter into an Agreement with Mandatary in terms of Section 37(2)\nof the Occupational Health and Safety Act, 85 of 1993, with all contractors appointed\nby The Contractor is entered into.\nThe Contracts Manager will ensure the contractors are issued with the Client Safety\nSpecification where reasonably practicable including any the contractor pack for the\nproject, should they not be contained in the Client Safety Specification.\nThe Contractor shall assist and ensure the Sub-Contractors engaged comply with all\nof these requirements and adhere to the requirements set out in the OHSA.\nContractors will be stopped from working in the event of unsafe conditions and\nactivities being observed.\nAll Sub-Contractors shall be covered by the Contractor Safety Plan and will be issued the\nsame.\n(h) Health and Safety in Practice\nExcavations\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all activities involving excavations, shoring, dewatering or\ndrainage,\na safe working procedure is submitted to the project Employer’s Agent for approval\nprior to work commencing. Excavation work exceeding the specified depth as\nstipulated in the OHSA regulations, shall comply with the following requirements:\na) The excavations are inspected before the shift starts, after heavy\nrain (inclement weather) and after any major condition which may\neffect the excavations stability and the findings are to be recorded\nand kept;\nb) All excavations regardless of the depth shall be adequately\nbarricaded to prevent persons falling into the excavation;\nc) The safe working procedure shall be communicated to all\nemployees who may be effected by the work; and\nd) The safe working procedures shall be enforced and maintained by\nthe appointed excavation supervisor at all times.\ne) For high-risk activities, all personnel working in the excavation\nshall be attached by means of a lifeline.\nf) Material excavated shall be removed from the point of excavation.\ng) Ensure stability of adjoining structures.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n187\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nDemolition\nThere is demolition work that has been envisaged on this project.\nExplosives and Blasting\nThere is a possibility of blasting being required on this project.\nStacking of Materials and Housekeeping\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all stacking will be supervised by a person competent\nto supervise over the activities, and that clearly defined and allocated storage areas are\nprovided for and identified, and that materials being stored within this area are stacked\nin accordance with sound stacking principles of sort-by-sort, access to be maintained,\nlevel surface, and the height will not exceed three times the base width.\nHousekeeping shall be maintained in accordance with the client requirements at all times.\n(i) Hazardous Chemical Substances\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the necessary training and information regarding the use\nand storage of HCS is provided, and that the use and storage of HCS is carried out\nas prescribed by the HCS Regulations.\nFurthermore, the Site Agent shall ensure that all chemicals brought to site have a\nMaterial Safety Data Sheet (MSDS) and the users are made aware of the Occupational\nhazards and precautions that need to be taken when using the chemical.\nThe First Aider shall be made aware of the MSDS and how to treat HCS incidents\nappropriately.\nAccess to all HCS records shall be afforded to the project Employer’s Agent at all times.\nFuel / Diesel\nBulk storage areas shall be demarcated, secured and sign posted with the relevant\nwarning pictograms.\nBulk storage areas shall be bunded.\nRe-fuelling shall be conducted in designated re-\nfuelling areas only. Spill-kits shall be available at all\ntimes in these designated areas.\nThe surface of the bunded areas and walls shall be of\nimpermeable material. The bunded area shall be sloped\ntowards a collection pit.\nAsbestos\nNo asbestos is to be used on this Project.\n(j) Plant and Machinery Construction Plant\n• All plant shall comply with the OHS Act requirements in relation to operation and\nmaintenance thereof.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n188\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Service and maintenance of the vehicles shall be of a high standard at all times.\n• All plant shall subject to design be fitted with back-up alarms and audible\nindicating devices.\n• The Contractor shall ensure that all construction plants moving parts are\nadequately protected.\n• Pre-start inspections shall be conducted on all motorised equipment daily;\ndeviations of such inspections shall be recorded.\n• Construction plant identified for use shall be operated by a trained and\nauthorised operator.\n• All construction plant shall be operated under the direct supervision of a person\ncompetent to identify potential hazards in the work he is conducting.\n• Work involving the use of construction plant shall be conducted in accordance\nwith an approved Risk Assessment.\n• The Site Agent shall ensure all operators are equipped with the necessary PPE\nnamely; safety shoes, overall, safety glasses, and gloves.\n• Plant shall be fitted with an extinguisher where practicable. Washing shall be\nconducted in the designated washing areas.\n• The Contractor shall ensure the all equipment moving to and from site is\nadequately secured, and that all contractors abide by this requirement.\nTransport of Personnel\nSafe vehicular transport shall be provided for personnel working on the project to\nthe workplace, which shall include proper seating, side restraints and cover.\nNo personnel shall be permitted to travel on any plant or equipment on the\nsite works. Road safety principles shall be adhered to on and off site.\nVessels under Pressure (VuP) or Gas Bottles\nThe Contractor shall ensure they comply at all times with the requirements of Vessels\nunder Pressure Regulations, with specific reference to the following:\nEnsuring all Equipment owned and hired-in Vessels under pressure, comply with\nthe 36-month pressure vessel inspection, and a certificate of testing is available on site.\nEnsuring that all personnel who shall use this equipment are competent and\ntrained. Ensuring the users of this equipment are issued with the required\nPPE.\nEnsuring the area is adequately identified as a noise area and warnings are posted.\nEnsuring daily pre-start inspections are carried out on all the equipment and the findings\nrecorded. Ensuring the correct fire prevention and fighting equipment is available at all\ntimes.\nNoise levels where possible shall be kept within reasonable operating norms.\nFire Equipment\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the following all fire equipment to be used on site\ncomply with the following:\nExtinguishers shall be placed in positions to ensure fast and easy access is maintained\nat all times. Placement of all extinguishers shall be depicted with the required\npictograms.\nExtinguishers shall be serviced once annually, and after discharge or visible\nsigns of depressurisation.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure all employees are adequately trained in the safe use of the\nextinguishers. The Site Agent shall ensure a person is appointed to inspect the\nextinguishers on a monthly basis and the results of which are to be entered into a\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n189\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nregister designed for that purpose.\nHired Plant and Machinery\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the following criteria is adhered to when considering hired\nplant and machinery:\nOnly approved hire companies shall supply equipment to the site.\nHired plant shall be checked for safety compliance prior to being accepted for use on site.\nShould hired equipment be accompanied by an operator, The Contractor shall\nensure that the operator’s competency be verified and the operator undergo an\ninduction training session.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the operators of hired plant attend weekly toolbox talks\nin conjunction with The Contractor site personnel.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that all operators are equipped with the required PPE\nbefore commencing work on site.\n(k) Scaffolding / Working at heights / Fall Protection\nWork involving scaffolding and work at heights shall comply with the requirements set\nout in the Construction regulations 2003 pertaining to these activities with reference\nto the SABS 085 code of practice.\nFall protection planning shall be done in conjunction with the risk assessment process.\nAll scaffold shall be erected under the control of a person trained and appointed to\nconduct such scaffold erection.\nFalsework / Formwork for Structures\nWork involving scaffolding and work at heights shall comply with the requirements set\nout in the Construction regulations 2003 pertaining to these activities with reference\nto the SABS 085 code of practice.\nLifting Machinery and Tackle\nThe Site Agent shall ensure that the use of Lifting Machinery and Tackle is done in\naccordance with the requirements of the Regulations, which include but is not limited\nto the following:\nLifting machinery and tackle to be used on site shall be marked with the Maximum Mass\nLoad (MML), which is the safe limit in which the equipment may be used.\nInspections on Lifting Machines and Lifting Tackle shall be inspected once per month on\nthe register provided and the findings recorded.\nDaily pre-start checks shall also be conducted on all Lifting Machinery and\nTackle. Records shall be kept of all lifting machinery and tackle inspections\nand Load Tests.\nLoad tests shall be conducted a minimum of once per annum, and a certificate of\ncompliance shall be kept on record.\nA valid logbook shall be maintained for all lifting machinery, which will comply with a\nminimum six- monthly service and maintenance.\nLifting machinery shall be operated under supervision at all times with a trained\nbanksmen who shall inspect all tackle before each lift.\nAll lifting equipment operators shall be trained once every two years and a copy of\nsuch training shall be attached to the appointment, which is to be made on site.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n190\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe Operators shall be tested for medical fitness.\nLadders and Ladder Work\nThe following requirements shall be complied with regarding Ladders and\nLadder work: Ladders shall be clearly numbered, and inspected on the\nregister provided.\nA competent person shall be identified and appointed as the ladder inspector.\nWhere aluminium ladders cannot be used, then wooden ladders shall be straight\ngrained, unpainted to allow for proper inspection of the grain for cracking.\nLadders shall be secured at the top and chocked at the base to prevent slipping.\nWhere chocking of the base is not possible, then the user shall ensure that the\nladder is held in position by another employee when ascending the ladder.\nLadders shall be inspected a minimum once per month by the person appointed as\nthe ladder inspector.\nProper storage shall be provided for all ladders when not in use.\n(l) General Machinery\nIn accordance with General Machinery Regulation 2(1), The Contractor shall:\nEnsure a competent person be appointed as defined in the above clause from the\nOccupational Health and Safety Act, 85 of 1993 and Regulations, to service and\nmaintain all machinery in use on site.\nThe Contractor shall appoint additional competent persons to assist the competent\nperson mentioned above in accordance with General Machinery Regulation 2(7)(a),\nas and when required.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that records are maintained of all services conducted.\n(m) Lighting and Power\nThe Site Agent shall ensure lighting circuits and power circuits are fitted with suitable\nearth leakage systems in accordance with the client, which will include the following\nactivities:\nEarth leakage system will be tested\nmonthly.\nMalfunctions shall be repaired\nimmediately or replaced.\nLighting shall be so positioned as not to interfere with construction activities.\nPortable Electrical Tools / Explosive Power Tools\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the following procedure is adhered to regarding Portable\nElectrical Tools and Explosive Powered tools:\nMinimum compliance with legislation.\nOnly competent persons shall be permitted to conduct routine and monthly\ninspections on the equipment.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n191\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPersons competent to inspect the equipment shall be appointed in writing.\nPersons who are trained to operate such equipment shall be appointed and shall\nbe the only authorised person to operate the equipment.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure operation of the equipment is in accordance with the\napproved Risk Assessment and Safe Working Procedure set out.\nAll users shall undergo regular awareness training to ensure compliance.\nThe Site Agent shall ensure the required PPE and clothing is provided and maintained.\n(n) Public Health and Safety\nIn the interests of public safety, The Contractor shall ensure that all persons who may\nbe affected by the work being conducted on site are informed and kept aware of the\ndangers, which may arise from the work being conducted on site.\nThis awareness shall be in the form of posters and inductions for visitors to site and\nwarning signs.\n(o) Night Work\nNight work shall only be conducted upon arrival of the project Employer’s Agent,\nwith the same safety standard being applied for these activities as with day work\nactivities.\nFacilities for Safe Keeping / eating areas\nThe Contractor shall ensure that adequate facility is provided for the personnel on site.\nThe area shall be provide the following:\n• Sufficient seating;\n• Seating under cover;\n• Protected change room;\n• Toilets.\n• Hand wash facility.\n• Potable water.\nNo food preparation shall be conducted on site and designated eating areas will be\nmade to allow adequate seating.\nWaste bins shall be strategically placed and cleared regularly.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n192\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURE A (Safety Policy)\nCONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE WITH HEALTH AND SAFETY FILE AS REQUIRED\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n193\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n194\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n195\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n196\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n197\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURE F (Audit Schedule)\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n198\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nPROJECT SPECIFIC OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND\nSAFETY SPECIFICATION\nFOR\nCOVID-19 SITE\nCONDITIONS MANAGED\nON BEHALF OF\nRAY NKONYENI MUNICIPALITY\n(THE “EMPLOYER”)\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n199\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nKEY ROLE PLAYERS\nEMPLOYER\nPrincipal Agent:\nCivil Engineer\nEnvironmental Control Officer\nHealth and Safety Agent\nPRINCIPAL CONTRACTOR\nContracts Manager\nSite Agent\nH&S Officer\nOther:\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n200\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURES\nANNEXURE A: BASIC RISK ASSESSMENT\nANNEXURE B: TYPICAL BILL OF QUANTITIES\nANNEXURE C: PPE ISSUE REGISTER\nANNEXURE D: COVID-19 QUESTIONNARE & DECLARATION\nLIST OF ABBREVIATIONS\nBRA Baseline Risk Assessments\nBEPs Built Environment Professionals\nBoQ Bill of Quantities\nPSHSS Project Specific Health and Safety Specifications\nCOIDA Compensation of Injuries and Diseases Act\nCR Construction Regulations\nDEL Department of Employment and Labour\nH&S Health and Safety\nOHSA Occupational Health and Safety Act No. 85 of 1993\nPPE Personal Protective Equipment\nPC Principal Contractors\nSSHSS Site Specific H&S Specification\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n201\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n1. DEFINITIONS\nThe Employer: The Ray Nkonyeni Municipality\nThe Act: Means, unless the context indicates otherwise, the Occupational Health and Safety\nAct, No. 85 of 1993 and Regulations promulgated thereunder, as amended\nCOVID-19: Means, the Novel Coronavirus (2019-n Cov2) which is an infectious disease caused\nby a virus that has previously not been scientifically identified in humans which\nemerged in 2019 and was declared a global pandemic by WHO in 2020\nWHO: Means, The World Health Organisation\n1. KEY REFERENCES\nThe following key references apply to the specifications:\n• Occupational Health and Safety Act No. 85 of 1993 and Regulations (as amended)\n• Government Gazette No. 43257 dated 29 April 2020 titled COVID-19 Occupational\nHealth and Safety Measures in Workplaces COVID-19 (C19 OHS), 2020\n• Regulation 10 (8) of the Regulations issued in Terms of Section 27 (2) of The Disaster\nManagement Act, 2002 (ACT No. 57 of 2002).\n• The Disaster Management Act, 2002 (Act No. 57 of 2002), as amended\n1. INTRODUCTION\nThe recent Regulations promulgated under the National Disaster Management Act, and the\nlockdown of 26 March 2020 have closed all construction projects except for those involved with\nessential infrastructure services and emergency facility preparedness. The Covid-19 pandemic has\ncatapulted the role of health and safety (H&S) into the forefront of all activities. Going forward as\nthe lockdown is slowly lifted, the construction industry will be required to focus as never before to\nprotect the vulnerable who are often at the forefront of any project.\nConstruction projects range in size and complexity, occur in rural communities and cities, where\nthe population is dense, or there is very little activity. Irrespective of the nature of the work, workers\nremain exposed to the typical risks and hazards that are typical of construction. Working at heights,\nconfined spaces, breaking, cutting and grinding to name a few. High levels of hard physical labour\nand long hours of work are typical, over and above the risk of contracting Covid-19. As such the\nOccupational Health and Safety Act No 85 of 1993 and its Regulations require to be complied with.\nMore specifically, the Construction Regulations of 2014 remain applicable and must be adhered to\nin order to protect the workers.\nThe Construction Regulations are very specific regarding the roles of stakeholders, namely the\nemployer, designers and contractors. Each of these categories have very specific roles and\nresponsibilities and cover both the built environment professionals (BEPs) and contractors. Each\nhave roles in terms of H&S as per the existing arrangements over and above their contractual and\nmonitoring duties on a project.\nThis H&S specification is based on Annexure 2 (Guideline for the Construction Sector Relating to\nHealth and Safety and COVID-19) of the Construction Sector Reactivation Protocols prepared by\nthe Construction COVID-19 Rapid Response Task Team and dated 26 April 2020.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n202\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n4.1 PURPOSE\nThe purpose of this specification is to specifically manage the operations of Construction work\nprojects during the confirmed 2020 Covid1-19 Novel Corona Virus to ensure that employees are\nwell informed and equipped to take all possible measures to prevent the spread and contain the\nvirus as far as possible and reasonably practicable when performing their duties on site.\n4.2 IMPLEMENTATION OF THE PROJECT SPECIFIC COVID-19 OCCUPATIONAL\nHEALTH AND SAFETY SPECIFICATIONS\nThese H&S specifications will form an integral part of the Contract and are in addition to Project\nSpecific Health and Safety Specifications (PSHSS) already in place and included at tender stage.\nWhere there is conflict of interpretation between the specifications, the Covid-19 specifications shall\ntake precedence.\nNo work may commence without written approval of the revised H&S plan by the H&S Agent, or\nthe responsible person in the MISA. Subsequent to approval, the contractor’s activities on site will\nbe monitored through regular H&S audits and Non-conformances will be issued and penalties or\nwork stoppage will be issued where appropriate.\n4.2.1 Roles And Responsibilities of Health and Safety Officers\nThe PC’s Health and Safety Officer (HSO) will be required to undertaken the following amongst\nother activities:\n• HSO officers will ensure that they carry out the COVID 19 awareness and ensure that all\nemployees receive the message and understand what to do and how to do it. The\ntraining awareness will include training of Security officers on what to do when receiving\nemployees to the premises.\n• HSO officers must ensure that before letting any employees inside the premises/site\ncamp, they issue them with face masks and sanitise their hands before passing through\nthe gate.\n• Screening questionnaire will be completed before employees can be allowed to gain\naccess to the premises.\n• The Employer specifications and requirements will be implemented accordingly.\n• Ensure hand sanitizer and hand soap is available at all times and issued to all including\ntraining on proper usage.\n• Undertake employee awareness campaigns on how the virus is spread from one person\nto another and also train them on how to prevent the spreading of the virus.\n• Conduct regular toolbox talks to ensure that employees are always reminded of correct\nbehaviour and handling of PPE provided.\n• Ensure that employees who are suspected to have the symptoms are isolated\nimmediately and the Department of Health immediately notified of such. While waiting the\nHSO officer must ensure that the isolated victim is entirely kept away from other\nemployees, this will help to lessen to spread the virus.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n203\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n5 BASELINE RISK ASSESSMENT\n5.1 SUMMARY OF RISKS IDENTIFIED DURING DESIGN AND IMPLEMENTATION\nThe existing Baseline Risk Assessment (BRA) will require to be amended to take into account risks\nrelated to the potential spread of the Covid-19 virus. A typical risk assessment schedule is provided\nin ANNEXURE A for use by the Contractor.\nExamples of risk factors include but are not limited to the following:\n• Lack of financial resources by Employer;\n• Employer not enforcing financial resources for projects;\n• Transportation, to, from and on sites;\n• Manual labour for physical tasks and tasks that will not allow for social distancing;\n• Workers arriving at work with a temperature or other symptoms of Covid-19;\n• Management of isolating potentially infected workers, and tracing of family/contacts;\n• The lack of clean ablution facilities;\n• The lack of clean welfare facilities where workers congregate for lunch break;\n• Lack of cleaning and hand-washing facilities;\n• Confined working areas;\n• Too few emergency supplies, first aiders and first aid boxes;\n• Sanitization of frequently used environments throughout the working day;\n• The need to continually monitor site activities;\n• Underlying chronic diseases and age of workers (experienced workers);\n• Specialised contractors having older workers, or need to cross borders, and\n• Rural projects, or small/private projects ignoring the current legislation.\n5.2 RE- INTRODUCTION TO THE WORK PLACE AFTER LOCKDOWN\nEmployees returning to the first day of work after the lockdown to answer a wellness questionnaire\nwith the HSO at the entrance to the main building or site camp. Upon successful completion of the\nquestionnaire (If satisfactory) the employee will be allowed to proceed to his/ her work area. All\nemployees will attend a mandatory re-induction (Revised induction for COVID-19 awareness\nincluding new measures to be taken.\nA COVID 19 Risk assessment will be communicated to all employees to highlight activities and\nareas considered “hot spots”\n6 SITE SPECIFIC HEALTH AND SAFETY PROCEDURES\n6.1 DEMOGRAPHICS\nIt will be incumbent upon the PC to establish a suitable and sufficient procedures for the\nidentification of potentially infected employees and workers, the management of exposure to the\ncorona virus on the project, including visitors and suppliers. Including a response plan for persons\nsuspected of being infected with or exposed the virus. The procedure is to be applicable to all levels\nof management and supervision, employees and local labour.\nAs part of this procedure, the contractor is to maintain a register of all employees and workers on\nthe project, including sub-contractor employees and workers, keeping records of the following\ninformation as a minimum (note the NIOH document that is currently available):\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n204\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n6.1.1 Age of Employee\nThe procedure is to take cognizance of the vulnerability of older workers and make provision for\nadditional or more frequent screening of workers above a specified age. A certificate of fitness\nshould be available to make the worker fit for duty, and should be available on site at all times.\n6.1.2 Health status\nA detailed record of all current and previous health conditions, specifically those identified as creating\na higher risk for contracting Covid-19, to be kept for all workers. The procedure is to make provision for\nstringent testing procedures and management of exposure to the virus for workers with higher\nvulnerability due to underlying health conditions. Such records are confidential and will remain with\nthe Occupational Health facility. A certificate of fitness must be available for each worker on site,\nincluding management and contractors.\n6.1.3 Socio economic status / Unskilled labour\nThe procedure is to consider the socio-economic status and skill level of workers, taking cognizance\nof the fact that these may have an impact on the worker’s level of exposure to the virus outside of\nthe workplace and the risk of being asymptomatic carriers of the virus to the project/site.\n6.1.4 Accommodation\nWhere accommodation is provided by the contractor/sub-contractor, factors to be considered in the\nprocedure include, inter alia:\n• Density of occupants to allow for adequate social distancing (minimum 1.5m) in sleeping\nand dining quarters;\n• Restriction on the number of persons using the same sanitary/hygiene facilities;\n• Provision of dedicated crockery and cutlery for each occupant, together with a procedure\nfor effective cleaning and safe storage of same and a prohibition on the sharing of\nutensils;\n• Dedicated facilities for safekeeping of personal belongings and abovementioned utensils\nfor each person. Such facilities are to allow for total segregation of belongings and must\nbe easy to sanitize. Provision of such facilities for safekeeping to be accompanied with a\nprocedure for the use and sanitizing of the storage facility to reduce the risk of cross-\ncontamination;\n• Facilities for accommodation provided by the contractor to have in place stringent\nprocedures for personal hygiene, ongoing maintenance of sanitizing and social\ndistancing, and\n• Additional rules to include a prohibition on the sharing of clothing, towels and other\npersonal belongings, as well as the laundering of clothing for multiple persons at the\nsame time.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n205\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n6.2 ORIGIN OF LABOUR AND TRANSPORTATION\nWhere a return to work will necessitate travel between provinces and cities for employees and\nworkers to return to the project, the PC is to have a procedure for, or provide transport for the return\nof workers to minimize the risk of exposure to the virus while in transit. While this is difficult to\ncontrol by the PC, induction training needs to include such information so workers can protect\nthemselves. Where on-site transportation is done, a policy needs to be available for how such\ntransportation will be made safe and limit any opportunity for cross infection. If possible, the PC\ncould provide their own transportation for their workers.\nParking areas for public and private vehicles need to be considered. Hand cleaning facilities when\nentering gates, doors, and security entrances.\n6.2.1 Public Transportation across borders/towns/cities\nThe contractor to source/recommend a transport service provider that complies with all travel\nrestrictions and requirements as gazetted by the government, inter alia:\n• Maximum occupancy of vehicles to allow for social distancing; Vehicle sanitized before\npassengers’ board;\n• Passengers provided with hand sanitizer and face masks prior to boarding;\n• Vehicle sanitized before boarding, and hand sanitizer provided to passengers prior to\nboarding, after each stop where passengers leave the vehicle for comfort breaks, and\n• Frequent hand sanitizing is recommended during transit.\n6.2.2 Contractor provided transportation across borders/towns/cities\nWhere the contractor provides transportation across borders/towns/cities to assist workers to return\nto work, provision is to be made for an adequate number of vehicles to comply with the maximum\noccupancy as in (a) above, and all such vehicles provided will be subject to the same requirements\nas abovementioned.\nSince it will not practicable to separate belongings and luggage in either instance mentioned above,\nthe contractor’s procedures must make provision for the sanitizing of personal belongings and\nluggage on arrival at the final destination.\n6.3 SOCIAL DISTANCING\nSocial distancing has been shown to be an effective method to slow down the spread of the corona\nvirus. It will be incumbent on the contractor to ensure that the construction site and facilities are set\nup in such a way that it will be possible as far as is practicable to maintain the required social\ndistancing of a minimum of 1 metre between persons when at work.\n6.3.1 Tasks that require more than 1 person to complete\nWhere it is not possible to maintain the required distance between workers due to the nature of the\nwork activity, e.g. curb laying, confined working areas, rebar tying, preparing wire cages, the\ncontractor will be required to implement, maintain and enforce a procedure to adequately protect\nsuch workers\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n206\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nagainst potential infection with the corona virus. This includes but is not limited to:\n• Providing adequate supplies of suitable PPE such as face masks, task specific\ngloves, safety glasses, disposable/additional coveralls;\n• PPE used during multi-person activities to be exchanged immediately after\nthe task is completed;\n• Sealed bins to be provided for disposable PPE such as masks, disposable\ncoveralls, disposable gloves, etc.;\n• Sealable bags provided to each person for keeping PPE requiring laundering,\nsuch as gloves and overalls, and\n• Sanitising/washing facilities provided for immediate sanitizing of hard hats,\nsafety glasses, shoes, safety harnesses etc. on completion of multi-person\ntasks.\nAll the above to be utilized when breaking for lunch or leaving the site, and before commencing\nwith the next or new work activity.\n6.3.2 Access/Egress of Site, Welfare Facilities, Meeting Areas\nThe PC is to ensure there is suitable and adequate provision to minimize the risk of persons who\nmay be infected with Covid-19 entering the site, the spread of the virus between persons who work\non or visit the site and the risk of potentially contaminated persons leaving the site and accessing\npublic spaces or going home to their families. To achieve this, the contractor is required to\nimplement, inter alia the following measures:\n• Persons accessing the site in groups to maintain social distancing of at least 1 metre\nwhile waiting to access the site;\n• Persons waiting to access the site to be segregated from the public where required by\nthe provision of dedicated, prominently identified public pedestrian walkways situated in\nsuch a way that social distancing is maintained between site personnel and the public;\n• Screening of each person who enters the site with a no-touch infrared thermometer;\n• Means of (fully) sanitizing each person and their belongings, who access and leave the\nsite;\n• Dedicated facilities for safekeeping of personal for each person. Such facilities are to\nallow for total segregation of belongings and must be easy to sanitize. Provision of such\nfacilities for safekeeping to be accompanied with a procedure for the use and sanitizing of\nthe storage facility to reduce the risk of cross-contamination;\n• Toolbox talks to be conducted outdoors, when possible, in order for persons to maintain\nsocial distancing. Where inclement weather does not allow for this, toolbox talks to be\nconducted with smaller groupings of workers in a sheltered area large enough to maintain\nsocial distancing, and\n• Eating areas to be set up in such a way that the maximum number of persons who will\nuse the area at any one time are able to maintain the required social distancing of 1\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n207\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nmetre. Should this not be practicable, meal times are to be staggered on a rotational\nbasis to avoid contact between persons.\n*This guideline is not an exhaustive list and the contractor is encouraged to develop\nrigorous control measures and procedures to safeguard all persons accessing or working\non the site against the risk of Covid-19.\nWhere possible remote means of monitoring such as use of drones or security cameras to monitor\nsite conditions and to do site inspections could be considered.\n6.4 ALCOHOL AND DRUG TESTING\nAlcohol testing may only be done using single use test units, and must be disposed of in the\nappropriate contaminated waste. Drug testing will only be done by an occupational health facility\neither using urine or blood sampling. A protocol will be drawn up by the PC to manage this with the\noccupational health service being used.\n6.5 MEDICAL SURVEILLANCE\nThe normal requirements of pre-placement, periodic and exit medicals will remain, with the\nOccupational health service providing a methodology of how they will be including factors relating\nto Covid-19. No lung functions or peak flows will be done until deemed safe to do so by the South\nAfrican Thoracic Society.\nIt is preferable that occupational health service providers use a cloud-based record keeping service\nto ensure easy tracking and tracing. Free apps such as Square 1 is such an example.\nAny person who contracts the virus may need to be reported to the Compensation Commissioner\nas an occupational disease where their work is to monitor and in contact with others. Such details\nare provided in the Compensation for Injuries and Diseases Act (COIDA).\nIsolation of workers who have a temperature or any symptoms, and removal to the closest facility\nfor testing and treatment, through the numbers provided. The PC is to ensure their policy on this\nincludes such information.\nWorkers will be required to complete COVID-19 questionnaires prior to returning to site. Any worker\nwith any symptoms is not to return to work, or notify the PC of same.\n6.6 ABLUTION FACILITIES\nAblution facilities are an essential facility that must be available for workers across a site. Facilities\nare a high-risk area and increased cleaning regimes are required to be introduced. A policy on how\nthis will be done is required, that will cover both portable and permanent facilities. The following are\nconsiderations, which include, inter alia:\n• Portable toilets to be provided at a 1:10 ratio\n• Cleaners to continually clean and have a formal cleaning regime\n• Hand washing facilities (soap and water, paper towel) to be available where possible, and\nif not, to provide hand sanitizer\n• Induction\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n208\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\ntraining to educate to ensure all users are hand washing correctly\n• Flush toilets preferably 1:15 unless increased cleaning regime present;\n• Restrict the number of people using toilet facilities at any one time e.g. use a welfare\nattendant;\n• Wash hands before and after using the facilities;\n• Enhance the cleaning regimes for toilet facilities particularly door handles, locks and the\ntoilet flush;\n• Portable toilets should be avoided wherever possible, but where in use these should be\ncleaned and emptied more frequently;\n• Provide suitable and sufficient rubbish bins for hand towels with regular removal and\ndisposal that need to be managed as hazardous waste;\n• Introduce staggered start and finish times to reduce congestion and contact at all times;\n• Consider increasing the number or size of facilities available on site if possible, and\n• Provide suitable and sufficient rubbish bins in these areas with regular removal and\ndisposal.\n6.7 SECURITY ACCESS\nPublic access to site is to be limited at all times, and non-essential visitors are not to be allowed\nentry. There is required to be staggered access at all times. The following aspects are to be\nincluded in a policy document as to how such issues will be managed, inter alia:\n6.7.1 Staggered access to site;\nThe PC should consider the following:\n• Introduce staggered start and finish times to reduce congestion and contact at all times;\n• Monitor site access points to enable social distancing – consideration for the number of\naccess points, either increase to reduce congestion or decrease to enable monitoring;\n• 50-100mm deep trough to be placed at entrances to site. Disinfectant is to be placed in\nthe trough and all shoes coming onto site or leaving site will be disinfected, without\nwetting shoes themselves;\n• Remove or disable entry systems that require skin contact e.g. fingerprint scanners or\nbiometric system;\n• Require all workers to wash or clean their hands before entering or leaving the site;\n• Ensure social distancing between people waiting to enter site;\n• Regularly clean common contact surfaces in reception, office, access control and delivery\nareas e.g. scanners, turnstiles, screens, telephone handsets, desks, particularly during\npeak times;\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n209\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Reduce the number of people in attendance at site inductions and consider holding them\noutdoors wherever possible, and\n• Drivers should remain in their vehicles if the load will allow it and must wash or clean their\nhands before unloading goods and materials.\n6.8 PROCUREMENT AND STORAGE FOR COVID-19 PPE AND GENERAL SUPPLIES\nThe following is to be implemented by PC:\n• Availability of personal protective equipment PPE is an imperative and should be\navailable at all times. Where this is not so, the work related to the activity will be stopped\nuntil adequate supplies are available.\n• Storage of PPE is to be tightly controlled, with records of issue. Damaged PPE is to be\nmanaged in the usual way, but all to be disposed of as if contaminated.\n6.9 WASTE MANAGEMENT FOR COVID-19 WASTE\nWaste management arrangements to be updated to include provision for the disposal of additional\nwaste generated due to preventative measures implemented. All waste to be managed as\nhazardous waste.\n6.9.1 Disposal of any gloves, masks\nThe contractor shall dispose of all used gloves and masks as hazardous waste and provide\nsealable bags and containers for the safe disposal of this waste.\n6.9.2 Paper towels\nThe contractor shall provide adequate supplies of paper towels on site. At points where these\ntowels are provided lined waste bins to be placed in order to collect all used towels and then to be\ndisposed of in hazardous waste.\n6.9.3 Disinfectant solution\nThe contractor to provide adequate supplies of disinfectant on site where the use of water and soap\nfor cleaning is not practical. If disinfectant dispensers are not refilled it should be disposed with\nother hazardous waste.\n6.9.4 Wastewater\nWastewater at washing points, toilets, and bathrooms to be contained in a drainage system that\nprevent surface spills. If wastewater is contained in waste buckets it must be sealed when removed\nand disinfected after it is cleaned.\n6.10 SIGNAGE\nThe PC is to review all current signs and notices displayed on site. The PC is to avoid conflicting\nmessages/notices that have been in place prior to lockdown and review according. Typical signage\nthat can be displayed on site is shown in Figure 1\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n210\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nFigure 1: Typical Signage\n6.10.1 Access rules\nThe contractor shall install additional signage with site rules specific to the prevention of spreading\nthe COVID-19 virus at the access control points of the site.\n6.10.2 Notices/Posters with protocols\nNotices and posters shall be placed and installed to raise awareness and regarding protocols to be\nfollowed on site. These notices and posters shall be placed conspicuously at various points on the\nsite including the following places:\n• Entrance\n• Site notice board\n• Site Office\n• Eating areas\n• Next to toilets and bathrooms\n• Hand washing stations\n• Storerooms\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n211\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n6.11 EMERGENCY PLANNING\nAn updated emergency plan is to be completed that is in line with the current Regulations of the\nNational Disaster Management Act.\n6.11.1 First aid\nExtra gloves, and disinfectants are to be available, first aiders are to be issued with at least FFPT2\nmasks should they be required to respond\n6.11.2 Evacuation plans\nEvacuation plans should consider social distancing.\n6.11.3 Isolation of potentially infected workers\nThe emergency plan is to consider how anyone who arrives on site and displays any of the\nsymptoms, or has a raised temperature.\n6.12 PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT (PPE)\nThe hierarchy of control applies with the use of PPE. Specific regard for the type and usage, training\nand control is to be outlined in the policies and procedures.\n6.12.1 Masks\nMasks are compulsory for all employees; personnel and professional team members, reducing the\npotential of inhaled COVID-19 droplets. For general administration purposes, for essential staff on\nsite only, cloth masks may be worn. N95 masks are only to be worn by first aiders or high-risk\nworkers, due to the national shortage thereof.\nAll employees to have access to N95 or FFPT2 masks when required, only in instances of HIGH\nRISK OF EXPOSURE\nAll N95 and FFPT2 masks to be disposed with or after 1 day’s use. Induction is to include training\non the correct use of face masks.\nCloth face masks to be used on entering and leaving the site. It is advised that each worker is\nsupplied with at least 3 cloth face masks. This would assist ensuring that the masks are hygienic\n(1 on the face; 1 in the wash and 1 as a backup).\nThe PC must ensure that sufficient stock is at all-time available on site. This will also have depended\non the type of mask being issued.\nAll disposable masks are biological waste and must be properly disposed of. This must be disposed\nin container (locked) or in bags to be either removed as medical biological waste by registered\nservice provider.\nCLEARLY IDENTIFIABLE BINS INDICATING BIOLOGICAL WASTE TO BE PROVIDED\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n212\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n6.12.2 Face Shields\nThe principal contractor could also look at options such as full face shields for preventing spreading\nof virus through eyes. This would assist the employee who is doing hard physical work to breathe\nmore easily but still protecting the mouth, eyes and nose.\nFace shields should be cleaned daily before the shift and at the end of the shift. Proper cleaning\nagents/disinfectant must be used. Face shields should be issued to employees and no sharing is\nallowed.\n6.12.3 Overalls\nAll employees must be issued with 3 overalls (1 overall wearing; 1 overall in the wash and one as\nthe backup). This will ensure that the employee will be able to wear clean hygienic overalls. This\nmust form part of the COVID-19 training for all employees.\n6.12.4 Hand Gloves\nIt is preferable that surgical gloves are not worn unless indicated and workers trained in the proper\nuse thereof. Gloves must only be used when the activity demand the wearing of specific type of\nhand gloves. This will be directed by the PC risk assessment.\nSite office personnel need to be made aware of the risks in the office environment, this include to\nhandling of documents and plans. These employees could be issued with the appropriate hand\ngloves or sufficient hand wash / sanitising facilities must be available in the site office.\n6.13 CONSEQUENCE MANAGEMENT\n6.13.1 Change Management\nEach Principal contractor / contractors to ensure that regular information pertaining to COVID 19\nand or any Health and Safety matters is distributed to ensure that required measures / controls is\ntimeously addressed. There are various information platforms available to the Principal Contractor\nand or employee that can assist in keeping them informed. These include inter alia:\n• Local Authority / Legislation\n• World Health Organizations\n• Health Care Departments / Health Care Professional’s / Centre’s / Hospitals\n• Public Service Announcements – National News\nEach contractor is responsible and required to keep his or her employees informed by means but\nnot limited by conducting the following;\n• Awareness campaigns -i.e. posters within work places\n• Daily site task Inspections (DSTIs)\n• Toolbox talks / Daily briefings\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n213\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Meetings\n• Company policies / procedures / Employee Wellbeing interaction\n• Company newsletters\n• Telecommunications – Cell phones Apps / e-mail\n6.13.2 Succession Planning\nEmployees who is performing specialize work/activities (including plant operators) must be\nidentified. These positions must have alternative employees that could perform these activities.\nThis could mean additional training for these employees.\nKey personnel on site should also have competent alternative employees that could perform these\nfunctions when needed. Where possible administrative staff should be working from home to limit\nany opportunistic exposure.\nIt is very important to understand that the availability of certain essential products and material may\nnot always be available and thus proper planning must be in place to ensure that the activities on\nsite are not interrupted.\nProactive planning must be in place to ensure that the following are ordered and available:\n• PPE (cloth face masks, face shields, overalls)\n• Hand sanitizers with at least 60% alcohol content\n• Disinfectants and cleaning materials\n6.13.3 Consequences\nWhen non-compliance activities are noted, that activity will be stopped.
