Should the remedial actions\nnot take place the site will be shut down till the corrective actions have been implemented.\nEmployees that do not work according to the SSHSS and SSHSP must be disciplined according to\nthe company’s disciplinary codes and practices.\nSupervisory employees on site must ensure compliance, and when non-conformances are noted\ndisciplinary actions should also be followed.\nPCs should note that they could be fined and even according to the Disaster Management Act,\narrested.\n6.14 WELFARE FACILITIES\nThe PC shall adapt arrangements regarding the provision of welfare facilities to be in line with\nGovernment guidelines and requirements.\n6.14.1 Clean, storage for food and personal belongings\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n214\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe PC to provide lockable storage for all employees on site, which shall be disinfected daily.\nTraining and awareness to address procedures and the importance of good hygiene practice.\n6.14.2 No personal belongings to be kept on site\nApart from extra clean personal clothing no other personal belongings allowed on site accept if kept\nin locker provided by the PC.\n6.14.3 No communal drinking facilities (shared cups etc.)\nThe PC to provide adequate supplies of bottled water to all employees on site. Empty bottles to be\ndisposed of as normal waste. Training and awareness to address procedures and the importance\nof good hygiene practice.\n6.14.4 Larger meeting areas/ preferably use technology (Skype, Zoom, Microsoft teams):\nThe PC is to limit the number of employees at all activities to the minimum required to do the work\nin a safe manner. Where possible meetings must be held in open areas limited to essential\npersonnel. Technological alternatives to be exploited for meeting attendance if possible. Training\nand awareness to address procedures and the importance of social distancing.\n6.14.5 Eating areas\nThe PC is to limit the number of employees at all activities to the minimum. Stagger lunchbreaks\nand resting periods for work teams. Training and awareness to address procedures and the\nimportance of good hygiene practice and social distancing.\nWorkers are required to stay on site once they have entered it and not use local shops.\nDedicated eating areas should be identified on site to reduce food waste and contamination.\n• Break times should be staggered to reduce congestion and contact at all times.\n• Hand cleaning facilities or hand sanitiser should be available at the entrance of\nany room where people eat and should be used by workers when entering and\nleaving the area;\n• Workers should be asked to bring pre-prepared meals and refillable drinking\nbottles from home;\n• Social distancing to be applied whilst eating and avoid all contact;\n• Where catering is provided on site, it should provide pre-prepared and\nwrapped food only;\n• Payments should be taken by contactless card wherever possible;\n• Crockery, eating utensils, cups etc. should be disposable if supplied;\n• Drinking water should be provided with enhanced cleaning measures\nof the tap mechanism introduced;\n• Tables should be cleaned and disinfected between each use;\n• All rubbish should be put straight in the bin and not left for someone else to clear up;\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n215\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• All areas used for eating must be thoroughly cleaned at the end of each break\nand shift, including chairs, door handles, vending machines and payment\ndevices.\n6.15 ADEQUATE RESOURCING OF PROJECTS\nThe PC will be required to price for the additional H&S related items that have arisen as a result of\nthe Covid-19 pandemic and the regulations promulgated under the Disaster Management Act 2002\n(Act No. 57 of 2002), as amended. The Bill of Quantities (BoQ) provided to the PC needs to take\ninto account the requirements that have been identified in the BRA and the SSHSS. Contractors\nneed to be able to price for the requirements and the appropriate BEP to assess the suitability of\nthe items and pricing thereof.\nA typical example of an H&S BoQ that would be adapted is included as Annexure B. The BoQ is\nnot exhaustive and is dependent much on the amended BRA as approved by the Employer or his\nPrincipal agent. Should risk factors change, amendments will need to be made to the BoQ.\n1. CONTRACTUAL CONSIDERATIONS\n7.1 GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS\nBefore considering the provisions of the common construction contracts in detail, the following\ngeneral recommendations are made:\n1. Should payment certificates not have been issued during the period of lockdown,\nthey should be issued as soon as possible after recommencement of works, but no\nlater than the end of May 2020.\n1. The lockdown period will give rise to various typical force majeure claims and\ncontractors will be entitled to submit extension of time claims for the period of the\nlockdown. These will be evaluated by the Principal Agent in accordance with the\nsituation/conditions on site during the lockdown.\n1. The Covid-19 epidemic and the continued lockdown regulations may have a further\nand continued impact to contractors after site re-opening. Those impacts will have to\nbe assessed by the contractors and further claims for resulting delays to the project\nprogrammes may need to be considered.\n1. Where time limits are applicable to claims and claim notifications, such time period\nshould only be calculated from, at the earliest, the date on which the relevant\nconstruction site re-opens.\n1. It is recommended that all claims arising from the lockdown be submitted and\nresolved as soon as possible after recommencement and well before any applicable\ntime limits.\n1. Any disputes emerging in regard to claims should be resolved expeditiously in\naccordance with the provisions in the contract.\n7.2 APPLICABLE GENERAL CONDITIONS OF CONTRACT\nThe following clauses in the General condition of contract are noted which are considered\napplicable to the Covid-19 Lockdown:\n7.2.1 GCC 2015\n1. Clause 5.12.1 provides that the contractor is entitled to an extension of time for the\ncompletion of the works if “circumstances of any kind whatsoever” will delay the\nachievement of practical completion of the works.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n216\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n1. Clause 5.12.2.4 specifically lists “any disruption which is entirely beyond the contractor’s\ncontrol” as a circumstance entitling the contractor to an extension of time. A contractor\nwill accordingly be entitled to an extension of time for delays caused by the lockdown and\nother circumstances resulting from Covid-19.\n1. In terms of clause 5.12.3, if an extension of time is granted the contractor shall be paid\nsuch additional time-related general items as are appropriate.\n1. Clause 5.12.4 provides that instead of granting an extension of time, if feasible, the\ncontractor may be requested to accelerate the rate of progress to achieve practical\ncompletion and will be paid for the costs of such acceleration.\n1. A contractor may also be entitled to a claim for extension of time with adjusted value in\nterms of clause 5.4.3 if an instruction to commence work was issued to the contractor but\nthe employer was unable to fulfil its obligation to give the contractor access and\npossession of site due to the limitations imposed during lockdown.\n1. However, once the contractor has been given possession of the site, a claim under\nclause 5.4.3 would not be applicable.\n1. Covid-19 may also fall within the definition of “excepted risk” and in particular the\nreference to “epidemic plague” set out in clause 8.3.1.7.\n1. In terms of clause 8.3.2, the contractor is entitled to an extension of time and can recover\nadditional costs where the contractor suffers a delay or loss directly or indirectly caused\nby an “excepted risk”.\n1. Clause 9.1.2 read with clause 9.1.4 provides that the contractor is entitled to claim\nadditional costs, which are not covered by the additional time-related general items,\ncaused by “a state of emergency, riot, commotion politically motivated sabotaged acts of\nterrorism or disorder” and “any such event beyond the control of the contractor” that\nmaterially affects the execution of the works.\n1. Clause 9.1.4 requires the contractor to notify the engineer within 14 days of becoming\naware of such increase in cost.\n1. In terms of clause 6.8.4 the contractor is entitled to any additional costs, which are not\ncovered by the additional time-related general items, if at any time within 28 days before\nthe closing of tender or thereafter, any act, ordinance, regulation or by-law is amended\nand this results in additional cost to the contractor.\n1. In terms of clause 10, the contractor is required to submit its claim for any extension of\ntime or additional payment as follows:\n(a) the contractor must submit its claim to the engineer in accordance with the specified\nrequirements, within 28 days after the circumstance or event giving rise to such\nclaim; and\n(b) if the event giving rise to a claim is of an ongoing nature, the contractor is additionally\nrequired to deliver updated monthly claims to the engineer and submit its final claim\nwithin 28 days after the end of the event or circumstance.\n1. The parties may deliver a written notice of dispute to each other and the engineer of any\ndispute provided that the dispute arises from a rejected claim and it is delivered within 28\ndays of the event giving rise to the dispute.\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n217\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n1. The dispute shall be referred to adjudication unless an amicable settlement is\ncontemplated. The parties may agree to settle any claim or any dispute amicably with the\nhelp of an impartial third party. If the other party rejects amicable settlement in writing or\ndoes not respond to the invitation within 14 days or if the amicable settlement is\nunsuccessful the dispute shall be referred to adjudication.\n1. Either party is entitled to disagree with any decision of the Adjudication Board and refer\nthe matter to arbitration or court proceedings, whichever is applicable in terms of the\ncontract provided that a party disputes the adjudicator’s decision not before 28 days or\nafter 56 days from receipt of the decision.\n7.2.2 GCC 2010\n1. The contract provisions are the same in effect save for clause 8.3.1.7 relating to the\nexcepted risk which is not part of GCC 2010.\n1. The contractor may also be entitled to a claim for extension of time with adjusted value\nand additional costs in terms of clause 5.4.3, 5.10.1 and 5.11.2 as explained above in the\nGCC 2015 version. These clauses have the identical operation as in the 2015 version.\n1. The clause numbering and content of the GCC 2010 dispute resolution process runs\nparallel to that of the GCC 2015, save that the 2010 version provides that the parties may\ndeliver a written notice of dispute to each other and the engineer of any dispute provided\nthat the dispute arises from an unresolved claim (and not a rejected claim as recorded\nin the 2015 version).\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n218\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURE A\nBASIC RISK ASSESSMENT\nLOW MED HIGH\n1 4 12\nBASELINE RISK ASSESSMENT\n2 6 18\n3 8 27\nRisk Rating multiplier: Low = 1; Medium = 2; High = 3 PREPARED BY:\nNote: This is a broad overview of the activities expected and available during the design stage of the project. Key issues will be addressed during the construction stage, and may be updated during\nthis time. Consolidation of activities where overlap or applicable throughout the project (plant, material or other common activities). Compliance with all the applicable legislation is required.\nPenalties for non-compliances will be applied where issues not addressed as per the H&S Specification (as amended).\nREFERENCES/ABBREVIATIONS: OHSA Occupational Health and Safety Act (applies overall); GAR = General Administration Regulations; GSR = General Safety Regulations; HBR = Hazardous Biological\nRegulations; CR = Construction Regulations; HCSR = Hazardous Chemical Substances Regulations; FR = Facilities Regulations; EIR = Electrical Installation Regulations; DMR = Driven Machinery\nRegulations; PER = Pressure Equipment Regulations; RTA = Road Traffic Safety Act; SANS = 1200 (unless stated) SANS 10085 = Access Scaffolding; SANS 10083 = Audiometry standards; SANS 1300,\n10142,10400 & 2001 = Building & Electrical Standards; SARTSM = South African Roads and Traffic Signs Manual; PC = Principal Contractor;\nBaseline Design: Residual\nBaseline design: RAW RISK risk\nDESCRIBE THE METHODS AND\nDESIGN ASPECTS ACTIVITIES USUALLY CATEGORYEXTRA CONTROL MEASURES NECESSARY TO\nLEGAL REF\nPRESENT PROVIDED BY THE PC AND REDUCE RISK/ REDESIGNLIKELY\nCONTRACTOR T T\nN N\nE E\nD D\nIC\nC A Y R O\nIC\nC A Y R O\nN G N G\nA E A E\nS E C N F O E R U S O P M R A K S IR T A C S E C N F O E R U S O P M R A K S IR T A C\nE U\nQ E\nY\nS\nL\nN\nE K O I C L\nY C\nN E U Q E\nF\nX\nR\nO\nE\nF\nY T IL\nIB A B O\nF\nR\nO\nH\nP\nG N\nI\nD\nK T S\nN\nA I\nA\nR R\nE U\nQ E\nY\nS\nL\nN\nE K O I C L\nY C\nN E U Q E\nF\nX\nR\nO\nE\nF\nY T IL\nIB A B O\nF\nR\nO\nH\nP\nG N\nI\nD\nK T S\nN\nA I\nA\nR R\n219\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n220\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURE C\nPPE ISSUE REGISTER\nLOCATION / SITE NAME: ....................................................................................... COMPANY NAME: ...............................................\nISSUED\nBY:..........................................................................................................................................................................................................\nENTER NAME\nAND SURNAME &\nSIGN BELOW\nEACH ITEM\nRECEIVED\nGloves Hard Hat Overhaul Safety Reflective Safety Dust Earplugs Safety Welding Welding Welding\nHelmet/\nBoots Vest Glasses Mask Harness Gloves Apron ENTER NAME AND\nGlasses\nSURNAME\n221\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nANNEXURE D\nCOVID-19 QUESTIONNARE & DECLARATION\nContractor Name:\nImportant : Please note that this is an individual Questionnaire.\nSite/Work Area:\nEmployee Name and Surname:\nDate:\nLine Manager:\nSymptomatic Screening Questionnaire Yes No\nHave you experienced symptoms of flu or had flu in the past two weeks?\nHave you experienced any coughing or breathing abnormalities lately?\nDo you currently have a fever or have you been experiencing symptoms of a fever? ( Red\neyes, burning sensation )\nIn the last 14 days, have you come into contact with any person that has displayed\nsymptoms or tested positive for COVID 19?\nHave you travelled outside the borders of South- Africa lately?\nHave you been tested for COVID 19?\nPersonal Commitment\n1. I Will further to the above , declare any immediate changes in my health to my line manager\n2. I will Adhere to all the guidelines set out by Thermaire & Ampair in the COVID 19 management plan\n3. I will maintain good hygiene practices\n4. I will maintain social distance from employees at all times\n5. I will utilize ppe and sanitizer provided at all times.\n6. I will ensure that shared equipment, as far as reasonably practicable , has been sanitized before\nhandover to other employees.\nI , Declare that the document is a true statement of my\ncurrent health and hereby will adhere to all guidelines set out.\nEmployee Signature\nTemperature reading If available (Refer to health practioner if above 38 degrees\nCelsius\nEmployee Cleared for entrance ( Circle ) Yes No\nQuestionnaire reviewed by :\n222\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nF.8 PARTICULAR SPECIFICATION – QMS: EMPLOYER’S AGENT’S QUALITY\nMANAGEMENT SPECIFICATION\nCONSTRUCTION QUALITY ASSURANCE PROCEDURES (CQAP)\nQMS1. INTRODUCTION\nThe Employer’s Agent subscribes to a Quality Management System accredited by a number of certification bodies\nincluding ISO 9001 (2015).\nThis document/specification summarises the Quality Control Procedures used by the Employer’s Agent in the\nQuality Assurance and Control on site works. These procedures are to be used by Employer’s Agent’s\nRepresentative Staff (i.e. ER and his assistants) and the Contractor’s staff on the following commonly encountered\nsites;\n• Pipelines\no UPVC Pipelines\no Steel Pipelines\n• Reservoirs\n• Building Works\n• Roadworks\nThe procedures have been developed as “intellectual” property of the Employer’s Agent and may only be used on\nsites managed by the Employer’s Agent. Any other use is subject to consent/agreement with the Employer’s Agent.\nAll references to approval by the Employer’s Agents and/or his Representative(s) require that the Contractor (via\nthe Site Agent) initiates the necessary request for approval). In addition, the Contractor will be required to maintain\na copy of all records as required by this Specification.\nThe application of the procedures will be agreed as appropriate between the Contractor’s Site Agent and the\nEmployer’s Agent (or his Representative) at the commencement of construction activities.\nIt will be deemed that the Contractor has incorporated in his programme and completion period and pricing,\nthe necessary requirements to comply with this Specification fully.\n1.1 Elements of Site Quality Assurance\nThe elements of Site Quality Assurance comprise the following:\n• general elements that apply to all sites and\n• site specific elements that are specific to sites and may be dependent on the type of construction.\nQMS2 GENERAL ELEMENTS APPLICABLE TO ALL SITES\n2.1 Construction Quality Control Organization\nThis section presents the requirements of key site personnel involved on construction sites, i.e. Employer’s Agent’s\nRepresentative (ER) staff and Contractor’s staff. The following quality assurance procedures for site quality\nassurance personnel should be followed:\n223\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n2.1.1 Employer’s Agent’s Quality Assurance Personnel\nThe following ER staff appointments’ procedure should be followed to ensure the right superintendence on\ncontracts:\nItem Activity Remarks Responsible Approval by\nParty\n1 Appointment of Employer’s Stated in contract data Employer’s Employer\nAgent for contracts Agent\n2 Supervision staffing Proposed prior to Employer’s Employer\narrangements construction work Agent\n3 Site staff Proposal for site personnel Employer’s Employer\nincluding CV’s Agent\n4 Roles Delegation of powers by Employer’s Employer’s\nEmployer’s Agent Agent Agent\nThe site staff will comprise the Employer’s Agent’s Representative (ER) and ER’s assistants (Field Officers):\n(a) Employer’s Agent’s Representative (ER)\nThe ER is the primary point of contact for the Employer’s Agent on all construction management issues. The ER\nwill monitor and approve each contractor’s quality submittal to ensure that the project is meeting the specifications\nand requirements. The ER will manage the implementation of the CQAP at the project sites with assistance from\nField Officers appointed by the Employer’s Agent.\n(b) ER Assistants/Field Officers (FO’s)\nField Officers (FOs) are responsible to the ER and support the ER’s management of the CQAP. The FOs will monitor\nthe day-to-day activities of the contractor. This includes ensuring that contractors comply with the drawings and\nspecifications, applicable SABS standards, good workmanship, and the CQC requirements. As part of this effort,\nFOs will:\n• conduct independent inspections to verify the quality of the work;\n• participate in contractor inspections;\n• review test and inspection reports; and\n• ensure that the required documentation is submitted.\nThe FOs will be alert to detect, record, and report any deviation from the contract documents, including calling any\ndeficient item to the attention of the ER and the contractors’ Site Agents. The FOs will keep accurate and detailed\nrecords of the contractor’s performance and progress, delivery of materials, and other pertinent matters, including\nthe daily inspection report.\n2.2 Contractor’s Quality Assurance Personnel\nThe contractors are responsible for the quality control of their constructed work product as well as the necessary\ninspections and tests required to ensure that their work complies with the contract documents.\n2.2.1 Contractor’s Site Staff\nThe contractors’ Site Agents are the primary point of contact for the Contractors on all construction management\nissues. The Site Agents must be full-time on site for the contractors. The Site Agents must have full authority to\ninstitute any and all actions necessary for the successful implementation of the CQC program to ensure compliance\nwith the drawings and technical specifications.\nThe following procedures apply with\n224\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nrespect to appointment of the contractor’s key personnel:\nAspect Remarks Approval When\nBy\nAppointment of Site Agent As per tender for quality Employer’s Prior to commencement\nbased evaluated tenders Agent of construction\nAppointment of Site As per tender for quality Employer’s Prior to commencement\nForepersons based evaluated tenders Agent of construction\n2.3 Site Establishment\nThe Employer’s Agent’s Representative shall inspect and approve/disapprove contractor’s site establishment\nusing Quality Procedure Form QC 01.\n2.4 General\nThe Site Agent is required to complete the necessary 1–2 page forms requesting for approval of the following\nGeneral Items. Thereafter the ER must undertake the following general items as appropriate:\n1 Confirm “Permission to Occupy” has been received from the relevant authority.\n2 “Handover of Site” to Contractor to be confirmed in writing.\n3 Inspect and approve Site Establishment (Form QC 01).\n4 Setup Site Files/Filing System.\n5 Ensure a copy of the Contract Document is retained on Site by the Contractor.\n6 Ensure a full set/s of approved drawings is/are retained on Site by the Contractor.\n7 Maintain a Drawing Register.\n8 Ensure a copy of the latest Contract Program is clearly displayed on Site.\n9 Establish Quality Assurance Procedures and carry out inspections as and when required.\n10 Issue Site Instructions as and when required.\n11 Ensure Safety File, including Dept. of Labour notification, is up to date and on Site and all relevant\nregulations, including issuing of PPE, are strictly adhered to.\n12 Ensure all relevant information is recorded in a daily Site Diary and counter signed.\n13 Hold regular Work Meetings with the Contractor.\n14 Hold regular Site Meetings with the Client, Professional Team and the Contractor.\n15 Maintain a copy of the Environmental Record of Decision on Site\n225\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nQMS3 SITE SPECIFIC QUALITY ASSURANCE PROCEDURES\nQuality assurance inspections and testing will be used to verify the adequacy and effectiveness of the contractor’s\nquality control program. The Employer’s Agent’s Quality Assurance Personnel detailed above will provide inspection\nand supervision within the scope of work, which includes monitoring of the following construction activities:\n• Manufacture of materials\n• Transporting and off-loading and storage of construction materials\n• Inspection of construction activities, including:\no Pipework\n▪ Steel\n▪ uPVC\no Reinforced Concrete Reservoirs\no Building Works\no Pump stations (mechanical & electrical installations)\no Roadworks\nThe Contractor will be required to formally request for inspection for any activity which he deems to be complete before\nproceeding to the next stage of the whole operation. Formal requests must be filled in the relevant QC Form.\nContractor Deficiency Correction\nWhen material, performed work or installation is found to be deficient and/or does not meet the project\nspecifications, the Employer’s Agent’s QA personnel will assure deficiency correction is implemented. In addition to\nresults of an inspection being recorded on the relevant QC Form, in the event of inspection failure, the Employer’s\nAgent’s QA personnel will fill in Form QC 008 “Failure Report”, to record the deficiencies. A copy of this report will\nbe handed over to the Contractor’s Site Agent. The Contractor will implement corrective actions to remedy work that\nis not in accordance with the drawings and specifications. The corrective actions will include removal and\nreplacement of deficient work using methods approved by the ER. Removal must be done in a manner that does\nnot disturb work that meets QC/QA criteria; otherwise, the disturbed material must also be removed and replaced.\nReplacement must be done in accordance with the corresponding technical specifications. Replacement will be\nsubjected to the same scope of QC/QA inspection and testing as the original work. If the replacement work is not\nin accordance with the drawings and specifications, the replacement work will be removed, replaced, re inspected\nand re-tested.\nActivities which specifically require approval before the next stage can proceed are as detailed in this section.\n3.1 Pipework\nThe following procedures will be used for pipework quality assurance:\nThe ER is responsible for ensuring the following quality assurance procedure is followed, as a minimum:\n1 Inspect & Approve Setting Out (Form QC 001).\n2 Inspect & Approve Pipeline Trenches (Form QC 002).\n3 Inspect & Approve Pipeline Bedding (Form QC 003).\n4 Inspect & Approve Pipe Installation – PVC (Form QC 004B).\n5 Inspect & Approve Pipeline Pressure Testing (Form QC 006).\n6 Inspect & Approve Backfilling to Trenches (Form QC 007).\nCopies of the Employer’s Agent’s forms are available for inspection at the offices of the Employer’s Agent.\n3.2 Reinforced Concrete Works\n226\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\nThe ER is responsible for ensuring the following quality assurance procedure is followed, as a minimum:\n1 Inspect & Approve Setting Out (Form QC 001).\n2 Inspect & Approve Excavations (Form QC 008).\n3 Inspect & Approve Backfilling to Excavations (Form QC 009).\n4 Inspect & Approve Excavations prior to Blinding (Form QC 023).\n12 Record Site Measurement (Form QC 025)\nCopies of the Employer’s Agent’s forms are available for inspection at the offices of the Employer’s Agent.\nQMS4 DOCUMENTATION\n4.1 Overview\nAn effective CQA Plan depends largely on recognition of all construction activities that should be monitored and on\nassigning responsibilities for the monitoring of each activity. This is most effectively accomplished and verified by\nthe documentation of quality assurance activities. The ER will document that quality assurance requirements have\nbeen addressed and satisfied. The ER will provide the Employer’s Agent with signed descriptive remarks, data\nsheets, and inspection reports to verify that monitoring activities have been carried out. The ER will also maintain,\nat the job site, a complete file of Drawings and Technical Specifications, a CQA Plan, test procedures, daily diaries,\nand other pertinent documents.\n4.2 Daily Site Diary\nA daily construction site diary will be prepared and signed by each Site Agent and the ER. The diary will include a\nsummary of the contractor’s daily construction activities. At a minimum, the daily construction diary will include the\nfollowing information:\n• Date, project name, location, and other identification\n• Description of weather conditions, including temperature, cloud cover, and rainfall\n• Reports on any meetings held and their results\n• Record of visitors to site\n• Locations of construction underway during that day\n• Equipment and personnel working in each activity, including subcontractors\n• Descriptions of work being inspected\n• Decisions made regarding approval of units of material or of work, and corrective actions to be taken\n• Description of problems or delays and resolution\n• Communications with contractor staff\n• Construction activities completed and/or in progress\n• Signature of the diary preparer\nThe daily site diary will be routed on a daily basis to the project QC/QA files and will be maintained as part of the\npermanent project record.\n4.3 Control of Quality Records\nThe ER verifies QA record accuracy and maintains copies of all quality-related documentation. This includes, but\nmay not be limited to:\n228\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C3\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Scope of Work\n• Daily construction QA records;\n• Inspection reports;\n• Non-conformance (Failure) reports;\n• Material receiving reports; and\n• Monitoring and test data.\nThese records will be stored in files maintained in the project document control files. All original documents\npertaining to QC information will be maintained in the project file located at the site. All records shall be available\nfor inspection and audit, at any time, by the Employer’s Agent and/or the Employer or their Agents\n229\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C4\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Site Information\nPart C4: Site Information\nTABLE OF CONTENTS\nPage\nC4.1 Locality Plan 231\nC4.2 Conditions on Site 232\nC4.3 Geotechnical Investigations Report 233\n230\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C4\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Site Information\nPART C4: SITE INFORMATION\nC4.1 LOCALITY PLAN\nThe Locality of the site is as per the attached Locality Plan.\nCoordinates\nProject Name\nLatitude Longitude\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA 300 53’ 4.60’’ S 300 13’ 38.08’’E\nBRIDGE IN WARD 8\nThe google earth image below shows the location of the site.\nFig 1: The location of Mgodlwa Vehicular Bridge\n231\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF MGODLWA BRIDGE IN WARD 8\n\n8/2/RNM0614 Part: C4\nRay Nkonyeni Local Municipality Site Information\nPS-3.2 Limitations\nC4.2 CONDITIONS ON SITE\nA brief description of the site conditions is given under this section.\nC4.2.1 Nature of Ground and Subsoil Conditions\nGeotechnical investigations have been undertaken on the bridge site and are included as an Annexure to the site\ninformation.\nC4.2.2 Weather Conditions\nThe following general weather conditions are prevalent on site\nSite Conditions Specific Details\nAltitude above sea level Maximum 230 m\nAmbient temperatures Maximum: 30ºC\nMinimum: 4ºC\n24-hour Average Max: 30ºC\nMaximum relative humidity 100%\nEnvironmental atmosphere Humid with high salt content, severely corrosive.\nLightning Severe\nC4.2.3 Limitations\nIn arriving at his rates, the tenderer/contractors must take note of the following limitations that\ncharacterize the site of the bridge. These must be taken into account in pricing and during\nconstruction of the bridge.\n(a) The contractor’s activities shall be restricted to the bridge construction site.\n(b) The contractor’s insurances a shall have to cover potential damage to private property as\naccess passes in close proximity to houses etc\n(c) The project is urgent and the tenderer/contractor must complete the works as soon as\npossible. An estimated construction period of 6 months has been allowed. The work shall be carried out in accordance with the latest available revision of the drawings\napproved for construction (AFC).\nAt commencement of the contract, the Employer’s Agent shall deliver to the Contractor copies of the AFC drawings and any\ninstructions required for the commencement of the works.
